Chapter 101: An Unexpected Clue

On Howes Street, Klein adjusted his half top hat as he climbed the stairs to the Divination Club's entrance.

He was not wearing his usual formal attire. Today, he had chosen a white shirt and pale vest beneath a lightweight black trench coat, an ensemble that made him look more energetic than usual.

The clothes were better suited to combat and had cost only one pound, including the tailor's fee for sewing a small inner pocket into the vest. Compared with the price of his suit, they were so inexpensive that he could almost weep.

Klein touched the revolver in his holster and the metal vials tucked into the hidden pocket. Then he took out the portrait and entered the Divination Club.

Unsurprisingly, he encountered the beautiful receptionist Angelica.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti. I thought it would be several days before you returned." Angelica looked startled at first, then immediately offered him a radiant smile.

Klein removed his hat and sighed.

"Good afternoon, Miss Angelica. I had a dream at noon--a dream about Mr. Hanass Vincent and matters connected to him. You know that a fortune-teller must never disregard a dream. It may contain a divine revelation."

Bewildered by his charlatan-like declaration, Angelica nodded thoughtfully and asked with curiosity, "What did you dream?"

"I saw Hanass Vincent arguing with someone." Klein handed her the folded paper.

As Angelica opened the portrait, he pinched his glabella and observed the colors of her emotions.

"This man..." Angelica studied the lifelike drawing and sank into thought.

Klein saw her emotions turn the blue of contemplation, a perfectly normal reaction.

"This man..." Angelica repeated. Slowly, she raised her head. "I've seen him before."

Klein's thoughts raced. "When?"

"I don't remember the exact date. Perhaps a month ago? I saw him accompany Mr. Vincent to the door while they spoke quietly. I remember him because of his thick, untidy eyebrows--and because Mr. Vincent was smiling, which was unusual for him," Angelica recalled. "Yes, the man had gray-blue eyes and, like many men his age, very little hair."

"Did you see him at any other time, either before or afterward?" Klein asked gently.

Angelica shook her head.

"No, I'm certain I didn't. I don't even know his name. Honestly, if anyone but you had shown me such a portrait, I would suspect him of being a police officer investigating Mr. Vincent's death. Heh. But no revelation you receive seems strange to me. You are a true Seer."

My apologies. I actually am a police officer... Klein retorted inwardly. With a sigh, he said, "A true Seer understands how insignificant he is before the vastness of fate. We glimpse only one blurred corner. We receive revelations, never answers. We must reflect upon them constantly while preserving our reverence and fear. Every hint must be interpreted with care. We must never imagine ourselves clever enough to control fate."

As Klein summarized what he had learned over the past several weeks, his Spirit Vision suddenly grew clearer. He could even faintly discern details within Angelica's aura.

For an instant, he felt like a nearsighted man putting on a perfectly fitted pair of spectacles.

This... Has the digestion of my Seer potion begun to show an unmistakable effect? Klein stared in astonishment.

"I never imagined that a Seer as accomplished as you would still possess such fear and reverence for fate. It's truly admirable," Angelica said earnestly.

She had seen too many members of the Divination Club learn a few techniques and immediately claim they could perceive truth and alter destiny.

Klein withdrew his gaze and chuckled.

"The more we learn, the better we understand how small we truly are."

As he spoke, he examined his condition and reflected upon his previous experiences. He could now broadly summarize the essence of the acting method: act in accordance with the potion's name, identify the hidden principles governing that role, and adhere strictly to those principles.

Only then could he alter the state of his body, mind, and soul, bringing them closer to the lingering psyche within the potion and gradually digesting it.

Recognition of his identity as a Seer was merely the surface. The spiritual lightness produced by others' acknowledgment came from how their responses reinforced his confidence in certain divinatory behaviors. Together, those behaviors constituted the rules for digesting the Seer potion.

Help others interpret revelations and guide them toward better outcomes, while always maintaining fear and reverence toward fate. Never become egotistical or arrogant, and never trust an interpretation blindly... These are the principles I've identified so far--the essence of acting that will guide me onward. If my progress continues at this rate, I won't need half a year. I may fully digest the potion in two or three months, perhaps even two or three weeks.

...That sensation was unmistakable. No wonder the mysterious Mr. Zaratul said a Beyonder would clearly sense complete digestion without being taught. It is what it is... Just as I know now that, although my Spirit Vision has improved slightly, this is merely a milestone along the road, not the final destination.

With that realization, Klein could not help thanking the suited clown for teaching him with his life.

Without that lesson, Klein might have spent months at the Divination Club, learning the Seer's principles through countless successes and failures before finally beginning to act in strict accordance with them.

"Mr. Moretti, sometimes I think you might be a philosopher," Angelica said with a sigh.

"Among my friends, 'philosopher' is usually an insult," Klein replied in good humor.

He bowed, put on his hat, bade her farewell, and left.

Although Angelica did not know the gentleman's name or identity, Klein was not discouraged. He had learned enough to begin the next stage of his plan.

At 36 Zouteland Street, inside the Blackthorn Security Company, Dunn studied the portrait with his deep gray eyes.

"You want us to search for this man?"

"Yes." Klein had prepared his explanation long ago. "Captain, I mentioned that I would visit the Divination Club and observe the members' reactions to Hanass Vincent's sudden death. I found nothing yesterday, but today I accidentally learned that the man in this portrait once met privately with Vincent. I reviewed our investigation report just now, but found no mention of anyone resembling him."

There were no flaws in the account. Even if Dunn took the portrait to the Divination Club, Angelica would give him the same answer.

Dunn looked away from the drawing and laughed.

"It appears those compensation funds weren't wasted."

...Captain, isn't your memory supposed to be poor? Why would you remember the compensation now? Klein maintained his smile without replying.

"Did you draw this?" Dunn asked casually.

"Yes. I used ritualistic magic to help me," Klein answered with complete honesty.

Of course, telling the truth and telling the whole truth were entirely different things.

Dunn nodded. "Have Old Neil produce several copies. I'll ask Kenley and Royale to investigate and request the police department's assistance. If the clue proves useful, you'll have made another major contribution."

"May the Goddess bless us." Klein solemnly tapped four points on his chest.

All he needed Dunn and the others to discover was the man's name and identity. Once he had those, he could divine the man's location above the gray fog.

Although it was his day off, Klein did not return home after leaving the Blackthorn Security Company. Instead, he took a public carriage to the harbor and stopped outside the Evil Dragon Bar.

In his view, although a Seer could neither confront an enemy directly nor cast spells at a moment's notice, combat took many forms. Not every battle began as an unexpected encounter. Given adequate preparation, a Seer could defeat an enemy through ritualistic magic, just as Klein had resolved the magic-mirror divination at Selena's home.

That meant a Seer ought to carry essential oils, herbs, and small candles whenever possible. Otherwise, the lack of a simple material at a critical moment could leave him helpless and dead. Not everyone was like Selena, with an entire collection of mysticism supplies readily available.

Frequent practice had consumed most of the materials Klein had requisitioned. What remained was stored in the hidden pocket of his vest.

He patted the banknotes in his pocket, pushed open the Evil Dragon Bar's door, and strode inside.

It was noon, and only a few patrons occupied the bar. No rat-baiting or boxing matches were underway, leaving the place unusually quiet.

Klein observed the customers drinking beer and playing cards as he walked toward the billiard room that concealed the underground market.

At that moment, a muscular older man emerged with a torn admiral's coat draped across his shoulders.

"Aren't you the friend Old Neil brought here last time?" Reeking of alcohol, the blue-eyed man with disheveled brown hair examined Klein and laughed.

Guessing his identity, Klein removed his hat and bowed.

"Yes. How should I address you?"

"Old Neil mentions you often. I'm Swain, the proprietor." The older man's arms were thick and powerful, his muscles firm, and he carried himself like a military officer.

The former captain of Tingen's Mandated Punishers... Rumor says he once served in the Royal Navy... Klein replied politely, "It's a pleasure to meet you."

"If you ever need money, come and see me," Swain said with a laugh before turning toward the bar.

Klein's thoughts stirred, and he immediately called after him. "One moment, Mr. Swain. There's something I'd like to ask you."

Swain stopped and half turned, chuckling. "You look--well, quite similar."

No, I'm not the one with memory problems... The corner of Klein's mouth twitched. He held up the portrait. "Have you ever seen this gentleman?"

He had suddenly realized that Hanass Vincent had probably brought Selena to the underground market, which would explain how Elizabeth knew about the Evil Dragon Bar. If the man in the portrait had ties to Vincent, might he have visited as well?

Swain examined the drawing carefully and answered with certainty, "I remember him. He asked whether I possessed any documents or objects connected to the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range."

Documents or objects connected to the Hornacis main peak? Klein froze as the answer linked itself to another memory.

When he borrowed the journal issue concerning the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range from Deweyville Library, the librarian had casually mentioned that another patron had only recently returned it. Because of that, the librarian had remembered the title clearly and had not needed to consult the catalog cards.

Could the gentleman who borrowed that journal before me be the man in the portrait?

The man who witnessed the exchange of the Antigonus family's notebook?

Chapter 102: The Cloth Merchant

The more Klein considered the possibility, the more likely it seemed. Why else would anyone borrow such obscure journal issues?

Research into the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range is an exceptionally obscure field. Apart from the lecturers and associate professors specializing in it, an ordinary enthusiast would probably never even have heard of the subject. The original Klein was a history graduate, yet he learned of it only through the Antigonus family's notebook... Tingen may be a city of universities, but there can't be many people interested in this topic. And any scholars who are would probably use their universities' collections rather than borrow the journal from Deweyville Library.

Most importantly, the issue was borrowed only recently...

Viewed that way, the situation truly is suspicious. I wasn't perceptive enough to notice it... Sigh. It appears I have no talent for detective work or for imitating Sherlock Holmes...

While those thoughts raced through his mind, Swain, proprietor of the Evil Dragon Bar, asked in confusion, "Is something wrong?"

With patrons and bartenders nearby, he could only inquire indirectly.

"Not at all. I'm merely wondering how I might investigate this gentleman. As you know, Hanass Vincent died at home." Klein had prepared his excuse in advance.

He did not want the Mandated Punishers to become interested in the ancient ruins on the Hornacis main peak.

"Vincent was one of Tingen's better-known fortune-tellers. He visited this place often." Swain's answer was perfunctory at first. Then, as he searched his memory, he added, "Now that I think about it, the man in the portrait originally came here with Vincent..."

"That's precisely what I hoped to learn. Do you remember his name?" Klein pressed immediately.

Swain shook his head and chuckled.

"I don't ask customers for their names or identities unless I already know them, as I did Old Neil."

"I see." Klein deliberately allowed disappointment to show.

In truth, it did not matter whether Swain knew. Klein could investigate at Deweyville Library.

Anyone borrowing books from a privately funded library had to provide personal information and credible identification.

After all, Klein himself had needed a letter of introduction from a senior associate professor before he could obtain a library card.

Even if the man falsified his information, he probably left some trace that could assist my divination...

Deep in thought, Klein watched Swain return to the bar, then entered the billiard room.

He was in no hurry to visit Deweyville Library. First, he intended to complete his purchases. He could not know whether the investigation would lead him into danger or require ritualistic magic.

After passing through several rooms, Klein reached the underground market. Only a handful of stalls and customers were present, clear evidence that the busiest hours had not yet begun.

As he stepped forward, he suddenly noticed Ademisaul--the monster capable of smelling death--standing in a corner.

The young man's face was pale, and terror and madness lurked in his eyes. He noticed Klein at the same moment and looked over.

The instant their gazes met, Ademisaul covered his face with both hands and retreated toward the wall in panic.

He soon reached a side door and stumbled through it, fleeing the market.

Was that really necessary? I nearly blinded you last time, but I didn't actually do anything... Honestly, you're acting as though I'm the devil. Klein's expression stiffened slightly.

He shook his head with a smile, put the monster out of his mind, and approached a stall to begin purchasing the supplies he needed.

Half an hour later, Klein had spent several pounds--most of his private savings.

He counted the three pounds and seventeen soli remaining and felt a pang in his heart. Then he touched the small metal vials inside his black trench coat.

This one contains Amantha extract, the kind Madam Daly used.

This is powder made from dragon-tree bark and leaves.

Essential oil extracted from Slumber Flowers.

Dried chamomile petals.

And this is Holy Night Powder I prepared myself.

Klein mentally reviewed the contents of every small pocket so that he would not fumble for the correct ingredient at a critical moment.

With his aptitude for mysticism, he quickly memorized every location and headed for the exit.

Suddenly, a familiar figure appeared at the edge of his vision.

It was a young woman in a casual green dress. Her smooth black hair was soft and lustrous, and her round face and long eyes gave her a sweet appearance and refined bearing.

Isn't that the young woman who was trembling strangely on the public carriage? She seems perfectly well... I never expected her to be interested in mysticism... Klein slowed. After several seconds, he finally remembered where he had seen her.

Apart from Justice, whose face he had never viewed clearly, he had to admit that she was the most beautiful woman he had seen since transmigrating into this world.

The sweet, elegant young woman stood before a stall selling books on mysticism. Disregarding etiquette, she crouched down and ran her fingers across an ancient volume.

The book had a black hardcover bearing the Hermes title *Book of Witches*.

"It records witches' black magic. I haven't dared try any of it myself, but someone I know did, and it truly worked," the vendor said, seizing the opportunity to promote his merchandise.

The beautiful young woman considered the claim. "What do you imagine a witch to be?"

"A wicked person who brings calamity, disease, and suffering," the vendor answered after some thought.

Klein did not hear their exchange. He had already hurried out through the entrance, anxious to complete his investigation at Deweyville Library and return home in time to prepare dinner for his brother and sister. Tonight's menu was tomato oxtail soup.

At the Crown Turf Club in Backlund, Audrey Hall stood in a private box and watched the horses thunder around the track.

She wore a long white dress with engageantes, ruffled trim, and lace across the bodice. A veil descended from a hat decorated with blue ribbons and silk flowers, while pale fishnet gloves covered her hands. Her cool, distant gaze seemed out of place amid the venue's excitement.

Just as a racehorse crossed the finish line, her friend Viscount Glaint approached and spoke in a carefully lowered voice.

"Audrey, every time I see you, I discover a new angle from which you're beautiful."

"How may I help you?" In the past, Audrey might have basked in the compliment. Now, however, she could read Glaint's ulterior motive in his words and manner.

Glaint's father had died young, leaving him to inherit the title of viscount at twenty. He was a slightly thin young man. After looking both ways, he chuckled quietly.

"Audrey, I know a genuine Beyonder--one who doesn't belong to the royal family."

You've disappointed me every time you've made that claim... Audrey continued watching the racetrack and replied elegantly, "Really?"

"I swear upon my father's name. I've witnessed his Beyonder powers," Glaint whispered.

The news no longer excited Audrey as it once would have; she was a Beyonder herself now. To avoid arousing Glaint's suspicion, however, she widened her eyes, produced a delighted smile, and let her voice tremble.

"When can I meet him?"

It would be useful to know other Beyonders. I can't bring every trivial concern to the Tarot Club... Besides, I need to gather resources of my own to exchange with Mr. Fool and Mr. Hanged Man. Money can't solve everything... Sigh. After sending away that thousand pounds, I'll have to be more economical.

Glaint was thoroughly satisfied by her reaction. Turning toward the racetrack, he said, "I'm holding a literary and musical salon tomorrow afternoon."

At Deweyville Library, Klein removed his identification card and badge and presented them to the librarians.

"I am a probationary inspector with the Awwa County Police Department's Special Operations Division. I require your cooperation with an investigation," he declared in a deep voice, recalling the police films he had watched in his former life.

The librarians examined the card and badge, exchanged glances, and nodded.

"Ask whatever you need, officer."

Klein recited the names of several journals, including *New Archaeology*, then said, "I need the borrowing records for these publications from the past two months."

He recognized one librarian as the man who had assisted him before, but the fellow plainly did not remember him.

"Certainly. Please wait a moment." The librarians began searching and soon produced the recent records.

Klein studied them carefully, looking for whoever had borrowed the same journal issue as he had.

There were few names. In fact, only one person had repeatedly borrowed the relevant publication, including the issue Klein remembered. The earliest entry dated from the end of May, while the latest was from the previous Saturday--the day before Hanass Vincent's death.

Klein ran a finger over the borrower's information and committed it to memory.

Sirius Arapis, cloth merchant, resident of 19 Howes Street...

Chapter 103: Following His Instincts

He lives at 19 Howes Street?

As he memorized the information, Klein immediately noticed the significance of the address.

Yes, Welch lived on Howes Street. The Divination Club is on Howes Street. And this cloth merchant, Sirius Arapis, supposedly lives there as well... In that case, it isn't strange that Welch knew Hanass Vincent. The two may even have met through Sirius Arapis.

The clues suddenly linked together, and Klein's thoughts became clear.

He had previously been unable to understand how Welch became acquainted with Hanass Vincent. The banker's son had shown little interest in mysticism; money mattered far more to him than divination. Now, however, Klein had a plausible explanation.

According to several magazines, middle-class and wealthy residents commonly visited neighbors of comparable status to establish mutually beneficial social circles. Welch and the cloth merchant Sirius would have had both the motive and opportunity to become friends, since they lived in the same neighborhood around Howes Street...

Nor is it difficult to explain how Sirius knew Hanass Vincent, who regularly visited the Divination Club on Howes Street. Perhaps they met by chance, or Hanass once assisted him. Whatever the reason, two men who frequently crossed paths in the same district could easily become acquainted...

When Hanass wanted to sell an ancient book, Sirius introduced him to Welch, an undergraduate in the history department...

Hanass's dream contained the figure of the suspected evil god known as the True Creator. He also knew the proper structure of an incantation. That proves he was deeply involved in mysticism, and I can't dismiss the possibility that he belonged to a secret organization.

Nor can I rule out Sirius having introduced him to such an organization.

As the ideas flowed effortlessly through his mind, Klein could tell without divination that the information Sirius had submitted was at least partly genuine.

Even if his name isn't Sirius Arapis, he isn't a cloth merchant, and he doesn't actually live at 19 Howes Street, he must reside somewhere on Howes Street or in the immediate vicinity.

With this new line of reasoning, Klein examined the borrowing records once more.

Sirius last visited Deweyville Library on Saturday, the day before Selena's birthday party and Hanass Vincent's death. Several days have passed, yet he hasn't returned the issues he borrowed.

According to the earlier records, whenever he borrowed only two issues, he usually returned them the following day.

Does this mean he learned of Hanass's death and became too frightened to return to Deweyville Library?

Yes. He began by borrowing a range of unrelated historical books and journals, then gradually narrowed his search to precisely what he needed. That's very similar to the path my own reading took...

That suggests no knowledgeable scholar guided him. He had no senior associate professor from a university history department assisting him. He discovered everything through trial and error.

What would a frightened target do? There are two possibilities. First, if he had already gathered all the information he needed, he would travel directly to the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range. Second, if he still lacked something, he would hide and observe the situation, emerging only after confirming that Hanass's death would not implicate him.

Having reached that conclusion, Klein closed the borrowing ledger and returned it to the librarians. He then showed them the portrait and asked whether anyone recognized the man. Unfortunately, the library received too many visitors each day, and no one remembered such an ordinary-looking patron.

"Very well. Thank you for your assistance." Klein put away his identification and badge.

He had no intention of continuing the investigation alone. That would be both dangerous and troublesome. He planned to return to Zouteland Street and turn the case over to the captain and his teammates. Afterward, he would go home, prepare tomato oxtail soup for his siblings, and ascend above the gray fog to divine the target's location and condition.

"Is there anything else, officer?" one librarian asked earnestly, breathing a sigh of relief.

Klein nodded slightly. "No. I'll return if new clues emerge."

Holding his black cane in his left hand, he headed toward the exit.

At that moment, a man entered the library with his head bowed. He wore a double-breasted coat with its collar raised high.

As they passed, Klein caught a glimpse of thick, disheveled eyebrows and gray-blue eyes.

Those were features even the high collar could not conceal.

Sirius? Sirius Arapis? Here, by coincidence? Klein froze. He had never expected to encounter his target in person.

What kind of luck is this?

Isn't this far too coincidental?

He assessed his condition and felt the ache in his muscles. Pretending that nothing was wrong, he continued toward the door.

When your instincts speak, follow them. Safety comes first!

It doesn't matter if I lose this opportunity, as long as Sirius remains in Tingen.

The man in the double-breasted coat reached the counter and handed a pair of journals to a librarian.

"I'm returning these," he said in a low, muffled voice.

The librarian casually accepted the journals, looked at them, and abruptly froze.

He raised his head involuntarily, his expression changing as his body began to tremble.

"Is there a problem?" the man asked in a deep voice.

The question was a spark striking a fuse. The librarian instantly lost control, sprinted to the side, and shouted,

"Officer!"

"The criminal is here!"

Klein, who had not yet left the building, cursed furiously in his heart.

His right hand instinctively reached for his holster and drew the revolver.

The man froze for an instant, then turned and broke into a sprint.

He did not run toward the door. Instead, he charged for an oriel window at the side, apparently intending to smash through the glass and leap into the street.

Flustered, Klein turned and saw what was happening. Then an abrupt calm descended upon him.

He realized that although he feared the target, the target feared him even more.

In such a sudden encounter, the man can't judge my abilities. He doesn't know what I specialize in, so he'll instinctively avoid a direct confrontation and seek another escape route. Confident in his analysis, Klein raised the revolver and pulled the trigger.

At that instant, the man in the double-breasted coat abruptly rolled across the floor to evade the bullet.

He planted his right hand against the ground and propelled himself into the air toward the oriel window.

Click!

Klein's first chamber was empty.

But he had expected that. Taking advantage of Sirius's inability to dodge in midair, Klein aimed at his torso and pulled the trigger again.

Bang!

The silver demon-hunting bullet tore through the air and punched into Sirius's back.

Crash! Glass exploded as Sirius flew through the window, leaving crimson droplets across the glittering shards and windowsill.

With his target wounded, Klein's fear diminished. He ran forward, stepped on a chair, and vaulted through the broken window.

This was the ground-floor rear of Deweyville Library. A row of trees screened off a lush green lawn.

The injured Sirius was running to one side, attempting to reach a narrow alley between two buildings. Klein had never practiced firing at a moving target and dared not shoot recklessly. Carrying his cane in one hand and revolver in the other, he pursued the man in the black coat.

Tap! Tap! Tap!

He followed the drops of blood on the ground and slowly closed the distance.

As Sirius approached the corner, his pace became increasingly sluggish. Klein was waiting for a chance to capture him when fear suddenly pricked at his mind. The figure ahead no longer felt human, but like a wolf or tiger concealing a terrible danger.

It was the instinct of a Seer and a warning from his spirituality.

Klein immediately slowed and looked down at the trail of blood.

Unlike the earlier drops, Sirius's blood was now black.

At that moment, a violent gust struck him, and Sirius's face filled his vision.

Thick, untidy eyebrows. Gray-blue eyes. Numerous bulging warts. An open mouth displaying two rows of white teeth.

Sirius had launched a sudden counterattack!

His face grew clearer in Klein's vision, and a particularly putrid stench assaulted Klein's nose.

Sirius had leaped seven or eight meters, far beyond the ability of an ordinary human. But because Klein had stopped his pursuit in time, nearly ten meters had separated them.

As the distance shrank to two meters, strands of viscous saliva and clusters of revolting warts formed a horrifying spectacle that stretched Klein's nerves taut.

Without thinking, he exploited the brief immobility of Sirius's leap, raised his right hand, and fired again and again at the man's head.

Bang! Bang! Bang! Bang!

At such close range, the silver demon-hunting bullets bored through Sirius's skull. Blood sprayed outward, and his face grew progressively more mangled until he staggered backward.

Klein emptied the revolver in an instant. He instinctively began retreating, intending to put some distance between them while confirming the outcome.

Then, to his horror, Sirius strained to stand upright once more. Klein abruptly raised the cane in his left hand.

Smack!

The sturdy black cane, inlaid with silver, struck Sirius across the neck and left a dark-red welt.

Smack! Smack! Smack!

Acting on instinct, Klein rained blows upon his opponent until Sirius stumbled and collapsed.

Huff! Huff! Huff! Supporting himself with the cane, Klein drew deep breaths. His gaze remained fixed on the target, afraid Sirius might abruptly rise again.

By then, Sirius's head had been reduced almost to pulp. The warts gradually receded, and after several spasms, his body became still.

Klein did not immediately examine the corpse. Instead, he tossed his cane aside, took out his reserve demon-hunting rounds, and reloaded the revolver.

Only after completing that task did he steady himself, suppress his revulsion, kneel, and search the pockets of Sirius's double-breasted coat.

Chapter 104: Mr. Z

One pocket, two, three... Klein soon found a bloodstained wallet, a Deweyville Library card, two sets of brass keys, an empty pipe, a sheathed dagger, and several neatly folded letters.

He laid everything but the letters on the ground and inspected the wallet. It contained only a dozen or so soli and a few copper pennies.

Such fine workmanship. What a pity... Klein sighed distractedly. Had he not spent most of his private savings, buying a wallet would have been on today's schedule.

Shaking his head, he unfolded the letters and skimmed them.

"Dear Mr. Z,

"Please allow me to defend myself. When Hanass and I sold the Antigonus family's notebook, it was neither stupidity nor betrayal. While in our possession, it displayed nothing extraordinary.

"I suspect the notebook is alive--a wicked object possessing a degree of life and intelligence, dangerous enough to require sealing.

"It reveals different contents at different times and to different people!

"The lamb at the police station has confirmed this.

"Although everything the notebook reveals appears genuine and is supported by ample evidence, I believe it will show its complete contents only to a descendant of the Antigonus family.

"Hanass and I saw only trivial family affairs, a general account of the Nation of the Evernight on the Hornacis main peak, and the three Sequence potion formulas we previously submitted to you.

"As you know, the Secret Order controls the Seer pathway and possesses formidable tracking abilities. Hanass and I therefore judged it too dangerous to retain the notebook. The value it had shown us did not justify the risk.

"Unable to await your reply, we agreed to sell it to Welch, who lived on the same street. He collected relics and ancient books and could afford a high price. You already know what followed.

"That is the first matter I wished to explain. As I write, Hanass is dead. He suffered a heart attack in his sleep. God must have blessed him, sparing him the suffering that would have followed capture by the heretics.

"I had no choice but to move somewhere safer and more concealed. I scarcely dared leave the house. Fortunately, the lamb informed me that Hanass attracted the heretics' attention neither because of the notebook nor because his identity had been exposed. He had simply taken a foolish young woman as his disciple, intending to develop her gradually into one of us.

"She secretly copied one of his incantations and attempted magic-mirror divination while a Nighthawk heretic was present. You can infer the rest.

"Regrettably, the lamb's position is too low to obtain the full details.

"According to all available information, the heretics do not yet suspect me. Their investigation ended with Hanass's sudden death.

"I will therefore return to public life and borrow several more journals from Deweyville Library in search of clues.

"The Antigonus family also controlled the Seer pathway. They must have divined their own destruction and left behind secret treasures capable of restoring their house!

"There is every reason to believe that treasure lies within the ruins of the Nation of the Evernight on the main peak of the Hornacis mountain range!"

Klein's pupils contracted, and the letter nearly slipped from his fingers.

The Antigonus family controlled the Seer pathway?

What a coincidence!

Thunder seemed to peal repeatedly through his mind, leaving him dazed. It felt like destiny.

The notebook that killed the original Klein and indirectly enabled my transmigration came from the Antigonus family, who controlled the Seer pathway. Emperor Roselle's diary led me to choose Seer, and Roselle favored that pathway because of the mysterious Zaratul--leader of the Secret Order, which also controls it!

...It's like a suffocating net woven by Fate.

What is hiding behind all this?

Letter in hand, Klein paced as he sought corroborating evidence.

The Secret Order controlled by the Zaratul family is pursuing the Antigonus family's relics. If both possessed the same pathway, they would have ample motive: filling gaps in their Sequences, obtaining rare ingredients for advancement, or acquiring the other family's experience in resisting loss of control...

From that perspective, it is entirely reasonable that the Antigonus family possessed at least part of the Seer pathway.

And when I divined clues to the Clown potion, nearly every image concerned the Antigonus family. The only exception was the suited clown of the Secret Order... The revelation meant that every scene contained a possible clue to obtaining Clown. I failed to recognize the essential connection.

With two lines of evidence supporting it, Klein almost completely believed Sirius's claim. He also understood why the forbidden whispers repeatedly spoke the word Hornacis.

He had first heard them immediately after drinking the Seer potion.

Gravely, he guessed that surviving contact with an Antigonus relic and becoming a Beyonder of the Seer pathway were the two conditions required to hear those whispers.

Did the Antigonus family truly hide a treasure in the ruins on the Hornacis main peak? No. I mustn't dwell on it. The notebook alone killed so many people. A complete treasure would be far more terrifying!

Klein shook his head and read the final sheet.

"Honorable Mr. Z, I hope to receive your assistance. I believe the treasure will interest you as well.

"Until then, I will present myself as an ordinary man with an ordinary interest in history.

"When the end of days arrives, I will offer every lamb in Tingen as a sacrifice to God.

"Your humble servant,

"Sirius Arapis."

Finishing the letter, Klein felt an urge to laugh.

Heh. Why do I feel as though I just saved Tingen? What was this lunatic planning? Heretics truly are unbelievable...

Who is Mr. Z? He appears highly placed--at least equal in Sequence to the captain.

And where was Sirius sending this? He omitted the address... Sensible caution for a cultist. He probably intended to add it immediately before dispatching the letter.

Wait. If the Antigonus family controlled the Seer pathway, could Clown be one of the three formulas Sirius extracted from the notebook?

Highly likely!

In that instant, Klein saw a path to the Clown potion.

Sirius had not carried the formula, but might have recorded it at his hideout. More importantly, it remained in his memories.

Klein looked at the corpse and considered how to make a dead man speak.

The answer came immediately.

"Mediumship!"

Spirit Mediums could communicate directly with spirits before they dispersed. Through ritualistic magic, Seers and Mystery Pryers could achieve something similar.

When confronting the suited clown's corpse, Klein had lacked time, materials, and confidence. By the time they returned to Blackthorn Security Company, most of the spirit had vanished, leaving even a Spirit Medium able to obtain only superficial information.

Now, however, Klein possessed the necessary tools and had already communicated with lingering resentment through dream divination.

The danger is that I might encounter something horrific, as the captain did in Hanass's dream... But he was merely weakened for two days, not gravely injured. I can try.

He hesitated for less than twenty seconds. He would not surrender this opportunity.

Looking toward the library's shattered window, he saw a crowd gathering beyond it.

Klein took out his identification and badge, returned to the window, and addressed the onlookers.

"I am a probationary inspector with the Awwa County Police Special Operations Department. I have shot and killed a criminal. Take this badge to the nearest police station and request assistance.

"Everyone else, cordon off this area. Allow no one to approach and contaminate the scene."

"Yes, officer!" The librarian whose shout had caused the trouble hurriedly accepted the badge.

Once the area was secured, Klein returned to the corpse. Fortunately, the crowd could not see that the dead man looked more monster than human.

He laid down his cane and revolver, reached inside his trench coat, and removed a metal vial.

Using a mediumship ritual combined with dream divination, he would make the dead man speak.

Chapter 105: Spirit Channeling

Klein unscrewed the golden vial and inhaled its sharp, invigorating scent.

It contained Holy Night Powder made from Slumber Flowers, Dragon Blood grass, dark-red sandalwood, mint, and other herbs. The mixture was simple, so he had prepared some as soon as he purchased the ingredients. Now it would prove useful.

He poured a little powder into his palm and composed himself, his irises darkening.

After returning the vial to his pocket, Klein infused the powder with spirituality and scattered it as he walked in a circle around Sirius's corpse.

An invisible barrier rose, separating them from the outside world.

Klein brushed the remaining powder from his hand, took out several other vials, and sprinkled Amantha extract and other liquids around the enclosed space.

His arrangement differed from Old Neil's ritual at Ray Bieber's house because its purpose was different. Old Neil had poured the liquids first, then used Holy Night Powder to create an environment second only to a true altar in holiness and serenity. Klein used the powder first, isolating Sirius's lingering spirituality from outside interference while preserving an environment barely sufficient for the ritual.

Had he followed Old Neil's procedure, he would have purified the remaining spirit and made communication impossible.

Once prepared, Klein entered Cogitation and softly recited in Hermes,

"I pray for the power of the dark night.

"I pray for the power of mystery.

"I pray for the Goddess's loving grace.

"I pray that You permit me to communicate with the heretic's lingering spirit within this altar."

As the incantation echoed through the sealed space, a vast, terrifying, mysterious power descended upon him.

His eyes turned completely black, without pupils or whites.

Seizing the moment, Klein repeated inwardly,

"The formula for the Clown potion.

"The formula for the Clown potion."

Through Cogitation, he entered a dreamlike state.

He found himself in a hazy gray world without sky or ground. Remaining unusually lucid, he observed a transparent, ethereal figure and touched Sirius's spiritual remnant with his right hand.

The scene changed with a thunderous rumble.

A dark-red desk appeared, bearing three candles in a silver holder and a blank sheet of paper.

Holding a pen, Sirius wrote in Loenese:

"This is the second formula. In the notebook, it is called Clown.

"The supplementary ingredients are eighty milliliters of pure water, five drops of tornapple juice, seven grams of black-rimmed sunflower powder, ten grams of golden cloak grass powder, and three drops of poison hemlock.

"The main ingredients are one crystal from the horn of a mature Hornacis gray mountain goat and one complete human-faced rose."

Sirius evidently knew the formula by heart and finished quickly.

After pausing for coffee, he unwound a silver pendulum from his wrist. Closing his eyes, he murmured phrases such as "the end of days," "peace of mind," "hope for the Lord's blessing," and "confession."

When the prayer ended, Klein finally saw the pendulum clearly.

A thumb-sized human figurine hung from its silver chain. It possessed the single eye characteristic of a Giant and dangled upside down, its bound legs connected to the chain above.

The Giant's lone eye suddenly glowed faintly red.

Crack!

The vision shattered. Klein's knees buckled, nearly sending him to the ground.

Pain exploded through his skull as though someone had struck him with a club. His vision turned bloodred, and his hands involuntarily moved to protect his knees.

Several seconds later, he recovered and forced himself upright. His spirituality felt terribly depleted, as if the mind-piercing whispers had returned.

Thanks to his progress in digesting the potion, however, the reaction quickly subsided.

The Hanged Giant, the True Creator... Were Sirius and Hanass members of the Aurora Order? But the captain saw a colossal cross in Hanass's dream. The terrifying figure crucified upon it wasn't the Aurora Order's Hanged Giant...

Klein took two deep breaths and waited for his spirituality to recover.

The Aurora Order had arisen two or three centuries earlier. Its members worshiped the True Creator, symbolized by the Hanged Giant. They believed every human possessed divinity and that enduring countless trials would allow a person to accumulate enough of it to become an angel.

Nighthawk records identified the Order's Sequence 9 as Secrets Suppliant. Such Beyonders could perceive mysterious and horrifying existences and possessed considerable knowledge of sacrifices and some ritualistic magic. Senior Secrets Suppliants frequently developed distorted worldviews and readily lost control.

Little was known of the Order's Sequence 7, while Sequence 8 was Listener--a terrifying profession. Listeners directly heard the whispers of hidden entities and consequently gained powerful, twisted, unusual abilities. Yet those unable to advance rarely survived five years. The Nighthawks' assessment was blunt: every Listener was insane, however normal one might appear.

As the records passed through his mind, Klein tentatively identified Sirius as a Secrets Suppliant.

According to the description, Secrets Suppliants are as helpless in direct combat as Seers. That matches Sirius's initial behavior. Did his wounds cause the transformation afterward? Frye did say every Beyonder undergoes unusual changes upon death...

Klein traced four points on his chest and praised the Goddess.

Once his spirituality recovered slightly, he concluded the ritual properly and dismantled the spiritual barrier.

A gust of wind swept past. Forcing himself to examine the corpse, Klein noticed one wart remaining on Sirius's ruined face. It was dark purple, almost black, with liquid and faint light seemingly moving inside.

"What sort of transformation is that?" Klein rubbed his temples and wisely refrained from touching it.

He retrieved his cane and leaned upon it. Sirius's spirituality had now been utterly destroyed; not even Daly could communicate with him.

Before long, Captain Dunn arrived with Leonard and Kenley.

"Your fate seems remarkably entangled with Beyonders and evil powers. In only a few weeks, you've encountered more supernatural incidents than we usually see in months," Leonard joked while examining the corpse.

"Perhaps it isn't coincidence," Klein replied, recalling the red chimney from his divination, the palace on the Hornacis peak, and the unseen attention focused upon him. He used the opportunity to mention the possibility in passing.

Dunn surveyed the scene, then fixed his gray eyes on Klein. "You channeled his spirit?"

Traces of Holy Night Powder and the aroma of essential oils remained.

"Yes. I was afraid his spirituality would disperse before you arrived," Klein answered truthfully.

"You look unwell. Are you all right?" the short Kenley asked.

Klein handed Dunn the unsent letter and recounted events from the beginning, explaining how Selena's visit to the Evil Dragon Bar led him to question Swain, and how Swain's mention of Hornacis materials reminded him of Deweyville Library.

Leonard listened with a smile, then interrupted. "So you brought your identification here and examined the borrowing records. But why did you fight him? Knowing you, even if you met face-to-face, you would pretend not to recognize him, leave, and ask us for help."

"There was no reason to risk yourself," Dunn agreed while reading. "Once you confirmed he remained in Tingen, we could have found him."

Embarrassed, Klein replied, "The librarian recognized him and shouted for the police.

"I could hardly pretend not to hear."

Leonard and Kenley exchanged looks. One suppressed a laugh; the other turned away.

Dunn nodded and returned his attention to the letter. "What did the channeling reveal?"

"I saw a pendulum shaped like the Hanged Giant. Its single eye flashed bloodred, and then I was expelled from the ritual," Klein answered honestly.

For now, he concealed the Clown formula. If the others found Sirius's hideout and records, reporting it would earn him nothing additional. If they failed, he could disclose it later and receive another contribution toward the potion's ingredients--two rewards from one task, a technique inspired by Old Neil.

"The Aurora Order?" Dunn murmured before asking several related questions.

After Klein answered, Dunn noticed his fatigue and waved him away.

"Well done. You foiled a plot against Tingen. Go home and rest. Kenley, bring Old Neil here."

Dunn shook his head with a bitter smile. "Before Sequence 6, Beyonders of the Sleepless pathway possess few auxiliary abilities. We can perform only the simplest ritualistic magic."

"Does that mean the pathway gains such abilities beginning at Sequence 6?" Klein asked curiously.

"Yes," Dunn confirmed.

On the carriage ride back to Daffodil Street, Klein nearly fell asleep several times. He lumbered inside, removed his hat and coat, and collapsed onto the sofa.

He awoke with a start sometime later and opened his pocket watch.

"Melissa will return in half an hour, Benson in forty-five minutes... If I don't get up now, dinner will be an hour late."

Rubbing his forehead, Klein entered the kitchen, washed his face with cold water, and took out the oxtail, tomatoes, carrots, and onions he had bought that afternoon.

After preparing the ingredients, he suddenly froze. The domestic scene formed a strange contrast with the afternoon's violence.

"I am a man who just saved Tingen..." Klein murmured in amusement.

Then he put on a white apron and began making dinner.

Chapter 106: Artist Klein

After eight that evening, Benson covered his mouth and gave a satisfied burp as he studied the shallow pool of soup remaining in his bowl.

"Even after eating it three times, I still find it delicious. The tomatoes' sweet acidity and the oxtail's chewy texture create a unique, perfect flavor. Klein, I'm sorry Blackthorn Security Company deprived Tingen of such an outstanding chef."

Melissa leaned back and nodded silently in agreement.

"Only because you haven't tasted truly fine cooking," Klein replied modestly. "Someday, we should try authentic Intis cuisine at Bonaparte Restaurant on Howes Street, and southern specialties at Coastline Restaurant in Golden Indus Borough."

The newspapers often featured both establishments, where a meal averaged one and a half pounds per person.

"I prefer your cooking," Melissa said without hesitation.

Benson chuckled. "Still, the tomato oxtail soup seems to lack something. Perhaps bread isn't the proper accompaniment?"

"Rice would be best," Klein agreed.

"Rice..." Melissa murmured longingly.

In northern Tingen, rice was available only at a few specialized restaurants. To Benson and Melissa, it existed chiefly in newspapers and textbooks.

"Once we've saved another six months' salary, we'll take a holiday at Desi Bay and try the food there," Klein promised.

Desi Bay lay in the far south of Loen, with a third of its territory belonging to Feynapotter. It was famous for abundant sunshine, beautiful scenery, and paella.

Before Melissa could object to the expense, Klein added, "I should receive another raise in three months. Then we can travel and still save for necessities."

"Why?" both siblings asked.

Klein coughed lightly. "Because of my professional expertise, the police department--which frequently cooperates with our company--intends to employ me part-time as a historical consultant. It will pay at least two additional pounds a week. If you ever see me wearing a police uniform or carrying police identification, don't be alarmed.

"Naturally, government offices move with the speed of a ninety-year-old woman. There are procedures and a thorough background examination. For the next two months, I'll often spend my days off at Khoy University, studying with my mentor and other faculty members."

Seeing their astonishment, he added solemnly, "As Emperor Roselle said, one is never too old to learn."

After several seconds, Benson said with rueful emotion, "Is it too late for me to enroll at a university? Knowledge truly is wealth."

And power, Klein added inwardly.

"You should begin with Klein's grammar books and classical literature texts," Melissa said, stealing the words from his mouth.

Benson's expression shifted. Finally, he gritted his teeth. "Give them to me tonight. Even if they do nothing but put me to sleep, I'll read for an hour--no, an hour and a half--every day. I swear by the Goddess! If I fail, I'm a curly-haired baboon!"

Klein immediately smiled. "No problem."

The next morning, he hung his coat and hat in the break room, then followed Rozanne's instructions to the duty room outside Chanis Gate.

Captain Dunn and Nighthawks Frye, Seeka, Royale, Leonard, and Kenley were already present.

"We hold a routine meeting every Thursday to review recent missions and discuss difficulties," Dunn explained.

I've survived plenty of regular meetings myself... Klein found a seat. "Do I need to introduce myself?"

Dunn smiled and turned to Kenley. "Brief us on the Sirius Arapis investigation."

Kenley was a former civilian employee of average height, with thick brown hair, a compact, muscular build, and a capable appearance.

"With Old Neil's assistance, we located Sirius's hideout. The books and objects there confirm that he was an outer member of the Aurora Order and a Secrets Suppliant.

"Evidence also proves that he and Hanass Vincent sold the Antigonus family's notebook to Welch. Anyone unfamiliar with Welch can ask Klein.

"We recovered three valuable potion formulas: Sequence 9 Seer, Sequence 9 Apprentice, and Sequence 8 Clown.

"Our next task is to investigate Sirius's social circle and correspondence, locate other peripheral Aurora Order members, and identify the cultist who infiltrated the police department. Everyone associated with Hanass must also be reinvestigated."

Dunn nodded and looked at Klein. "As you heard, we found the Clown formula, though the Holy Cathedral must verify it.

"Your contribution against the Aurora Order was crucial. Together with your role in killing a Secret Order member, you will soon have enough merit for promotion. But not everyone is Daly. Suppress your desire and wait three years. Discovering the formula must not tempt you into risking loss of control."

Captain, you don't understand the power of acting... And I confirmed the formula above the gray fog last night. Klein nodded obediently. "I'll control myself."

Seeka Tron, the quiet, white-haired Midnight Poet, reported that they still had no clues concerning Instigator Tris and suspected he had fled Tingen.

After the meeting, Klein resumed his mysticism lessons with Old Neil. That afternoon, Gawain put him through strength, endurance, and coordination exercises.

At five, with the sun still bright, Klein showered, changed, and took a public carriage to Besik Street.

He had forgotten neither the red chimney from his divination nor the suspected Psychology Alchemist who purchased ingredients for the Spectator potion. Both were inconvenient to investigate as a Nighthawk.

"Number 27, Henry's Private Detective Agency... This is it."

The newspapers described the agency as reliable. Klein donned a mask, lowered his top hat, raised his collar, and climbed upstairs.

He knocked on the half-open door.

"Come in," called a phlegmy voice.

Inside the open-plan office, four employees occupied partitioned cubicles.

"Detective Henry. How may I help you?" A man in a white shirt and black vest approached, pipe in hand. He had a prominent jaw, sharp eyebrows, and dark-blue eyes that appraised his client.

Shielding half his face with his collar, Klein said hoarsely, "I have two commissions. What are your rates?"

"That depends on their difficulty." Henry indicated the guest sofa.

Once seated, Klein unfolded a drawing. "First, find a house with a chimney like this and identify its owner and present tenants."

The image, produced above the gray fog through prayer to himself, showed the chimney's color and surroundings.

"Excellent work..." Henry murmured, then frowned. "The task isn't complex, but it requires time and manpower. Seven pounds, and at least two weeks."

"Agreed. Second, find this man and determine his identity." Klein presented another portrait. "He occasionally visits the Evil Dragon Bar near the harbor. He must not notice your investigators. His powers of observation are formidable."

Klein hoped contact with a Psychology Alchemist might yield valuable information or materials--perhaps even a formula he could trade to Justice.

"Three or four pounds. Your drawing will save us considerable time," Henry replied.

"Ten pounds altogether?" The price pained Klein.

"Yes. Two pounds as a deposit, another three to five upon progress, and the balance at completion."

"I'll return next week." Klein avoided bargaining lest the observant detective remember him too clearly.

After signing a standard contract, he surrendered two one-pound notes. Only one pound and seventeen soli remained of his savings.

Henry watched the masked man hurry away, suspicion in his eyes as he smoked.

Why seek that particular chimney?

He must be an artist--or at least a professional sketch artist...

That afternoon, at Viscount Glaint's luxurious mansion, Audrey arrived with her maid and offered the host her hand. He brushed it with a ceremonial kiss.

"Your beauty enhances my salon," Glaint said, then lowered his voice. "The lady is already here. She's a Beyonder--and an author."

Chapter 107: Fors

"An author?" Audrey asked casually while observing Glaint's reaction.

Since they were discussing an ordinary subject, she did not need to worry about her maid Annie overhearing.

Glaint straightened and chuckled. "Yes. I believe you've read her work. She wrote *Stormwind Mountain Villa*, which has received considerable acclaim over the past two months."

"I enjoyed it, especially the composed Lady Sissi," Audrey replied with a faint smile, inwardly rolling her eyes at her own hypocrisy.

Her current interests had nothing to do with novels. She had stopped reading *Stormwind Mountain Villa* a month ago, only a third of the way through it. Since joining the Tarot Club, encountering the powerful Fool, and becoming a Beyonder, she had immersed herself in mysticism and the systematic study of psychology.

Glaint guided her toward a sofa. "I'm certain Miss Fors Wall will impress you. She's much like Lady Sissi--calm, intellectual, and lazy.

"And, my dear Audrey, will you play the piano later? Nothing could be a finer tribute to literature."

Audrey studied his profile. His expression, tone, and posture all conveyed a desire to show off.

He wants to use me to impress his guests... It was as though she were seeing her old friend for the first time.

Maintaining an elegant smile, she replied, "My teacher, the pianist Mr. Vicanell, says my playing has deteriorated and requires more practice."

Before Glaint could answer, he noticed a woman selecting desserts from a long table. "Audrey, that is Miss Fors Wall, author of *Stormwind Mountain Villa*."

Fors was about twenty-three and roughly five feet five. She wore a pale-yellow ruffled dress, and her slightly curled brown hair framed pale-blue eyes and a contemplative smile.

In less than three seconds, Audrey noticed several details.

Faint yellow stains mark her fingers. She smokes...

She has calluses where a pen rests, consistent with being an author...

Her movements reveal unexpected strength. Either she exercises, possesses a naturally strong constitution, or once practiced another profession...

The calm, rational precision of her novel probably reflects that former occupation...

Her eyes and emotions are relaxed. She seems to look down upon Glaint and me. Is this the superiority a Beyonder feels toward ordinary people?

If Glaint discovered her identity accidentally, she should be anxious. The unknown always inspires fear. Her composure suggests she approached him deliberately after learning of our interests. She's confident about what will happen next...

Why approach Glaint? Does she need money, ingredients from a noble treasury, or assistance with something else?

Glaint began the introductions. "Madam, this is Miss Audrey Hall, the most dazzling jewel in Backlund. Her father, Earl Hall, is a trusted adviser to His Majesty and a respected cabinet member."

"Good afternoon, Madam Fors. *Stormwind Mountain Villa* remains beside my bed even now." Audrey curtsied according to aristocratic etiquette.

Because I still haven't finished it after a month...

Fors returned the greeting simply. "Good afternoon, Miss Audrey. Your beauty is unforgettable. I believe I already have an idea for my next novel. Viscount Glaint also tells me you're exceptionally gifted in music."

In public, they exchanged nothing beyond compliments. Fors soon returned to the dining table in pursuit of cream cake, while Audrey accompanied Glaint toward the sitting room, reviewing every detail she had observed in preparation for their later conversation.

So absorbed was she that she stepped on her dress and nearly fell.

Annie caught her in time. "Miss, this dress's unusual design means you mustn't walk too quickly," she whispered.

"I know." Audrey nodded, her face reddening.

I was so busy observing everyone else that I forgot to watch my own feet...

Throughout the salon, Audrey met celebrated authors, critics, and musicians while preserving her sweet, elegant smile. At last, just as her facial muscles began to ache, she noticed Glaint's signal.

Several minutes later, she excused herself to visit the washroom. Once certain no one was following, she went to the ground-floor study.

"I have something to discuss with Glaint," she told Annie. "Guard the door and let no one enter."

Annie found nothing unusual in the request. She knew Audrey and Glaint shared an interest in mysticism and often discussed it privately.

Audrey entered and locked the door. Glaint sat behind the desk, playing with a pen, while Fors stood before a bookcase and leafed idly through a volume.

"Allow me to introduce you properly," Glaint said, setting down the pen. "Madam Fors, a genuine Beyonder."

"Is that so?" Audrey deliberately exaggerated her doubt.

Fors returned the book and smiled. "It appears I must demonstrate."

She approached the door and grasped its handle. Audrey's vision blurred. Fors seemed to become incorporeal and passed directly through the door.

When Audrey concentrated again, the woman was gone.

Seconds later, the handle turned and the locked door opened. Fors walked in from outside, smiling. Annie, standing nearby, seemed unaware that anything had happened.

"What an extraordinary ability!" Glaint exclaimed.

Audrey drew a breath. "I have no further doubts."

The demonstration also clarified Fors's motive. A Beyonder with such an ability would have little difficulty obtaining money or materials.

Glaint has no Beyonder guards... She wants to use our status and resources to accomplish something. Audrey struggled to maintain her Spectator state.

Fors chuckled. "Let us be honest with one another. We don't have much time.

"I was once a clinic doctor. More than two years ago, I received an opportunity to become a Beyonder.

"I need you to do something for me. In return, I will sell you a Sequence potion formula and the corresponding ingredients, allowing you to become true Beyonders."

Glaint immediately asked, "What do you need?"

"My partner is imprisoned and awaiting final judgment. I want you to save her, by whatever means necessary," Fors answered simply.

Audrey frowned. "Madam Fors, wouldn't the ability you just demonstrated make you better suited to the task?"

Fors laughed and shook her head. "No. She cannot pass through the places I can. I can only visit and speak with her.

"Besides, risking my life to rescue her seems unwise. Life is short, and we have many things left to do."

Observing Fors's face and posture, Audrey asked carefully, "What crime did your partner commit?"

Fors immediately looked awkward.

"My partner is highly respected and can inspire heartfelt obedience. She is kind and possesses excellent character. Well... the method she used to persuade a thug may have been somewhat excessive..."

After entrusting his commissions to Detective Henry, Klein resumed his usual schedule: mysticism lessons in the morning and combat training in the afternoon. The regularity almost made him forget he was a Nighthawk. Even his apparent curse of encountering supernatural incidents seemed to have disappeared.

On Saturday, it was his turn to guard Chanis Gate.

"Help yourself to the coffee I left here or the black tea in the clerks' office," Dunn said, surveying the room with his deep gray eyes.

Having already prepared an excuse for his siblings, Klein nodded happily. "All right, Captain. You truly are a generous gentleman."

Dunn laughed. "They'll help you relax. Remaining tense all night is bad for your health."

He took his hat and cane and headed for the door. Just before leaving, he turned back.

"I forgot to remind you: no matter what you hear, do not open Chanis Gate unless it opens from within.

"Remember--no matter what you hear, no matter what happens."

Captain, that's rather frightening... Klein instantly tensed. The basement's darkness suddenly seemed stronger than the gaslight.

Chapter 108: Deep into the Night

Though dawn was still hours away, gas lamps illuminated the well-ventilated but silent underground passage. Protected by glass, their dim-yellow flames burned steadily along the empty corridor.

Klein sat in the duty room, casually leafing through the newspapers, magazines, and books piled before him while keeping part of his attention on the corridor and Chanis Gate.

His trench coat and top hat hung on a rack near the entrance. His cane rested within easy reach against the wall.

The rich aroma of coffee filled the room. Klein inhaled appreciatively, then massaged his temples to combat his heavy head and weary body.

As a university student on Earth, he had often gone to sleep at five in the morning and awakened at noon. During his years of employment, he sometimes stayed awake all night and still arrived at work full of energy. But games had been exciting, novels engrossing, and television and movies entertaining.

This world lacked nearly every necessity for an enjoyable night awake.

Seriously, Emperor Roselle. If you intended to show off, you should have done it properly. Devote your finite life to the infinite cause of leading this world into the information age! Klein complained inwardly. At least newspapers, magazines, and increasingly interesting novels existed.

He had initially considered studying to keep himself alert, but serious concentration would conflict with his duty. Once absorbed, he might miss activity in the passage or changes at Chanis Gate.

Whew. Klein raised the coffee and blew carefully across its surface.

He took a sip, savoring the fragrance before swallowing.

"Fermo coffee from the Paz Valley. Extremely bitter, but wonderfully invigorating," he praised, setting down the cup.

The Paz Valley lay on the Southern Continent and produced excellent coffee beans. Intis and Loen currently contested the region, having established colonies on opposite banks and destroyed the original Paz Kingdom.

In the eerie stillness, Klein picked up a magazine and discovered it was *Ladies' Aesthetic*, a publication devoted to fashion and courtship.

"This must belong to Rozanne..." Amused and intrigued, he began turning the pages.

Thanks to advances in photography during the past decade, the magazine contained numerous monochrome photographs as well as illustrations. Famous theatrical and musical performers modeled fashionable clothing combinations. In only seven years, the once-regional Backlund publication had spread across the kingdom.

"Lovely dress. The model is beautiful too..." Klein made no attempt to conceal his appreciation.

He was a physically and mentally healthy man who enjoyed admiring beautiful women. Yet his goal was to return home, so he kept his distance from romance rather than waste another person's time or leave emotional burdens behind.

As for streetwalkers, he was far too fastidious.

Benson and Melissa were already bonds he could no longer break. Somehow, he would have to repay them in the future... His heart grew heavy, and he sighed.

The farther one wandered from home, the more melancholy quiet nights became.

Losing interest in the models, he set aside the magazine and picked up a novel.

"*Stormwind Mountain Villa*, by Fors Wall."

The tranquil night, yellow light, and leather-bound volume reminded him of renting books in his youth. Nostalgia prompted him to read.

The novel followed Lady Sissi, a five-foot-five, ninety-eight-pound woman who became a governess at Fruys Mountain Villa.

One pound is roughly half a kilogram... Is this an alternate world's *Jane Eyre*? Klein ran his fingers over the smooth paper and predicted what would follow.

Just when he concluded it was a romance, an evil spirit appeared. When he decided it was a ghost story, Lady Sissi revealed herself as a detective and produced a brilliant deduction. The moment he became certain it was detective fiction, the male lead suffered a blow to the head, lost his memory, and transformed the story into heartrending melodrama.

"So in the end, it's still a romance." Klein closed the book and drank more coffee.

Thump!

Thump! Thump! Thump!

Violent knocking suddenly reverberated through the dim, empty corridor.

Klein jolted upright, every nerve tense.

He instinctively drew the revolver from his shoulder holster, adjusted the cylinder and hammer, and slowly approached the doorway in search of the sound.

Thump! Thump! Thump!

Bang! Bang! Bang!

The pounding intensified. Following it with his gaze, Klein found himself staring at the outward-opening black doors engraved with seven Sacred Emblems.

"It's coming from beyond Chanis Gate?" He narrowed his eyes, heart hammering.

Bang! Bang! Bang!

The gate trembled beneath tremendous impacts.

"Surely not... Something happens on my first watch? Did transmigration give me a constitution that attracts misfortune?" Cold sweat slicked Klein's hand around the revolver.

Then he remembered Dunn's instruction: never open Chanis Gate, whatever he heard, unless it opened from within.

Could this be normal? Klein abruptly calmed.

The commotion continued, but the heavy black gate merely shook and displayed no other change.

"So it is normal. That nearly frightened me to death..." Klein muttered, preparing to return to the duty room.

A piercing grind stopped him. Chanis Gate bulged outward, and a crack opened between its doors.

Screeech!

Through the gap, Klein saw a figure no taller than a man's forearm. It wore a miniature black court gown in an antique style, marred by a conspicuous stain.

Its roughly fashioned face had black eyes and tightly closed lips.

A cloth puppet!

As Klein reflexively raised his revolver, the puppet pressed itself into the opening and unfurled a sheet of paper.

Symbols representing concealment covered it--some familiar, others unknown--together forming a vertical eye.

Before Klein could comprehend the sight, an invisible force seized the court-gowned puppet and dragged it behind Chanis Gate.

Creak!

The doors closed. The pounding ceased.

Silence returned to the basement as though nothing had happened.

"I need to tell the captain that Chanis Gate opened from within... though it closed again." His thoughts finally resumed, bringing alarm, fear, and confusion.

Then he remembered the puppet's file, which official membership had granted him clearance to read.

"Number: 0625.

"Name: Misfortune Cloth Puppet.

"Danger Grade: 3. Considerably dangerous. Exercise caution. May be requisitioned only for operations involving at least three people.

"Security clearance: Official Nighthawk or higher.

"Sealing method: Isolation from humans.

"Description: A cloth puppet wearing a court gown fashionable around the year 1300. The gown bears a nearly indelible stain of uncertain origin.

"Police discovered the puppet's presence in several tragic cases involving the financial collapse of families in Tingen. It was invariably found in a child's bedroom on the bedside table.

"Nighthawks investigated and initially determined that it brings misfortune, causing those nearby to suffer increasingly dangerous accidents until they die. Within two weeks, one tester approached bankruptcy.

"The puppet exhibits no signs of life and no inclination to escape confinement.

"Extended testing established that remaining more than ten meters away, or spending less than half an hour near it each day, prevents contamination. Once transferred to another person, its misfortune immediately releases the previous victim.

"Appendix: The puppet first appeared in the home of Tess, an elderly toymaker on Lower Iron Cross Street. Advanced age, her husband's serious illness, and the early deaths of both children forced them into poverty.

"It was the final toy she sold. She exchanged it for poison hemlock and ended both her own life and her husband's after they had starved for more than three days."

Recalling the record, Klein felt increasingly horrified and bewildered.

Didn't the file say it showed no signs of life? That it had no desire to escape?

Then what did I just witness?

What dragged it back?

And what did the vertical-eye symbol mean?

The scene resembled a murder victim pounding desperately upon a door for help, only to be dragged back by the killer...

Klein decided against acting alone.

He returned to the duty room and pulled a rope.

The line tightened, gears turned, and an urgent bell rang on the second floor of the Blackthorn Security Company.

Leonard Mitchell and the other Sleepless immediately abandoned their card game and rushed toward the basement.

Chapter 109: Deduction

The sound of running footsteps calmed Klein as he waited at the duty-room entrance.

Leonard arrived first, revolver in hand. "What happened?" he asked gravely.

Watching him struggle to stop, Klein remembered something Rozanne had once mentioned. Three years earlier, shortly after becoming a Sleepless, Leonard had run down these stairs before adapting to the potion's power and tumbled the rest of the way.

Suppressing a cough, Klein pointed toward Chanis Gate. "Something knocked from inside, then began pounding violently. After that, the gate opened slightly."

"Chanis Gate opened?" the short Kenley asked in shock.

"Only a narrow gap." Klein noticed that Leonard, Kenley, and Royale had stopped several paces away. They formed a loose arc around him instead of approaching the duty room.

After a pause, he asked, "Do you suspect me?"

"It isn't suspicion. It's protocol," Kenley replied.

Leonard retained his usual levity despite the tension. "Other Churches have experienced similar incidents. Once, the Beyonder guarding a Chanis Gate lost control, rang the alarm, and killed two teammates who came to help."

Klein's resentment immediately disappeared. "Then how do I prove I haven't lost control?"

Leonard's smile vanished. He tapped his chest four times and recited hoarsely,

"Without clothing or food, they have no refuge from the cold.

"Rain drenches them, and lacking shelter, they huddle among the rocks.

"They are orphans torn from the breast, bereft of hope; they are the poor driven from the righteous path.

"Evernight did not forsake them, but bestowed Her love upon them."

The sacred, sorrowful prayer echoed through the basement, bringing tranquility and purification to body, mind, and soul.

When Klein displayed no abnormal reaction, Leonard stopped and smiled.

"No problem. You're still our trusted companion."

Royale, who had remained silent, looked toward Chanis Gate. "What did you see when it opened?"

"The Misfortune Cloth Puppet--3-0625, the one in the antique black court gown," Klein answered, still shaken. "Three seconds later, an invisible force pulled it back and the gate closed. What happened?"

Leonard, Kenley, and Royale exchanged looks.

"We're as ignorant as you are," Leonard admitted. "Since the gate has closed and nothing else appears abnormal, we shouldn't enter. We'll wait for the captain at dawn."

"I'll remain and guard it with you," Royale said calmly.

"So will I, as the strongest person present." Leonard laughed. "Kenley, return upstairs in case the police bring an emergency and find no one to answer."

Kenley nodded and left without argument.

Leonard glanced at the others. "Perhaps we should continue our card game. Entertainment will help us relax."

Klein returned his revolver to the shoulder holster. "Fine with me."

Royale merely smoothed her black hair and entered the duty room.

While they played Fighting Evil, Klein remarked casually, "According to its file, 3-0625 exhibits no signs of life..."

"Three aces." Leonard laid down his cards. "For forty years, it has shown none. Let us assume the record is correct and reason from there."

"Pass. You have a theory?" Royale asked.

As Klein debated playing his three deuces, Leonard sipped fresh coffee. "If 3-0625 has no life of its own, another factor must have influenced it. That factor must be recent, or this behavior would have appeared long ago.

"Has anything changed behind Chanis Gate during the past month?"

Royale considered for several seconds. "Only one thing. The Antigonus family's notebook and Sealed Artifact 2-049 were stored there for a night."

Leonard tapped the table. "If 2-049 affected the puppet, the same phenomenon would have occurred behind Backlund's Chanis Gate long ago. Therefore, I suspect the notebook."

Klein nodded. "That's the most likely explanation. Leonard, I never expected you to be so capable at deduction."

Romantic poets and sound deductive reasoning ordinarily seemed mutually exclusive.

"He's recently become obsessed with detective novels," Royale explained indifferently. "Two kings, then a straight from eight through king. No takers? Three sixes. I'm out."

Klein and Leonard fell silent.

Distracted by their discussion, they had forgotten something important: Royale was the Evil player this round.

As she shuffled, Klein asked, "Then what pulled 3-0625 back?"

Leonard chuckled. "Do you imagine Chanis Gate is protected only by buried chambers and several elderly keepers?

"Once the sun fully sets, the keepers leave and return to Saint Selena Cathedral. The power behind the gate is strongest at night, when the area becomes unsafe for any living creature. It weakens only after sunrise. That's why the captain ordered us never to enter, whatever we heard."

In other words, the captain forgot to explain why... Klein asked, "Defensive mechanisms such as nexus formations? Enlarged versions of charms and amulets?"

"Yes," Royale answered. "Chanis Gates stand beneath each city's central cathedral for a reason. The sincere prayers offered daily by worshipers contribute fragments of spirituality to the nexus formations. Many tiny contributions become an abundance."

"I see..." Klein nodded while examining his terrible hand.

Leonard added, "There is more than one defense. Saint Selena's ashes are interred behind this gate. She was a High-Sequence Beyonder."

Saint Selena's ashes? The remains of a High-Sequence Beyonder? What power do sacred ashes possess?

Saint Selena had been a devotee during the Church of Evernight's founding in the Third Epoch. Her deeds appeared throughout scripture, making Selena a common name among believers.

As though reading Klein's thoughts, Leonard said, "Rumor claims that the bones or ashes of High-Sequence Beyonders retain incredible power. But those are only rumors."

Klein nodded and returned his attention to the game.

Nothing else happened during the next several hours, but Klein lost exactly two soli. Leonard, fully embodying a romantic poet's temperament at cards, lost four soli and five pence. Royale was the unquestioned victor.

"The sun has risen. It's my turn." The quiet author Seeka Tron entered at six.

Klein recorded the night's incident in the logbook and returned upstairs with Leonard and Royale. He was exhausted, while the Midnight Poet and Sleepless remained energetic.

So this is the difference between Sequences...

He was preparing to leave for home and sleep when Dunn entered.

"Good morning, Captain." Klein could not suppress a yawn.

Dunn removed his hat. "Good morning. Go home and rest. Did anything happen?"

Klein summarized the incident and Leonard's deduction.

"I see." Dunn offered no opinion and continued toward his office. "I'll telegraph the Holy Cathedral."

Klein left 36 Zouteland Street and inhaled the cool morning air. Feeling slightly more alert, he suddenly remembered something.

I forgot to tell them about the paper in the puppet's hands!

How could I forget that?

It was as though some power prevented me from mentioning it to the other Nighthawks...
