The notebook passed through Chanis Gate some time ago. If it affected 3-0625, why did the puppet behave abnormally only last night?

Was it because this was my first watch?

Did it expend all its strength merely to show me that symbol?

What does the Antigonus family's notebook want?

Is this connected to my surviving contact with it--and becoming a Seer?

Suspicion after suspicion rooted Klein to the spot. He could not decide whether to pretend he remembered nothing and go home to sleep, or return upstairs and report everything to the captain.

Chapter 110: Confirmation

After some deliberation, Klein decided to return home and confirm one thing.

If the Misfortune Cloth Puppet had not deliberately shown him the image, the captain and the others would surely find traces during their investigation. Whether he reported it now would make little difference.

If the opposite were true, however, the matter demanded careful consideration.

That was precisely what Klein intended to determine.

He took a trackless public carriage to Daffodil Street. Since it was Sunday, neither Benson nor Melissa was awake when he arrived, and the sitting room remained dark and quiet.

Klein boiled water, added tea leaves, and ate wheat bread with his drink. Then, carrying his coat, hat, and cane, he headed upstairs, instinctively lightening his footsteps.

As he reached the second floor, the bathroom door opened and a sleepy Melissa emerged in an old dress.

"You're home..." She rubbed her eyes.

Klein covered a yawn. "Yes. I need to collapse into bed. Don't wake me before lunch."

Melissa acknowledged him, then remembered something. "Benson and I are going to Saint Selena Cathedral for prayer and Mass. Lunch may be a little late."

As lukewarm believers in the Evernight Goddess, they attended church once every two weeks. Klein, despite being a Nighthawk, had not entered a church since the Secret Order member followed him.

No, I'm at the cathedral every day--just in its basement... Klein justified inwardly.

His greatest concern was that the Goddess might abandon such an insincere believer. If his ritualistic magic failed at a critical moment, he would be in grave trouble.

But considering Old Neil, the Goddess seems remarkably tolerant of Nighthawks. Yes, exactly! Reassured, Klein nodded to Melissa.

"No problem. That means I can sleep longer."

He passed her, entered his bedroom, and locked the door.

After gathering his resolve, he took out the ritual dagger and established a wall of spirituality.

He walked four steps counterclockwise while reciting the incantation, endured the chaotic ravings, and appeared above the gray fog.

In the boundless illusory world, he was the sole living presence seated at the head of the long bronze table.

After nearly a minute of silence, Klein conjured goatskin parchment and wrote a divination statement:

"The image displayed by the Misfortune Cloth Puppet."

He had seen the paper clearly for only an instant. Anxiety had left him with merely a rough recollection, but that posed no problem for a Seer. Through the appropriate method, he could reproduce anything he had once witnessed.

According to mysticism theory, spirituality retained everything a person saw. Given the correct method, any scene could be recovered.

Klein found the Psychology Alchemists' theory described by Daly increasingly convincing. Conscious memory consisted only of islands rising above the sea and could contain very little. A person's spiritual essence preserved most information beneath the surface as the subconscious--the ocean itself.

If spirituality was not the entire ocean, it at least encompassed the waters surrounding each island.

After repeating the statement, Klein leaned back and entered a dream through Cogitation.

Within the blurred, fragmented world, Chanis Gate once again opened with a heavy grinding sound.

The puppet in the antique black court gown pressed into the gap and unfurled its paper.

Numerous mysterious symbols covered the sheet, together forming a vertical eye.

Klein studied it carefully, left the dream, and used the unique power above the gray fog to reproduce the image upon the brown parchment while the memory remained fresh.

The sinister, mysterious vertical eye stared up at him.

After considering it, Klein wrote beneath the image:

"This is the key to the treasure left by the Antigonus family."

He set down the pen and unwound the silver chain from his sleeve. Holding it in his left hand, he suspended the topaz above the statement and image. The pendant remained still.

Klein closed his eyes, cleared his mind, and repeated the sentence seven times.

When he looked again, the topaz was tracing small clockwise circles.

Affirmative.

The vertical-eye symbol truly is a key to the Antigonus family's treasure... Klein nodded thoughtfully.

Tapping the bronze table, he murmured, "With Ray Bieber dead, perhaps no Antigonus descendants remain. Does the notebook regard me--a Seer who survived contact with it--as its heir?

"Did it influence 3-0625, entrust the treasure's key to the puppet, and wait until my watch to reveal it?

"The logic appears sound, yet it isn't convincing.

"How could the notebook know no descendants remain?

"And I have no connection to the family. If the original Klein shared their blood, he wouldn't have committed suicide after reading it.

"Hmm... There seems little harm in telling the captain. But first, let me investigate further."

He divined the treasure's location, but received no details. As Sirius's letter to Mr. Z suggested, he could determine only that it was connected to the Hornacis mountain range's main peak and the ancient Nation of the Evernight.

After completing his divinations, Klein noticed another faint fluctuation in the crimson star from which he had previously heard prayers.

He touched it using the method for answering a prayer and again saw the brown-haired youth in the distinctive close-fitting black outfit.

The young man knelt before the flawless crystal ball, still murmuring.

Having deliberately studied some Jotun, Klein finally understood one sentence.

"I pray... Save... Father and Mother."

So it truly is Jotun. Where is a language thousands of years obsolete still spoken? Unfortunately, the mysterious ruler above the gray fog is powerless. Even if I wanted to save them, I couldn't... Klein sighed and decided to continue observing.

Once I've learned enough Jotun to understand what happened to his parents, I'll see whether I can help.

He withdrew his spirituality, wrapped it around himself, and descended.

Back in his bedroom, Klein dismissed the spiritual barrier, changed into old, comfortable clothes, and went to sleep.

He slept until half past twelve, when Melissa knocked to announce lunch.

After a generous meal, he saw her bring out her new dress and veiled hat.

"Are you going somewhere this afternoon?" he asked.

Benson sat on the sofa frowning at a grammar book. Without looking up, he answered for her. "Mrs. Shaud next door said there's a lecture on household management at the municipal hall. Melissa wants to learn how to handle everyday domestic matters."

"Selena and Elizabeth are coming too," Melissa added.

"Excellent. I hope the lecturer tells you that a family like ours needs at least one maid," Klein joked.

Before Melissa could object, he added, "We should invest our limited time in more valuable pursuits."

Melissa stared at him, pursed her lips, donned her hat, and left.

At two, Klein returned to Blackthorn Security Company.

Rozanne and Dunn, both in the reception hall, asked together, "Didn't you go home to rest?"

Klein smiled. "I was heading to the Divination Club, but I couldn't stop thinking about last night, so I came here first. Has the Holy Cathedral replied?"

Dunn glanced at Rozanne, silently turned, and walked through the partition into his office.

Rozanne made a face at his back. "Honestly, Captain..."

Well done! Suppressing his laughter, Klein followed Dunn and closed the office door.

Sniffing his pipe, Dunn said, "The Holy Cathedral determined that the notebook caused the disturbance. It has been reclassified as a Grade 1 Sealed Artifact. Unfortunately, you no longer possess clearance to read its records."

Grade 1: highly dangerous. Only bishops, Nighthawk captains, and those above them know the details. That means even the captain doesn't understand everything... No wonder. Klein felt both regret and relief.

"The Cathedral also ordered us to determine whether the notebook contaminated anything else behind Chanis Gate," Dunn continued. "Only 3-0625 showed abnormalities, and its sealing method has been changed."

"Did you discover anything further?" Klein asked with feigned curiosity.

Dunn shook his head. "No."

Klein nodded thoughtfully and abandoned the subject. After a little conversation, he left for the Divination Club to continue his journey of digestion.

At the municipal hall, Melissa, Selena, and Elizabeth sat near the door awaiting the lecture.

"If the speaker is terrible, we'll slip out," Selena suggested excitedly.

Elizabeth immediately agreed. "Then we'll go shopping at Harrods."

Chapter 111: Letting It Slip

After a while, a high-cheekboned woman stepped onto the wooden platform. She cleared her throat.

"Good morning, kind and charitable ladies. My name is Xaviera Hedda. Today, I will share my experience in managing household expenses. The lecture has three parts. First, how a family earning one hundred pounds a year should balance food, housing, clothing, and domestic help. Second, where a family earning two hundred pounds should increase its spending to maintain a respectable appearance..."

Melissa listened attentively. She knew her brothers' annual income by heart.

It's already more than two hundred pounds... The thought brought equal measures of relief and fear.

She was content with their present life, yet afraid it might disappear in an instant.

Selena, whose hair was wine red, covered her mouth and whispered, "She appears to worship the Lord of Storms. She's wearing a Storm badge."

Melissa looked and saw a badge depicting violent winds and surging waves pinned to Xaviera's left breast.

"Mrs. Shaud, who told me about the lecture, also follows the Lord of Storms," Melissa explained. "It isn't strange that the speaker does too."

"Agreed. We're here to learn about budgeting," Elizabeth said.

"But apart from Melissa, none of us needs--or has the right--to manage the family finances," Selena complained with a pout.

Elizabeth answered immediately, "We'll eventually marry and establish households of our own."

Since the magic-mirror incident, Selena had been somewhat intimidated by Elizabeth. She nodded awkwardly and pretended to concentrate on the lecture.

Xaviera raised her right hand. "The foundation of every household budget is respect for the opinion of the man of the house. Men provide the income and form the family's pillar. They confront the anxieties, pressures, troubles, and disorder of society to secure everything we enjoy. We must therefore create a peaceful home untouched by the outside world's worries, allowing them to relax, cleanse their spirits, and prepare for the challenges ahead...

"As the celebrated philosopher, sociologist, humanist, and economist Mr. Leumi said, a woman is the angel of the household."

Selena touched her cheek and traced a dimple, whispering excitedly, "Leumi? The man who said human beings are born free?"

Elizabeth hesitated. "Yes, but he worshiped the Lord of Storms."

Xaviera continued, "Mr. Leumi also teaches that women are innately deficient in intelligence and logic. Unable to judge matters independently, they should accept the decisions of father and husband as they accept those of the Church..."[1]

Melissa, Selena, and Elizabeth stared at one another, speechless.

"Shall we leave?" Selena finally suggested.

Melissa and Elizabeth nodded.

"Yes!"

They collected their veiled hats, bent low, and crept toward the side door, hoping to escape unnoticed.

Once outside, they had barely straightened when applause erupted from the small hall.

Melissa instinctively glanced back and saw Mrs. Shaud and many other women clapping.

Whew. Praise the Lady... She exhaled and left the uncomfortable place with her friends.

"Shall we go to Harrods?" Selena suggested beneath a tree, already having forgotten the lecture.

After several seconds of silence, Melissa said, "I plan to go home and study."

"Study..." Selena toyed with her wine-red hair as though ordinary life had abruptly returned.

"I also need to buy bread, beef, potatoes, and fruit. Klein is working today, and Benson went to the municipal library. So yes, I have to go home." Melissa suddenly realized how much she loved her textbooks, gears, and springs.

Selena decided to keep her distance from the unusually strange Melissa. She turned dutifully to Elizabeth and smiled. "Shall we visit Harrods together? I've spent all my savings, but window-shopping is still delightful."

"Certainly." Elizabeth accepted, then casually asked, "Melissa, does your brother Klein work on Sundays?"

"Yes. His day off is Monday, unlike most people." Melissa unconsciously raised her chin a little.

After leaving Blackthorn Security Company, Klein took a public carriage to Howes Street.

He did his utmost to suppress his emotions and avoid thinking about the Antigonus family's treasure. Instead, he concentrated upon acting.

He needed to digest the potion as quickly as possible. Improving himself was vital under any circumstances.

Act like a Seer... Heh, I'm still not professional enough. Fortune-tellers back in the Foodaholic Empire consulted the almanac before doing anything... Klein sat in the carriage with his cane in hand.

He decided to divine whether visiting the club today would be beneficial.

That was behavior befitting a Seer.

After leaving the carriage, Klein took out a halfpenny. His vision narrowed, his pupils darkened, and he silently repeated,

It is suitable to visit the Divination Club today.

It is suitable to visit the Divination Club today.

Ting!

He flicked the coin upward without watching it spin, calmly extending his hand.

The halfpenny landed in his palm with the number facing upward.

That means visiting the club today will bring misfortune... After brief consideration, Klein crossed the street and waited for a public carriage bound for Daffodil Street.

He felt increasingly like a charlatan.

Outside Harrods Department Store on Howes Street, Selena was about to enter when she abruptly stopped and looked aside.

"Is something wrong?" Elizabeth asked.

Selena puffed out her cheeks. "Elizabeth, I was thinking about my mysticism teacher, Mr. Vincent. He died the morning after my birthday...

"Could it have happened because I copied and used his secret incantation? I've felt guilty and uneasy ever since. And I've had rather bad luck lately."

"So?" Elizabeth asked quietly.

Selena bit her lip. "I want someone at the Divination Club to determine whether his death was connected to me."

Judging by what happened at my birthday banquet, Elizabeth is hiding something from me... I remember the back of that man in the tuxedo...

"Can't you perform the divination yourself?" Elizabeth asked in surprise.

Imitating her father, Selena sighed. "Not in my present condition."

"Very well. We'll visit the club first."

They followed the stairs to the second floor.

"Good afternoon, Miss Angelica. It's wonderful to see you again," Selena greeted brightly.

Angelica smiled. "If you visit after lunch, you're almost certain to find me here."

After exchanging pleasantries and lamenting Hanass Vincent's death, Selena said, "I need a divination."

"You know the club's rules. Here is the list of members accepting clients. Since it's the weekend, most are present," Angelica explained automatically.

Selena and Elizabeth put their heads together and examined the names and descriptions.

"I used to ask for my teacher directly. The club has far more members offering divinations than it did last year," Selena remarked excitedly.

Suddenly, she stopped.

"Klein Moretti... Klein Moretti? Isn't that Melissa's brother's name?"

Elizabeth froze, reread the entry, and nodded. "It is..."

"Miss Angelica, is Mr. Klein Moretti here?" Selena asked, eyes sparkling.

Angelica shook her head. "I'm sorry. Mr. Moretti didn't visit today."

"Then we'll choose someone else." Selena did not mind missing him, but laughed. "I know it can't actually be Melissa's brother, yet the name inspired a headline worthy of the *Intis Press*."

Founded by Emperor Roselle, the *Intis Press* was famous throughout the Northern Continent for sensational headlines.

"What headline?" Elizabeth asked distractedly.

Selena cleared her throat. "Is This the Collapse of Morality or a Failure of Society? History Graduate Resorts to Weekend Divination to Earn a Living!"

[1] Adapted from Jean-Jacques Rousseau's *Emile, or On Education*.

Chapter 112: Azik's Explanation

In Backlund's Empress Borough, Audrey Hall sat in a hanging chair in a breezy corner, gazing at flowers blooming beneath the sun while considering Fors Wall's request.

According to Viscount Glaint's investigation, a young woman named Xio Derecha was indeed being held in a temporary prison in North Borough.

She had been charged with grievously assaulting a respectable gentleman during a financial dispute. Her victim remained bedridden and might never walk again.

Fors claimed the supposed gentleman was actually a gang leader from East Borough who made his living as a usurer.

One borrower had discovered that the interest on his debt was several times higher than expected--so excessive that even complete bankruptcy could not satisfy it. When negotiations failed, he sought out the well-known mediator Xio Derecha and asked her to persuade the lender to waive the unreasonable portion.

The man responded poorly to Xio's arbitration and threatened to seize the borrower's wife and children that very night. Xio therefore changed tactics and resorted to force, accidentally injuring him severely.

Glaint had confirmed Fors's account. The gangster had since lost control of his subordinates, and following a mysterious midnight visit, he forgave the borrower's debt and petitioned the prosecutor for leniency toward Xio. Nevertheless, an assault of such severity could not simply be dismissed because the victim withdrew his complaint.

"Glaint wants to resolve the matter legally. His people consulted several familiar attorneys, but they can promise only a reduced sentence. An acquittal would be extremely difficult unless she obtained a medical certificate declaring her mentally incompetent or intellectually disabled..." Audrey murmured, leaning toward her friend's view.

She believed it wisest to avoid any connection with Fors Wall or Xio Derecha. Since joining the Tarot Club, Audrey no longer considered herself an innocent, naive young lady.

"Earl Wolfe is holding a ball tomorrow evening. I'll tell Glaint there to follow the lawyers' advice." Audrey nodded, having reached a decision.

In Loen, lawyers were divided into solicitors and barristers. Solicitors gathered evidence, interviewed the parties, drafted wills, supervised the division of property, and provided legal advice. They could also represent clients in simple cases before magistrates.

Barristers studied the evidence and argued cases in court. The law required them to maintain objectivity, so they could not communicate directly with litigants and relied upon solicitors as intermediaries. They were genuine legal experts, gifted speakers, and skilled debaters.

Relaxed, Audrey watched the colorful flowers from the shadows until a thought occurred to her.

A certificate of mental incompetence... A psychiatrist...

If the Psychology Alchemists understand acting, might some of them work as psychiatrists?

Certain she had found a promising direction, Audrey's eyes sparkled like gemstones.

Then she noticed her golden retriever, Susie, slipping behind the flower beds into an area normally reached only by the gardener.

Susie... What is she doing? Audrey watched blankly from concealment.

The surrounding fragrances seemed to confuse the dog's keen nose, preventing her from detecting her owner. Susie opened her mouth and made noises like someone exercising her voice.

The air vibrated, producing stiff, halting words.

"Hello.

"How are you?"

Audrey's mouth fell open, all elegant etiquette forgotten. She could scarcely believe what she had witnessed.

She sprang upright. "Susie, you can speak? When did you learn?"

The golden retriever jumped in fright and turned. Her tail wagged rapidly with nervousness as she opened and closed her mouth several times.

"I... don't know how to explain. I am a dog, after all."

Audrey found herself completely speechless.

On Monday morning, Klein reviewed and consolidated his mysticism knowledge as planned, then took a public carriage to Khoy University.

He intended to spend more time with Mr. Azik and discover what the man knew.

In the history department's gray three-story building, Klein spoke with his mentor, Cohen Quentin, and exchanged information concerning the ruins on the Hornacis main peak.

Having learned nothing new, he waited until his mentor left on an errand, then entered the office across the corridor and approached Azik's desk.

"Mr. Azik, may I speak with you?" Klein removed his hat and bowed to the bronze-skinned lecturer with gentle features and a small mole beneath his right ear.

Azik's eyes seemed to contain the passage of countless years. He tidied his books and replied, "Certainly. Let us walk beside the Khoy."

"All right." Cane in hand, Klein followed him outside.

They walked in silence. When the river came into view and no teachers or students were nearby, Azik stopped, half turned, and asked, "How can I help you?"

Klein considered several tactful approaches, then discarded them all.

"Mr. Azik, you are trustworthy and honorable. I want to know what you see in me--or what you know. I mean your claim that something in my fate was disharmonious."

Azik lowered his cane and sighed with a laugh.

"I didn't expect such directness. I'm almost at a loss for an answer.

"In truth, that disharmony is all I perceived. Beyond it, I know no more than you do."

Klein hesitated. "Then how did you perceive it? I don't believe divination gave you the answer."

Azik turned toward the Khoy River, bleakness entering his voice.

"You misunderstand. Divination can reach such heights; everything depends upon the diviner. But yes, my talk of divination was merely an excuse.

"Some people are... unusual. They are born with strange abilities. I believe I may be one of them."

"You believe?" Klein immediately caught the uncertainty.

"Yes. I don't know whether I was born this way. Perhaps the price of my ability was forgetting myself--my past and my parents." Melancholy clouded Azik's eyes.

Increasingly confused, Klein repeated, "You forgot your past?"

Azik smiled without humor.

"Before entering Backlund University's history department, I lost most of my memories. I retained only my name and basic knowledge. Fortunately, I still possessed identification documents, or I might have become homeless. For years, I searched for my parents through those documents, but found nothing--despite my ability to glimpse a corner of Fate.

"During university, I gradually discovered that I possessed strange powers beyond common sense."

"Do you know why you lost your memories?" Klein asked attentively.

He suspected Azik might be an amnesiac member of the Life School of Thought, perhaps even a Mid-Sequence Beyonder of some standing. That secret organization possessed the Monster and Soothsayer pathways and generally transmitted its knowledge from master to disciple.

Azik shook his head firmly. "No. It was as though I simply awakened one day having forgotten everything."

He resumed walking.

"After leaving Backlund, I began having dreams--many strange dreams..."

Dreams? Interpreting dreams is my specialty. "What kind?" Klein immediately asked.

Azik gave a muffled laugh. "Many kinds. Sometimes I dream of the interior of a dark mausoleum, filled with ancient coffins. The corpses inside have white feathers growing from their backs. Sometimes I wear armor and carry a ten-foot spear while charging an enemy.

"Sometimes I am a feudal lord with fertile lands, a beautiful wife, and three children. Sometimes I am a vagrant walking a muddy road in the rain, cold and hungry.

"Sometimes I have a daughter--not one of those earlier children. She has long, smooth black hair and loves sitting on a swing I built. She always begs me for sweets. At other times, I stand beside a gallows and coldly watch a corpse hanging there."

Listening to Azik's seemingly mad account, Klein realized he could not interpret the dreams. Their symbols were mutually contradictory.

Azik's gaze returned to the present, and his voice lost its distant quality.

"Feynapotter worships Earth Mother. Her Church teaches that every life is a plant drawing nourishment from the soil. It grows, flourishes, and withers. When it withers, it falls back to the earth and returns to the Mother's embrace. The next year it grows again, blooming and withering year after year. So it is with life--one existence following another.

"Sometimes I want to believe that teaching. Perhaps my unusual nature allows me to dream of previous lives, and of lives before those."

He looked at Klein and sighed. "I've never told Cohen any of this. I'm telling you because I..."

Azik paused, then smiled.

"Forgive me. My earlier description was incomplete. The disharmony in your fate isn't the only thing I can perceive. I see something else as well.

"Klein, you are no longer an ordinary person. You possess an extraordinary, unnatural power--one much like my own."

Chapter 113: A Request

Mr. Azik can tell I'm a Beyonder? His ability truly is remarkable... Klein froze briefly, then answered honestly.

"Yes."

After a moment's thought, he added, "It happened because of the incident involving Welch and Naya."

"As I suspected..." Azik sighed. "Two of the police officers who questioned Cohen and me possessed extraordinary powers."

Probably the captain and Leonard. They handled Welch's case... Klein nodded without interrupting.

Azik raised his cane. "You must have entered their circle. I hope you'll help me search for clues about my origins. You needn't go out of your way; simply remember me if you encounter anything relevant."

He smiled bitterly.

"I know no one else with extraordinary powers. You cannot imagine what it means to be a man without a past. You're like a boat adrift upon a boundless ocean. The worst thing isn't confronting a storm, but having no harbor and no way to navigate toward shore. You endure one disaster after another without end, never knowing peace or safety."

No, Mr. Azik. I understand because I'm in a similar position. Fortunately, I possess fragments of the original Klein's memories--and I have Benson and Melissa... Klein silently reflected before asking, "If you possess such an extraordinary ability, why didn't you join a similar organization and search for clues yourself?"

Azik met his gaze and smiled self-deprecatingly.

"Because I'm afraid. Afraid of dying.

"I've grown accustomed to this life, and I like it. I lack the courage to take such a risk, so I can only rely upon you."

Klein asked no more questions. "I'll pay close attention if I encounter any clues."

"Good. We should return to the office. When Cohen finishes his work, the three of us can have lunch. Do you remember the East Balam Restaurant at the university? It's quite good. Heh, my treat." Azik pointed with his cane.

My apologies, but I have no memory of it. How could the studious original Klein have afforded the East Balam Restaurant? Even if Welch offered to pay, he would have refused somewhere so expensive... Klein adjusted his hat and followed Azik back toward the history department's gray stone building.

After several steps, Azik spoke again.

"Once I finish my university work, summer vacation begins. You may visit my home or write to me."

Klein nodded. "I thought you might vacation at Desi Bay."

"The south is too hot at this time of year, and I dislike sunbathing. Look at my complexion; I tan too easily. I'd rather travel to Winter County or northern Feysac to ski, see the landscape, or hunt seals." The bronze-skinned Azik smiled.

I would too... Having only recently joined the Nighthawks, Klein could not hide his envy.

After lunch, he returned home and napped before reviewing charms and amulets. He hoped to master them soon enough to create items useful in combat.

Shortly before three, Klein put away his materials and sealed the room with a wall of spirituality.

Above the gray fog, a long, mottled ancient table stood within the majestic divine hall.

Klein sat in the place of honor, his face concealed by dense mist. Justice and The Hanged Man appeared in their customary seats, equally indistinct.

Hmm. Miss Justice's emotions are unstable--worry, unease, and confusion... Through Spirit Vision, Klein observed the Tarot Club's sole female member.

Audrey Hall could scarcely describe her feelings. Susie's sudden speech had shocked her profoundly.

She had imagined becoming a celebrated detective or psychologist with Susie as her assistant. But if it became the dog detective Susie accompanied by her assistant, Miss Audrey, that would be somewhat...

No, not somewhat. It would be utterly bizarre!

Audrey straightened, intending to seek help from Mr. Fool and The Hanged Man, but swallowed the words.

How should I ask? What should someone do when her pet becomes abnormal?

How do you interact with a pet that can speak and possesses considerable intelligence?

No, no. This is the Tarot Club, not a gathering for exchanging pet-care advice. Whatever good impression Mr. Fool and The Hanged Man have of me would shatter!

Her thoughts raced until she finally organized a question.

"Honorable Mr. Fool, and Mr. Hanged Man, who has helped me so often, I have a question. What can a pet with Beyonder powers do for its owner? In other words, how useful might it be?"

The instant she finished, Mr. Fool and The Hanged Man fell silent. The atmosphere turned peculiar.

Say something! Don't look at me that way. I did nothing! Truly, I'm asking for a friend! Audrey wanted to disappear into a hole from embarrassment and immediately regretted the question.

She previously asked what would happen if an ordinary animal drank a Sequence potion. Did she actually share her potion with a pet? That sounds exactly like something Miss Justice would do... I feel rather pathetic presiding over a "heretical cult" with her as a member... Klein rested his right hand against his forehead and pinched it twice without answering.

After nearly twenty seconds, Alger Wilson replied in an odd tone. "That depends upon the pet's abilities. If it were a Spectator, for example, it could observe or eavesdrop in certain situations. Humans are wary of one another, but no one imagines that the pet sitting at their feet might be listening."

That makes sense! Father excludes me when discussing important matters with nobles, cabinet members, and ministers. They often lock the door. But if Susie hid until they closed it, no one would remove her... And many ladies converse only within private social circles... Audrey's eyes brightened as possibilities flooded her mind.

Now that Susie can speak, she can repeat everything directly... Susie, you're wonderful! I must treat you well and teach you correct pronunciation and vocabulary...

Should I teach her an aristocratic accent or ordinary Backlund speech? Would other dogs recognize where she came from by her accent? Wait, why am I considering that? Susie won't speak human language to other dogs...

But why did Mr. Hanged Man choose Spectator as his example?

C-could he have guessed?

Audrey's expression shifted. She quickly recovered her posture and smiled.

"Mr. Fool, I found another page from Emperor Roselle's diary."

Fors Wall gave it to me.

"Excellent. Your debt is now repaid," Klein answered in a good mood.

"I'm sorry, but this page contains very little." Audrey began reproducing the remembered text upon goatskin parchment.

Klein raised a hand, and the page appeared in his palm. "That doesn't alter my promise. Besides, the material you gave me previously occupied two pages."

The pages Justice and The Hanged Man collected were copies made by researchers, not originals. Some transcribers compressed the contents for convenience, while others preserved the diary's original layout.

Klein lowered his gaze to the few lines.

"December 20th. The new year approaches, but the reports I've received leave me confused and deeply troubled.

"There is no crude oil in this world!

"There is no crude oil anywhere!"

Chapter 114: Standards for Membership

No crude oil? Had it simply not been discovered, or did none exist?

Nearly a hundred and fifty years had passed since Emperor Roselle's assassination, yet there was still no trace of petroleum.

Klein's pupils contracted, and the diary page trembled in his hand.

Without crude oil, the future of the internal-combustion engine was uncertain, and the chemical industry might stagnate. Earth's modern industrial age might never emerge in this world.

In short, Klein could no longer predict the direction of its development.

Though incapable of inventing advanced technology himself, he had assumed his broad knowledge and familiarity with history's course gave him an advantage. Once he accumulated enough capital, he could invest across several promising industries rather than put all his eggs in one basket.

He had imagined enormous wealth as only a matter of time. He would employ respectable intermediaries--"white gloves"--to establish international charitable foundations. Publicly, they would aid the poor. Privately, they would fund movements against society's upper strata and improve conditions for the lower classes.

If he found a way home, he would divide his estate into thirds: one for Benson, one for Melissa, and one for the foundation.

Unfortunately, half of that perfect future had just shattered.

At least electricity and magnetism exist here. The telegraph proves their potential. I should concentrate future investments there... Klein composed himself and continued reading.

"December 21st. I will think no more about crude oil. Advancing my Sequence is what truly matters!

"December 22nd. The filth in Richeux Borough is intolerable. Had I not gone there incognito, I would never have known it remained unchanged from my youth. I shall summon the ministers and formulate a Capital Sewer and Public Lavatory Improvement Plan. We must also correct the people's habits: boil drinking water, wash hands and faces regularly, stop littering and relieving themselves wherever they please, and use condoms whenever possible... Haha! I know what to call the initiative: the Patriotic Health Campaign!

"Thus, the invention of condoms must be accelerated, along with masks, paper cups, and similar items. Even primitive versions will suffice. We must experiment. Fortunately, this world still possesses rubber trees.

"December 23rd. Perhaps I should consider that proposal and prepare a path of retreat outside the Church of the God of Craftsmanship. For example, I could join that ancient and mysterious organization said to influence the world from the shadows."

The page ended there. Klein's feelings were impossible to describe.

Emperor Roselle, what was that ancient organization manipulating the world from the shadows? Is it one I know?

How could you stop there? Why didn't you write more?

It's exactly like reaching the end of a novel only to discover the author abandoned it...

And a Patriotic Health Campaign? The Emperor certainly knew how to amuse himself.

The entries must date from after Roselle became Consul of Intis, perhaps even after he proclaimed himself Emperor Caesar.

When I return, I should consult foreign histories and determine when the Capital Sewer and Public Lavatory Improvement Plan was enacted.

After nearly twenty seconds, Klein reined in his thoughts and let the diary page vanish.

"You may begin your discussion."

Audrey exhaled in relief, entered her Spectator state, and smiled faintly. "I'd like to know whether there is a potion called Arbiter, and whether any Beyonder can pass through wooden doors or render locks useless."

I know this... Klein was about to answer when The Hanged Man spoke first.

"In exchange, I need you to investigate something."

"What?" Audrey asked with interest and confusion.

Alger glanced toward The Fool. "I want to know whether the king intends to retaliate against the Feysac Empire and begin another war on Balam's eastern coast before June of next year."

The Tarot Club conversed in Loenese, and their accents had been evident since the first Gathering. Alger knew Justice was a Loen noblewoman, just as she presumably knew he came from Loen. He believed The Fool's use of the language was merely a disguise adopted for their convenience.

Since witnessing the ritual, Alger had begun thinking of The Fool with the pronoun reserved for a deity.

Audrey reviewed everything she had heard at social gatherings. "I can do that, but I'll need time to confirm it."

"I can wait. With Mr. Fool as our witness, I trust you'll honor the agreement," Alger replied.

Audrey looked toward the silent, mysterious figure shrouded in gray fog. The corner of her mouth curved upward.

"But that information is worth more than answers to my two questions."

"Once you confirm it, I'll provide additional compensation appropriate to its value," Alger answered, having anticipated the objection.

Miss Justice, Mr. Hanged Man, do you need a virtual currency to establish prices? Klein smiled and leaned back.

Audrey relaxed and cheered inwardly.

Well done, Audrey! You negotiated! Excitement nearly broke her Spectator state. Remembering something, she asked, "Mr. Hanged Man, did you receive the thousand pounds?"

"I'm afraid I'm still at sea and haven't returned to land." Alger had no wish to linger on the topic. "As for your question, a Beyonder capable of passing through wooden doors and defeating locks is probably a Sequence 9 Apprentice. The Theosophy Order possesses the formula, though it may also be found through other channels, such as a Fourth Epoch tomb."

The Theosophy Order--the secret organization with countless connections to the Demoness Sect... Klein leisurely rubbed his chin.

Seeing no contradiction from The Fool, Audrey sighed. "Had I found the Apprentice formula earlier, I might not have chosen Spectator."

The ability was simply marvelous.

Alger ignored the remark. "There is also a potion called Arbiter. You should be familiar with its pathway, because it belongs to Loen's Augustus family and Feynapotter's Castiya family. In ancient times, Low-Sequence formulas were sometimes granted as rewards, so certain noble houses may also possess them."

The Augustus dynasty ruled Loen; the Castiya family ruled Feynapotter.

So the Augustus family are Arbiters... Audrey suddenly understood.

No wonder I always follow their arrangements, feel uncomfortable, and concede as though I'm no longer myself in their presence. I thought I was merely timid...

"An Arbiter possesses persuasive charisma, considerable authority, and excellent combat ability for handling unexpected situations," Alger summarized.

Audrey nodded and leaned back elegantly. "I have no further questions."

After some thought, Alger looked toward the head of the bronze table.

"Honorable Mr. Fool, is the True Creator's sacred residence proclaimed by the Aurora Order the legendary Forsaken Land of the Gods?"

The Forsaken Land of the Gods? I've seen the term only once in Roselle's diary. It may appear in classified Nighthawk files, but I lack access... How am I supposed to answer? The corner of Klein's mouth nearly twitched.

After brief consideration, he replied calmly, "That is not something you should know yet."

Alger's heart tightened. He immediately bowed his head. "Forgive my transgression."

Audrey abandoned her own desire to ask about the place.

Silence settled over the lofty divine hall.

Feeling obliged to say something, Audrey asked, "Mr. Fool, if--and I mean only if--I had an opportunity to join another organization, such as the Psychology Alchemists, would that be permitted?"

Still leaning back, Klein chuckled. "Certainly. I require only that the Tarot Club's existence remain secret.

"Membership in another organization would increase the information and materials you could offer in trade."

He suddenly remembered that he too belonged to another organization: he was a genuine Nighthawk, while The Hanged Man was almost certainly connected to the Church of Storms.

Is the Tarot Club actually an alliance of rebels? A gathering of traitors? Klein sank into thought.

"I understand." Audrey was delighted, but immediately thought of another question. "Mr. Fool, if I discover a suitable gentleman or lady, may I guide that person into the Gathering? How should I proceed?"

Alger followed with a question of his own. "Mr. Fool, what qualifications must a prospective member possess? How will we evaluate them?"

Ambitious, ethical, educated, and disciplined... Four words instantly appeared in Klein's mind.

He remained silent until Justice and The Hanged Man began to grow uneasy.

"Inform me here of anyone you consider suitable. I will decide whether to admit them. Before then, you must offer no hint that might expose the Tarot Club's existence. Remember, when speaking to anyone outside the Gathering..."

Klein paused, then continued in a weighty voice.

"You must not speak my name without my permission."

Chapter 115: The Swindler

"You must not speak my name without my permission."

Several minutes after the Gathering ended, the words still echoed in the ears of Audrey and Alger, now returned to her bedroom and his ship.

The mysterious and powerful Mr. Fool usually seemed relaxed, composed, and unfathomable. Rarely did He adopt such a stern and imperious manner.

Consequently, both were deeply alarmed and sincerely submitted to His demand.

They were familiar with such prohibitions, but normally encountered them only in *The Revelation of Evernight* or *The Book of Storms*.

On Daffodil Street in Tingen's West Borough, Klein opened his curtains and let golden sunlight flood the bedroom.

After Justice and The Hanged Man departed, he had examined the crimson star that previously carried a prayer, but received nothing new.

Since the star appeared able to preserve prayers like messages sent to an absent recipient, Klein concluded that the Jotun-speaking youth had not prayed again since Klein's previous visit above the gray fog.

He feared there was no hope left for the young man's parents and that the youth had surrendered...

With his back to the sunlight, Klein walked to his bed and lay down, unwilling to move.

He knew he should visit the Divination Club and continue digesting his potion. Instead, he remained motionless and savored a rare interval of rest.

From Tuesday through Friday, his mornings were filled with mysticism lessons and practical exercises, his afternoons with shooting and combat training. By evening, he was mentally exhausted. Saturday morning was no different, and that afternoon he began his watch at Chanis Gate, remaining underground until Sunday dawn.

Sunday morning was reserved for sleep, while circumstances determined whether he visited the club that afternoon. On Monday morning, he had gone to Khoy University; in the afternoon came the Tarot Gathering and further thought about acting as a Seer. He had been occupied for an entire week without genuine rest.

All Klein wanted was to lie uselessly in bed and daydream.

No. How can the leader of a cult be this worthless? If Miss Justice and Mr. Hanged Man learned of it, my image would collapse... He buried his face in the blanket and tried to motivate himself.

"I possess the Clown formula. All I need is to digest the Seer potion completely... I possess the Clown formula. All I need is to digest the Seer potion completely..."

Repeating the words, he finally pushed himself upright.

Klein took out a bronze coin and divined whether visiting the club was advisable. The answer was affirmative.

"Five, four, three, two, one!"

At the end of the countdown, he forced himself to stand, crossed to the clothes rack, and selected his suit and hat.

In a shaded corner of the Divination Club's meeting room on Howes Street, Klein sipped Sibe black tea and read the *Tingen City Honest Paper*. Only six or seven members were nearby.

As he laughed at a grammatical error in an employment advertisement, Glacis entered, monocle in place and silk top hat in hand. A woman in her thirties wearing a blue dress accompanied him.

She had arched eyebrows and large but dull eyes. In her left hand, she carried an Intis hat decorated with black-swan feathers.

That hat is absurd. Wouldn't wearing it make her neck ache? Klein wondered. He glanced toward them and rubbed his glabella as though easing fatigue.

Spirit Vision showed both to be physically healthy, but anxious, angry, and flustered.

"Good afternoon, Glacis. Mr. Lanevus proved untrustworthy, didn't he?" Klein asked with a smile, remaining seated.

Glacis had previously asked him to divine an investment in Lanevus's steel company and received a negative answer. Yet his indecision had suggested he might gamble on it anyway. Klein hoped he had not invested everything he possessed.

Glacis froze, then smiled bitterly. "I deeply regret ignoring your divination. Heh, this is the second time I've said something like that. Let us hope--no, I am certain--there won't be a third."

He turned to the woman. "Madam Christina, you see? Mr. Moretti understood why we came before either of us spoke. He's the most extraordinary fortune-teller I've ever met. I would gladly call him a true seer."

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti. We are indeed here because of Lanevus." Christina bowed simply, unable to conceal her anxiety.

"Shall we use the Topaz Room?" Glacis asked.

Klein rose with a laugh. "This is a fortune-teller's work."

Inside the empty room, Glacis locked the door and sighed as he sat.

"Lanevus has disappeared. Claiming he needed to supervise excavation in Sivellaus County, he left Tingen and never returned. We sent someone by steam locomotive and discovered that his enormous iron mine existed only on a map. Fortunately, I remembered your warning and invested only a third of my original plan. Otherwise, I would have destroyed my family and perhaps myself."

Klein's pupils darkened as he studied them. "Before making such a major investment, didn't you send a representative to confirm his claims in the Hornacis range?"

"Our representative was deceived," Christina answered quickly. "Lanevus hired people, rented premises, and fenced off land to create the appearance of a mine."

Maintaining the bearing of a Seer, Klein asked no further questions. "What do you wish to divine?"

"Whether anything can still be recovered," Christina said, glancing at Glacis.

Klein took out paper and a fountain pen. "Then we'll use astrological divination. I'll ask questions, and you will answer."

As they spoke, he marked the Thunderous constellation and the symbols corresponding to their circumstances, gradually completing the chart. He incorporated more elements than an ordinary fortune-teller, allowing his interpretation to approach the truth more closely.

"Madam, sir, you stand at a crossroads. If greed and anxiety overcome you, you will descend farther into the abyss and never escape. If you remain patient, restrain yourselves, and wait without greed, an opportunity to see sunlight will come," Klein explained unhurriedly.

"I understand." Christina nodded, then asked, "Mr. Moretti, can you divine Lanevus's whereabouts?"

"Probably not. The information he left is almost certainly false; even Lanevus may not be his real name. Without accurate details, how could I locate him? I would need either highly specific information or an object he carried constantly," Klein answered truthfully.

After a moment's silence, Christina pushed a one-soli note toward him.

"Glacis told me you are a genuine seer who reveres fate and isn't greedy for money. Consider the excess a donation to the club."

"Thank you for your confidence."

Christina rose, bade him farewell, and hurried away.

Not greedy for money? No, I'm extremely materialistic! Klein regretted having acted so convincingly like a charlatan.

After she left, Glacis closed the door again. "Is there truly no way?"

"I just told you what would be required." Klein leaned back with a smile.

Glacis sighed. "Lanevus escaped with more than ten thousand pounds taken from over a hundred victims. I was fortunate to lose only fifty--my savings, but no borrowed money. Miss Christina invested a hundred and fifty pounds, an amount she cannot easily bear."

"Have you contacted the police?" Hearing the total, Klein felt genuine anger toward the swindler.

Ten thousand pounds would qualify someone as wealthy even in Backlund.

Would the police involve the Nighthawks, Mandated Punishers, or Machinery Hivemind in an ordinary fraud case? Klein wondered distractedly.

"We filed a report, and the police are treating it seriously," Glacis replied. "The victims agreed to offer part of any recovered money as a reward. Information leading to Lanevus is worth ten pounds. His precise location and assistance in capturing him are worth one hundred."

Ten pounds for a clue? One hundred for his capture? Klein's eyes nearly shone, and his breathing grew heavier.

He had been worrying about paying Detective Henry.

His extra three-pound salary this week would barely cover the second installment. If the detective completed both commissions the following week, Klein would be several soli short even if he spent nothing else from his savings.

Perhaps the police possess something Lanevus used regularly. But it won't help if he has already left Tingen... Excitement and disappointment mingled within him.

During the next hour and a half, Angelica referred two more clients. One requested a reading for a one-year-old child. Klein created the appropriate natal chart and gave an explanation that thoroughly satisfied the customer.

The other sought a missing object. Combining tarot with dream divination, Klein identified a general location. The client was astonished, having never met a fortune-teller capable of giving such precise information.

Perhaps I could earn enough merely by performing divinations... After receiving several tips, Klein donned his hat, took up his cane, and headed toward the exit.

At that moment, Christina returned with a young woman in a sunhat.

She immediately approached him. "Mr. Moretti, you said you might divine Lanevus's whereabouts if we possessed something belonging to him?"

"Correct." Klein nodded.

Christina sighed with relief and asked gravely, "Then would his child count as something belonging to him?"

Huh? Klein stared blankly for a moment.

Chapter 116: Lanevus's Child

Christina failed to notice the Seer's blank expression. Glancing toward Angelica at the reception desk, she lowered her voice.

"I mean Lanevus's child."

She indicated the young woman in the sunhat. "This is my niece, Megose. Her mother is my elder sister. I deeply regret believing Lanevus was an excellent young man and introducing him to Megose when he was single. They became lovers.

"At first, Megose's parents were delighted with him as well. They planned to invest all their savings in his steel company after the engagement. Fortunately, Lanevus fled before that happened, so they suffered no ruinous financial loss. Unfortunately, my sister and brother-in-law must explain to their relatives and friends why the engagement has been canceled--and decide what to do about Megose's child.

"We worship the God of Steam and Machinery, not the Lord of Storms. We don't believe chastity before marriage is mandatory. We don't blame Megose; we pity her. Nevertheless, the child's existence creates difficulties, particularly with such a father."

He exploited people financially and sexually... Klein looked toward the silent Megose and realized she was quite beautiful.

She had a broad, smooth forehead, long blond hair, and large eyes resembling Christina's. Her lips were pressed tightly together, and she appeared both depressed and composed.

What an infuriating swindler--and he escaped successfully... After silently cursing Lanevus, Klein considered the question.

"If the child had already been born, I could use the connection between them to divine Lanevus's whereabouts. Unfortunately, that requires waiting several months. Perhaps this is what the earlier result meant: remain patient and persistent, resist greed, and eventually you will see sunlight."

"Several months..." Christina shook her head. "After so long, even finding Lanevus might not recover our money."

She looked at Megose and unconsciously lowered her voice. "Do you possess anything Lanevus carried regularly?"

"No," Megose answered softly and clearly. "Would the ring he gave me suffice?"

"It must be something he carried for a long time." Klein shook his head.

Christina was silent before turning fully toward her niece.

"You must make a decision. Keeping this child will make your future difficult. Will you tell him that his father was a swindler who stole from countless people, including his mother?

"It's time to visit a clinic or hospital. And doing so might help us find Lanevus and recover what we lost."

Isn't this proposed divination rather brutal? It was not Klein's place to interfere in another family's affairs. He waited patiently while commenting inwardly.

Megose lowered her head and remained silent for a long time.

Then she touched her stomach and smiled gently.

"He is different from his father. He will be a thoughtful, lovable child.

"Every day, he kicks me softly to tell me how he feels. He even hums songs, whistles, and uses music to help me sleep..."

Something suddenly felt wrong to Klein.

The beginning sounded normal enough, but the rest resembled a madwoman's ravings.

Did the incident damage her mind? Pretending to massage away fatigue, Klein raised his right hand toward his glabella.

Before he could activate Spirit Vision, Megose abruptly turned and walked toward the exit, leaving only one sentence behind.

"Perhaps after he's born, his father will secretly return and leave some of the money for his child..."

The unexpected reaction left Klein momentarily stunned. By the time he recovered, Megose had left the club and descended the stairs.

Christina sighed. "I'm sorry, Mr. Moretti. Forgive us for troubling you. We'll search for an object Lanevus carried for a long time."

Klein nodded slightly, watched her leave, then shook his head with a sigh.

The next morning, he entered Blackthorn Security Company, greeted Rozanne, and asked, "Where are today's newspapers?"

The sweet brown-haired woman studied him suspiciously. "Klein, you're acting strangely."

"Why?" he asked with a smile.

Rozanne rolled her eyes. "You usually read during lunch because your mornings belong to mysticism lessons. Old Neil is already waiting in the armory!"

"I heard about a case offering a reward. I want to memorize the criminal's appearance. Perhaps I'll encounter him one day," Klein explained.

"Really?" Curious, Rozanne began searching through the newspapers. "The wanted notice... Lanevus, correct?"

"Yes."

After reading for nearly twenty seconds, Rozanne suddenly cursed, "Wicked swindler! He stole almost ten thousand pounds!"

Klein shared her anger. "It's outrageous. Even I want to apply for the case."

Rozanne continued reading, then shook her head regretfully. "There appears to be no supernatural involvement. Even if there were, the case would belong to the Lord of Storms' Mandated Punishers."

Klein took the paper, understood her meaning, and sighed. "Yes. He deceived so many people that followers of all three Churches must be involved, and his supposed steel company was in the south."

When a supernatural case involved only followers of one deity, it went to that Church's team. If believers of all three major faiths were involved, jurisdiction determined responsibility. The Nighthawks controlled Golden Indus, North, and West Boroughs; the Mandated Punishers controlled East, South, and the harbor; the Machinery Hivemind covered the universities and suburbs.

Klein studied and memorized Lanevus's appearance.

The man had a broad forehead, black hair, brown eyes, and nearly round spectacles. His faint smirk seemed to mock everyone.

Apart from the spectacles, he possessed no notable features and looked thoroughly ordinary.

After chatting briefly with Rozanne, Klein passed through the partition and prepared to go underground.

He saw the pale, aloof Corpse Collector Frye and the white-haired, black-eyed author Seeka Tron leaving the recreation room.

Following a brief greeting, Klein watched them depart and noticed Dunn, wearing a black trench coat, beside the open doorway.

"A case?" Klein asked curiously.

Two Nighthawks would not normally leave together at that hour without reason.

Dunn nodded. "A possible supernatural incident in West Borough. I sent Seeka and Frye to investigate. You needn't concern yourself. Until you master combat, I don't intend to assign you field missions. I'm responsible for my team members."

Captain, you're a wonderful man. Apart from your receding hairline and poor memory, you're flawless... Klein asked for confirmation, "So I only need to attend mysticism lessons and combat training? I contribute nothing and still receive my salary?"

"Only temporarily," Dunn confirmed.

I get paid generously merely to attend classes and exercise. What a wonderful thought... Klein rejoiced.

Please, no more coincidences! he prayed silently.

The following days passed peacefully. On Friday, after combat training, Klein took a carriage to Besik Street.

Outside Henry's Private Detective Agency, he looked both ways. Once certain no one was watching, he donned the gauze mask, raised his trench-coat collar, and hurried upstairs.

Detective Henry, the burly middle-aged man, answered his knock.

"Good afternoon, sir. We've completed one of your commissions." Henry's drinking and smoking had left his voice hoarse.

Klein deliberately lowered his own voice. "You identified the man from the Evil Dragon Bar?"

The suspected Psychology Alchemist who purchased the Spectator potion's supplementary ingredients...

"Yes." Henry waved his pipe, then fell silent and smiled expectantly.

Understanding, Klein produced four one-pound notes. "The second payment."

He paused. "And write me a receipt."

His private savings had fallen below one pound.

"Of course." Henry coughed, checked the notes' anticounterfeiting marks, and asked an employee for pen and paper.

After inviting Klein to sit, he swiftly wrote and stamped the receipt.

Then he drew upon his pipe. "My assistant and I waited at the Evil Dragon Bar for three days before the man appeared.

"He's exceptionally alert and observant. Fortunately, we're experienced.

"His name is Daxter Guderian. He's a physician at Greenhill Mental Asylum."

Chapter 117: Contact

Daxter Guderian, a physician at Greenhill Mental Asylum...

Klein repeated the detective's words inwardly and considered how to approach the man he suspected of being a Spectator affiliated with the Psychology Alchemists.

He did not intend to take unnecessary risks. He could not allow the Nighthawks to discover anything suspicious about him, much less lose his present life merely to exchange information and resources.

Moreover, Daxter was probably a Spectator. Anyone without specialized training would find it difficult to conceal motives and thoughts from him.

Should I use an intermediary to appear more mysterious? No. The more people involved, the greater the chance of failure... Perhaps I can conceal truth within truth. I'll allow the doctor to read my genuine feelings and intentions from my expression and posture--but not the complete truth.

As Detective Henry continued describing Daxter, Klein searched for a method that would minimize danger without compromising his objective.

Inspiration gradually came from a detective film he had once watched.

Yes, that might work, though I'll need to practice it repeatedly... Klein nodded inwardly and returned his full attention to Henry.

The detective cleared his throat. "We're still investigating the red chimney. Many buildings in Tingen possess similar features. More clues would make the search considerably easier."

Klein laughed dryly. "If I had more clues, I wouldn't have needed to hire you."

The investigation's length had already discouraged him. The hidden mastermind had plainly detected Klein's divination and had ample time to move elsewhere.

His only hope was that information about the chimney's owners and tenants might produce another clue.

And that alone costs seven pounds... The thought made his heart ache. After Henry completed his report, Klein collected his cane and left.

At twenty minutes before nine on Saturday morning, Daxter Guderian entered his office at Greenhill Mental Asylum.

The gold-rimmed-spectacled physician removed his jacket and hat and hung them on the rack. He had just picked up his tin of coffee when someone knocked.

"Come in," Daxter called casually.

The half-open door swung wider, admitting a young man in a black trench coat.

Daxter did not recognize him. "Good morning. You are?"

Klein closed the door, removed his hat, pressed it to his chest, and bowed.

"Good morning, Doctor Daxter. Forgive my unannounced visit. I am Probationary Inspector Klein Moretti of the Awwa County Police. Here are my identification and badge."

"Inspector?" Daxter murmured as he accepted them.

"Special Operations Department..." He slowly looked up, his gaze calm and appraising.

Short black hair, dark-brown eyes, a scholarly air, and no present hostility...

Daxter returned the documents and indicated a chair across the desk. "Please sit, officer. How may I assist you?"

Klein sat, placed his cane beside him, and slowly put away his identification.

"Allow me to introduce myself again.

"I am also a member of Tingen's Nighthawk team, specializing in supernatural incidents.

"Good morning, Mr. Spectator."

Before he finished, Daxter's pupils contracted. He drew back one hand as though preparing to flee.

"Officer, I don't understand." Daxter could barely preserve his composure. "I dislike this sort of joke. Perhaps I should summon security."

Still smiling, Klein slowly drew the revolver from his shoulder holster.

"Mr. Daxter, you can see my confidence and lack of hostile intent. Heh. To be honest, I wasn't entirely certain--but your reaction supplied the answer."

Every word I've spoken is true... Klein added inwardly.

Daxter relaxed slightly, though his gaze remained fixed on the revolver. "I don't understand why you sought me out. I don't believe I exposed anything."

Klein laughed. "It was coincidence--or perhaps fate arranged our meeting.

"We once crossed paths in the Evil Dragon Bar's underground market, though you didn't notice me.

"Buying the potion's supplementary ingredients first was clever. Unfortunately, I knew the formula, and your purchases attracted my attention."

Daxter exhaled as though abandoning his defense.

"I see... I thought I had been careful enough. To think that..."

He met Klein's eyes. "Officer, I know you didn't come to arrest me. What do you actually want?"

"I differ from many Nighthawks. I don't believe every independent Beyonder is a criminal waiting to happen. That would be unjust to those who obey the law."

Daxter shifted into a more relaxed posture. "The world would be peaceful if every Nighthawk, Mandated Punisher, and Machinery Hivemind member thought as you do."

"You know about all three organizations?" Klein feigned surprise. "Someone who accidentally became a Beyonder shouldn't possess that knowledge. There must be an organization behind you."

He leaned back and smiled. "The Psychology Alchemists?"

Daxter's expression twisted.

"I could see that you anticipated my response, yet I still stepped into your verbal trap..." he said in frustration.

He was beginning to understand that the Spectator state was not omnipotent. It revealed the broad purpose of another person's visit, not every specific intention.

Klein stroked the revolver's cylinder. "Doctor, we need an honest conversation, and I will begin.

"I don't regard unmanaged Beyonders as potential criminals, but I do believe every Beyonder should be registered and monitored. It is a precaution against loss of control and the greater dangers that may follow.

"I won't disrupt your ordinary life, but I want limited cooperation between us."

"Limited cooperation?" Daxter repeated thoughtfully.

"Yes. Report your condition to me regularly. You should know that someone who hasn't completely lost control may still be saved, and the Nighthawks have considerable experience.

"Provide information if you learn that a Beyonder--particularly one from your organization--intends to endanger innocent people.

"And if you wish, exchange items you don't need for ones you can use. Consider that a perk. You know what that means.

"In return, you needn't fear being suddenly prosecuted by the Nighthawks, Mandated Punishers, or Machinery Hivemind. You can live peacefully and securely.

"We'll give you an object proving your protected status, to be used only when no alternative remains."

Daxter listened in silence. Finally, he asked, "You want me to betray my organization?"

"Not betray it," Klein said sincerely. "Protect justice, morality, and compassion. Prevent evil, cruelty, and bloodshed. Beyond that, I won't demand your organization's secrets."

Daxter thought for a moment, apparently comforted by the justification. After several seconds, he extended his right hand.

"To successful cooperation."

Klein shook it with his free hand. "Successful cooperation."

After a pause, he chuckled. "Doctor, can you now confirm that you belong to the Psychology Alchemists?"

"Yes." Daxter nodded.

Spirit Vision showed no change in his emotional colors. Klein discreetly asked, "How did you join them?"

Daxter looked into his eyes. "While treating a patient here, I discovered he could see through me completely. His mind was far too lucid for a lunatic.

"His name is Hood Eugen."

Klein committed the name to memory. They spoke a little longer and established secret methods for communicating and meeting.

For the moment, Klein avoided exchanging information about potions, formulas, or rumors. At an appropriate time, he said farewell, holstered his revolver, and left.

Once Klein disappeared, Daxter exhaled and collapsed into his chair, feeling both tormented and relieved.

At 36 Zouteland Street, Dunn sat behind his desk and swept his gray eyes over Klein.

"What happened?"

Half an hour late, Klein organized his thoughts. "Captain, I located a Beyonder and confirmed that he's a Psychology Alchemist.

"He's a legitimate physician and willing to cooperate. I believe maintaining the present situation would be best. He could help us understand the Psychology Alchemists' current condition."

After several seconds, Klein added, "I want to develop him into a Nighthawk informant--a covert external member."

The word informant came from Intisian and had been coined by Emperor Roselle.

Dunn slowly nodded. "You handled it well, though in the future you should inform me before confronting such a situation.

"Give me the doctor's information and a written report describing what you did. I'll provide an object confirming his status.

"And don't tell Leonard or the others. They're trustworthy, but protocol requires strict secrecy.

"You will remain responsible for contacting the doctor."

Klein silently exhaled and smiled. "All right."

Chapter 118: August

Time passed swiftly, and Tingen bid farewell to the height of summer. Temperatures now hovered around twenty-six or twenty-seven degrees Celsius.

Splash!

Klein rose from the bathtub and stepped onto the floor, scattering droplets around him.

Standing naked, he looked down and flexed. Distinct lines appeared across his abdomen.

Daily training had produced results, and he looked considerably more vigorous than before.

That very day, Gawain had begun teaching him the basic footwork for punching and the proper techniques for generating force.

Tap. Tap. Tap. Barefoot on the bathroom floor, Klein slid forward, retreated, dodged right, and threw a punch while keeping his other arm raised defensively.

Whew. He stopped, exhaled happily, and reached for a towel.

After contacting Daxter Guderian, the asylum physician, Klein enjoyed two entire weeks without suspicious coincidences. Freed from a relentless succession of supernatural incidents, his life became stable. He received his salary on time, studied mysticism, practiced shooting and combat, experimented with recipes, helped Benson and Melissa gradually acquire proper utensils and decorations, questioned his teammates about old supernatural cases, and performed divinations at the club while strictly observing the principles he had identified.

His life grew increasingly secure. Were it not for the nights when he still missed Earth, the undiscovered red chimney, and the Misfortune Cloth Puppet's symbol that occasionally haunted his dreams, he might have grown accustomed--even attached--to his present existence.

Three Tarot Gatherings had taken place during that period, though Klein received no new pages from Roselle's diary. Justice reported that she had met two Beyonders and was maintaining contact with them. Once she entered their broader circle, she might trade for additional diary pages.

The Hanged Man had also returned to land and was handling certain affairs. He promised to resume searching when he had more time.

Justice considered both acquaintances potential Tarot Club members. Each possessed a respectable identity, distinctive sources of information, sound principles, and a character unlikely to betray secrets. Their only weakness was that both were merely Sequence 9, perhaps unsuitable for an elite secret organization like the Tarot Club.

An elite secret organization? It sounds more like a pyramid scheme... Klein had concealed his speechlessness behind a solemn sigh and agreed only to observe them further.

Justice was no longer entirely naive. She had carefully withheld their names and distinguishing traits, afraid The Hanged Man might use them to identify her.

Miss Justice says she can clearly feel the potion being digested and may complete her Spectator acting in three or four weeks. I need to obtain the Telepathist formula sooner than planned... Klein discarded the towel and began dressing while reviewing the previous day's Gathering.

During the past twenty days, he had met Daxter only once. Believing haste would undermine the relationship, he merely discussed the doctor's condition and asked inconsequential questions about the Psychology Alchemists.

Justice's rapid progress now forced him to consider how to acquire the Sequence 8 Telepathist formula from Daxter sooner.

Klein buttoned his shirt and wrapped a dry towel around his head.

Compared with Justice, his own digestion had progressed even faster than expected. By that week, the forbidden sounds and visions had disappeared whenever he entered Cogitation or used Spirit Vision.

Turning the towel, he dried his hair again and looked toward the door.

"The Seer principles I identified are remarkably effective. By next week, I should digest the potion completely. But where can I obtain a crystal from a mature Hornacis gray mountain goat's horn and a complete human-faced rose for Clown?

"Perhaps I could submit a special application like Daly. But that would attract the higher-ups' attention, and I want to develop at my own pace. We found the Aurora Order believer inside the police, but still haven't identified Mr. Z...

"Henry promised to finish investigating the red chimney by the end of this week. My private savings have recovered to slightly more than seven pounds, so at least I can make the final payment.

"The information he previously supplied about houses and tenants revealed nothing abnormal, but I don't have time to investigate every one personally...

"Perhaps I should focus on red-chimney houses with recent tenants.

"Hmm. That's one possible approach."

After another half minute of silence, he put on black trousers, a bow tie, and his shoulder holster. Then he picked up his sweat-soaked knight's training clothes, tossed them into the laundry basket, and left the bathroom.

He was still at Gawain's home after Wednesday afternoon's combat training.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti." Gawain's maid happened to pass and quickly curtsied.

Klein indicated the untidy bathroom. "Would you please clean that?"

"Of course, sir. The laundress will handle your clothes when she arrives at six," the maid replied with lowered eyes.

Laundresses received neither lodging nor meals and therefore worked for several households. They rushed from one home to another or collected clothing in bulk, washed everything together, and returned it afterward. Even then, they barely earned a living.

Klein said nothing more and returned to the sitting room to bid farewell to Gawain.

His instructor sat listlessly in a rocking chair, a light-brown blanket across his legs and the *Awwa Evening News* in his hands.

Klein knew Gawain was only in his early fifties, but his lethargy made him appear eighty.

During training, Gawain remained silent except when offering necessary instruction. Klein was too exhausted by the exercises to force casual conversation, so their relationship remained distant.

Judging by his demonstrations, Instructor Gawain is still terrifyingly strong and quick. He could probably fight three of me without difficulty... He receives police pay and owns farmland outside Tingen that provides rent. He employs a cook, a maid, and a laundress... In the Foodaholic Empire, a wealthy man in his fifties would be traveling the world.

Klein looked away, shook his head, and collected his top hat and black trench coat.

After straightening his appearance, he took his cane and followed the weed-covered stone path toward the gate.

A two-wheeled carriage stood outside the iron fence, with a familiar man beside it.

"Leonard?" Klein looked suspiciously at his disheveled teammate.

Leonard wore a white shirt, black trousers, and laceless leather boots, and twirled his hat between his hands. Seeing Klein, he smiled. "Pleasantly surprised?"

Surprised, yes. Pleased, no... Ignoring Leonard's behavior, Klein looked into the false poet's green eyes.

"What happened?"

Leonard donned his hat. "The captain wants you to work with Frye and me. I'll explain on the way."

"All right." Klein climbed into the carriage.

As the scenery sped past, Leonard picked up a document pouch and tossed it over.

Klein caught it neatly, removed the papers, and read.

"August 11th, 1:00 p.m. At a workhouse in West Borough, the bankrupt Salus attempted arson. He ultimately burned only himself to death...

"August 11th, 10:00 p.m. Harbor laborer Zid jumped into the Tussock River, ending his poverty-stricken life...

"August 11th, 8:00 p.m. On Lower Iron Cross Street, Mrs. Lauwis, who earned her living selling matchboxes, died of a sudden illness..."

The first two incidents puzzled Klein. The deaths appeared ordinary and should not concern the Nighthawks. Even the police would hesitate to waste resources investigating such obvious causes.

But as he continued reading, his brow gradually furrowed.

Two pages later, he looked sharply at Leonard.

"Aren't there far too many?"

When ordinary deaths accumulated to such an extraordinary degree, they ceased to be ordinary.

For once, Leonard was entirely serious. "The death rate over the past two weeks is five times normal.

"When Tingen Police Headquarters compiled the figures, they recognized the problem and referred it to us, the Mandated Punishers, and the Machinery Hivemind.

"Although preliminary inquiries found natural explanations for every death, the captain believes we should reinvestigate. Divination or ritualistic magic may be necessary."

Understanding dawned on Klein's face. "I see."

Leonard snapped his fingers. "You, Frye, and I form one team. He's waiting on Lower Iron Cross Street. Seeka, Royale, and Old Neil are investigating North Borough. The captain remains at the office to respond to emergencies."

"Understood." Klein nodded gravely, then remembered something. "Can we stop at my home so I can leave a note?"

He needed to tell his siblings that unexpected work would keep him from dinner.

Leonard laughed. "Of course. It's on the way."

Reassured, Klein returned to the reports, searching the names, times, and causes of death for a common thread.

Then a realization struck him.

Is this my first team mission since becoming a Nighthawk?

Chapter 119: The Real Lower Street

At 2 Daffodil Street, Klein locked the door after leaving a note and hurried toward Leonard Mitchell, who waited beside the road.

Leonard's short black hair had grown during the past month. Without grooming, it looked increasingly disheveled.

Yet the unruly hair complemented his handsome features, emerald eyes, and poetic air, giving him a distinctive charm.

Indeed, any hairstyle looks good if the face beneath it is handsome... Klein remarked inwardly. Pointing toward Iron Cross Street, he asked, "Is Frye waiting there?"

"Yes." Leonard smoothed his untucked shirt. "Did you notice any clues in the reports?"

Walking beside the road with his cane in his left hand, Klein answered, "No. The times, locations, and causes of death reveal nothing in common. Any ritual involving an evil god or devil should require a particular time or method."

Leonard touched the custom revolver concealed at his waist and chuckled.

"That isn't absolute. In my experience, some evil gods and devils are easily satisfied if the request happens to interest them.

"Besides, many deaths probably were natural. We must eliminate those before reaching the truth."

Klein glanced at him. "That's why the captain ordered us to investigate again.

"Leonard, your tone suggests considerable experience. Yet you've been a Nighthawk for only four years, and we average two supernatural cases a month--many of them simple."

Klein had always found Leonard mysterious. The man suspected something unusual about him, and his manner shifted unpredictably between quiet, arrogant, frivolous, and solemn.

"Did you also experience some fortunate encounter--something that makes you view yourself as the star of a play?" Klein guessed, drawing upon the films, novels, and dramas he knew.

Leonard laughed. "You think that only because you aren't a fully trained Nighthawk yet.

"Every six months, the Holy Cathedral compiles records of supernatural cases from all dioceses and distributes them to its members.

"In addition to your mysticism lessons, you can ask the captain for permission to enter Chanis Gate and read them."

Understanding dawned on Klein. "The captain never mentioned that."

He had yet to enter Chanis Gate himself.
