"Your mysticism lessons will soon be complete. Judging from that last test, you should now be able to create charms on your own."

"That's what I plan to do over the next few days." Klein sighed with satisfaction.

Charms differed from the protective amulets he had given Benson and Melissa. They had to be engraved with the aid of ritualistic magic and possessed distinct supernatural effects that could be used in combat.

However, a low-level charm was far from all-powerful. The spirituality infused within it gradually faded and had to be replenished every two weeks. Furthermore, each charm required a specific incantation to activate; it could not be used at will.

The charms available to the Nighthawks were also restricted to the domains of the Evernight Goddess. For the moment, Klein could create only three varieties.

The first was the Slumber Charm. Its effect resembled Dunn Smith's and Leonard Mitchell's ability to lull people to sleep through song. The second was the Requiem Charm, which could soothe ghosts, souls, zombies, and similar entities. To a certain degree, it could also counter wraiths and evil spirits. The last was the Dream Charm, which allowed its wielder to enter another person's dream.

These effects resembled the abilities granted by the Sleepless pathway's Midnight Poet and Nightmare Sequences, so Dunn and Leonard had little use for such charms. Corpse Collector Frye and the Sleepless members Royale and Kenley typically carried one or two, but they had not needed them in quite some time. They often returned their charms to Old Neil to be "recharged."

Old Neil glanced at Klein and smiled.

"I recall you saying that you've practiced so much this month that you've exhausted your materials. Are you planning to visit the underground market?"

Klein was briefly taken aback, then nodded with an aching heart.

"Yes."

He knew perfectly well how expensive the materials were. He could only hope to succeed on his first attempt instead of wasting any...

Tasked with bringing lunch underground, Klein put on his coat and hat, took up his cane, and returned to the Blackthorn Security Company on the second floor.

As he passed the recreation room, he saw that Leonard and the others had already returned and were eating lunch.

Knock! Knock! Knock!

He knocked on the Captain's door.

"Come in," Dunn's mellow voice called.

Klein opened the door and removed his hat.

"Captain, did you catch Instigator Trissy?"

Dunn rubbed his temples and shook his head wearily. "We didn't find her at Tingen Station. However, according to a telegram from Backlund, a passenger saw her in the first-class carriage of the earliest train. Unfortunately, she disembarked midway through the journey."

"What a pity." Although Klein had expected this result, he still sighed. "Divination isn't omnipotent..."

Dunn's gray eyes swept over him.

"There's no need to feel discouraged. Capturing a Sequence 7 Beyonder is no easy task. At the very least, we disrupted Trissy's evil ritual and saved more than forty innocent lives. Furthermore, we now understand her circumstances. She can no longer act with impunity.

"If she attempts anything similar, she could be noticed, identified, and reported at any time. Sooner or later, the Nighthawks, Mandated Punishers, or Machinery Hivemind will capture her. They may even kill her."

"Let's hope so. May the Goddess bless us."

Klein traced the crimson moon across his chest.

After a pause, he carefully considered his words.

"Captain, for more than a week now, I haven't heard any voices or seen any visions that shouldn't be there. That's true even when I'm in Cogitation or using Spirit Vision."

"Really?" Dunn frowned in puzzlement.

Klein immediately elaborated, "I feel as though I'm close to gaining complete control of the Seer potion. It might be related to my frequent visits to the Divination Club and the fortunes I've told for others."

"What makes you think that?" Dunn immediately adjusted his posture, looking bewildered.

Klein deliberately stammered. "E-every time I go to the Divination Club, I can feel my spirituality becoming more active. And whenever I perform a divination for someone, my body, mind, and soul all feel more relaxed. I've also developed a set of... well, a set of principles for being a Seer, and I've followed them strictly. It's similar to the Mystery Pryer's maxim, 'Do as you wish, but do no harm.' That saying inspired me to devise a maxim specifically for Seers.

"I believe this might help Beyonders gain control of their potions more quickly and reduce the risk of losing control. It's similar to how Madam Daly has always remained a Spirit Medium."

At some point, Dunn had taken out his pipe. He held it beneath his nose and inhaled its scent, seemingly forgetting Klein's presence as he contemplated the matter for several minutes.

"A remarkable hypothesis, and an intriguing experiment..."

Klein had only intended to plant the idea this time and establish a foundation for his later explanation, so he did not pursue the subject. Instead, he adopted a half-joking tone.

"Perhaps I'll become the fastest Nighthawk in history to gain control of a Sequence 9 potion."

"May the Goddess watch over you," Dunn replied, clearly not taking the claim seriously. He soon sank into thought once more.

Seeing this, Klein bid him farewell and turned to leave the office.

As he closed the door behind him, another troublesome question suddenly occurred to him.

How in the world was he supposed to act as a Clown?

Do I have to join a circus? Tingen doesn't have a permanent one. They're all traveling troupes...

Klein's expression turned bitter.

Being a Seer was still a respectable occupation. Even if someone he knew saw him practicing it, Klein could hold his head high. But if he became a Clown, his reputation would be beyond saving!

Perhaps there are other ways to act as a Clown. Circuses and clowns didn't even exist when the Blasphemy Slate appeared in the world... Forget it. I won't have the chance to advance for another two or three weeks, so there's no need to worry about this yet.

Klein pushed the question aside and headed to the reception area, where Rozanne, Mrs. Orianna, and Bredt were waiting, to collect lunch for himself and Old Neil.

Chapter 128: The Impoverished Fool

After lunch, Klein rested for only half an hour before hurrying to the Shooting Club to practice with his revolver. He did not dare relax in the slightest.

Following day after day of practice and more than a thousand rounds of ammunition, his marksmanship had finally earned Dunn Smith's basic approval. He was now fairly proficient at shooting stationary targets.

After practicing for a while, Klein put away his revolver and took a public carriage to a stop near the home of his combat instructor, Gawain. From there, he walked another ten minutes to reach the door.

He changed into the knight's training clothes he had left there to dry in the sun. By the time he had finished running, skipping rope, lifting weights, doing squats, and completing his footwork and punching drills, sweat drenched his body and exhaustion weighed on every limb.

"Take a fifteen-minute break."

With his graying blond hair and deeply lined face, Gawain looked stern and unyielding. He took out his pocket watch, flipped it open, and checked the time.

Ever since their training began, Gawain had remained almost entirely silent. He spoke to Klein only when changing exercises or correcting a mistake.

Klein panted heavily, but he did not dare stop immediately. Instead, he paced back and forth at a slow walk. The most obvious result of his combat training was his deep tan. Under the summer sun, his skin had turned bronze.

Gawain put away his pocket watch and stood beside the crude training ground behind his house. Arms crossed, he silently watched Klein cool down, as motionless as a marble statue.

"Teacher, in addition to unarmed combat, could you teach me how to use a straight sword, broadsword, rapier, and spear?" Klein asked of his own accord. Having just digested the Seer potion, he was in an excellent mood.

He had seen straight swords, rapiers, breastplates, and full suits of armor in Gawain's collection room. He knew his instructor's expertise extended far beyond hand-to-hand combat.

Bathed in sunlight, Gawain swept his gaze over Klein and replied in a low voice, "Learning those things would be useless. Such weapons have fallen behind the times. Their only remaining place is in museums and private collections..."

He fell silent for several seconds, then added in a voice heavy with the passage of time, "They have been rendered obsolete. You should concentrate on firearms. Even unarmed combat is merely supplementary."

Looking at his dispirited instructor, Klein chuckled.

"I don't agree."

"Every minister, every Member of Parliament, and every general does," Gawain replied through clenched teeth.

Klein stopped pacing and assumed the air of a seasoned armchair strategist.

"No. They've merely withdrawn from the front lines of the battlefield. They still have uses elsewhere.

"Why must hand-to-hand combat compete with firearms? The two can complement each other. Someone more agile, quicker to move, and faster to react can use a gun far more effectively."

When he saw Gawain's eyes sharpen, Klein grew smug and continued, "Nor have other weapons truly become obsolete. They merely need certain improvements to make them more portable..."

"We could organize a highly mobile unit designed to bypass the front lines, attack from the enemy's rear, and strike directly at their heart. During a small-scale surprise assault like that, a fighter skilled in close combat and familiar with many kinds of weapons could play a vital role. Just imagine it..."

Drawing on his broad but shallow knowledge, Klein combined various special-forces tactics from Earth and described them to his instructor.

At some point, Gawain's breathing grew heavier. He remained rooted in place, seemingly unwilling to disturb the scene unfolding in his imagination.

Klein stole a glance at the man's reaction and felt a surge of satisfaction. Clearing his throat, he asked in a deliberately restrained manner, "Teacher, what do you think of my proposal? Could it actually be put into practice?"

Gawain shuddered as though awakening from a dream. He stared deeply into Klein's eyes.

"Your break appears to have done you good. Repeat the entire set of exercises ten times."

Huh?

Klein stared at him blankly.

Before long, he was running again, finally realizing what had happened.

Ten sets? Teacher, no!

I don't want to celebrate fully digesting the Seer potion like this!

Hey, didn't anything I said inspire you at all...?

As he watched Klein run toward the other end of the training ground, Gawain suddenly uncrossed his arms and covered his face with one hand.

His eyes squeezed shut, making the wrinkles across his face appear deeper than ever.

After training until he nearly vomited from exhaustion, Klein took a shower, changed his clothes, and bade farewell to the still-silent Gawain. Then he boarded a public carriage and departed.

Rather than returning home, he headed to the Evil Dragon Bar near the harbor. He intended to inquire about the prices of Beyonder ingredients and purchase materials for crafting charms.

Throughout the journey, Klein remained acutely conscious of the pitiful sum of money he carried. Forcing himself to stay alert, he finally reached his destination.

I need to reserve four pounds for the remaining payment to the detective agency. That leaves only three pounds and five soli for tonight...

He touched the banknotes in his pocket, gripped his cane, and stepped down from the carriage.

The sun was already sinking toward the horizon, bathing the surrounding buildings in the glow of twilight. Inside the Evil Dragon Bar, the boxing matches and dog-versus-rat contests were beginning to warm up.

Klein passed through the billiards room and several other chambers before entering the underground market.

He looked from side to side but saw no sign of Monster Ademisaul, who was usually active nearby.

Didn't Old Neil say Ademisaul survived only because the owner of the Evil Dragon Bar fed him? Klein wondered.

As a Nighthawk, he remained vigilant about anything unusual. Approaching the muscular man guarding the entrance, he asked, "Where is Ademisaul?"

Without smiling, the guard replied, "No idea where he's sleeping. He's been like that lately. He curls up, shivering, and mutters, 'Dead, dead. They're all corpses. Everyone has to die.'"

What did he see this time? What could have triggered him?

Klein frowned and asked for further details. He wanted to know where Ademisaul slept, but the guard did not know that either.

Once I'm finished here, I'll use divination to find him and determine what happened...

Keeping the matter in mind, Klein walked toward the two rooms at the far end of the market.

According to Old Neil, the room on the left handled loans and repayments, while the one on the right dealt in valuable goods, including Beyonder ingredients.

When Klein opened the door on the right, he discovered that a partition divided the room into an inner and an outer area. Three other customers were waiting outside.

He pulled his silk top hat lower and joined the line behind them. Leaning forward on his cane, he waited in silence.

Before long, the partition door opened and a customer wearing the blue-gray uniform of a harbor worker emerged. The man kept his head down and hurried away.

Klein softly clicked his left molars twice and examined him with Spirit Vision, then did the same with the remaining three customers. Aside from the minor ailments common to most people, nothing appeared unusual.

After another ten minutes or so, his turn finally arrived.

He opened the door and entered the small room illuminated by a kerosene lamp.

After locking the door, he sat in the chair reserved for customers and looked at the old man in the black felt hat across from him.

"I'd like to know which Beyonder ingredients you currently have and how much they cost."

The old man's cheek muscles sagged, and deep wrinkles marked the corners of his eyes, though his body remained powerfully built. He found nothing strange about Klein's request. Many customers preferred not to reveal what they sought until they knew whether it was available, and typically asked to hear the entire inventory first.

The old man turned to the latest pages of his notebook, glanced at Klein, and took a sip of mead.

"Water Ghost brain tissue costs between three and fifteen pounds, depending on how intact it is. Star Crystal is one hundred and fifty pounds per fifty grams. Queen Bee Grass is two hundred pounds apiece. An adult black-spotted frog costs one hundred and seventy pounds... A Human-Faced Rose is two hundred and eighty pounds, but there's only one available..."

Klein carefully controlled his reaction. After listening to the entire list, he was surprised that an underground market of this size carried fewer than thirty Beyonder ingredients.

He touched the seven pounds in banknotes inside his pocket and recalled Miss Justice's casual attitude toward a thousand pounds. With a sigh, he said, "Unfortunately, you don't have what I'm looking for."

Before the old man could ask any questions, Klein quickly turned, opened the door, and left.

Back in the underground market, he stared around in a daze. After standing motionless for a moment, he gave a bitter sigh.

I'm probably the poorest leader of any secret organization...

The experience only strengthened his resolve to obtain the ingredients through the Nighthawks or trade for them with Justice and The Hanged Man.

After circling the underground market twice, Klein selected the materials needed to craft charms: a partially finished piece of silver, the herbal powders required for the rituals, several natural ores, and other ingredients. Altogether, he spent one pound and fifteen soli.

I have only five pounds and ten soli left in my private savings. After paying the detective the remaining balance, I'll have one pound and ten soli...

Calculating his financial situation, Klein felt utterly helpless.

Of course, he knew he had been employed for little more than a month. Over the course of a full year, he ought to be able to save more than a hundred pounds.

In another two weeks, I'll have to tell Benson and Melissa that my salary has risen to three pounds. Then we can hire a maid, but I'll no longer have any private savings...

Lost in thought, Klein walked toward the underground market's exit.

Just then, he saw Old Neil slowly entering in his customary black robe.

"Did you find everything?" Old Neil greeted him with a chuckle.

"Yes," Klein answered frankly.

Old Neil immediately clicked his tongue. "You arrived quite early."

"That's because I'm still hungry, whereas you've already eaten dinner," Klein replied casually.

After they chatted for a while, Swain, the proprietor of the Evil Dragon Bar, entered with a naval officer's uniform draped over his shoulders. Wearing a grave expression, he approached the two of them and lowered his voice.

"I need your help."

"What happened?" Old Neil immediately turned serious, and Klein felt his heart tighten.

Swain's brown hair was disheveled, and his breath reeked of alcohol. In a low voice, he said, "A member of the Mandated Punishers has lost control nearby. We must put him down before he harms any civilians!"

Chapter 129: Rampager

Lost control?

Klein's heart clenched, and the question nearly escaped his lips.

Although Dunn and Old Neil had repeatedly emphasized the possibility of losing control and the terrible consequences it brought, this was Klein's first direct encounter with such an incident. Horror, confusion, fear, and sorrow mingled within him.

Dunn's words flashed through his mind, slowing his reaction.

"Of all the cases we handle each year, roughly a quarter involve Beyonders who have lost control... And many of those Beyonders are our own teammates."

Far more experienced with such situations, Old Neil immediately asked, "Where is the Rampager? What do you need us to do?"

His response caught Klein by surprise. He had expected someone as slippery and "semi-retired" as Old Neil to find an excuse to refuse Swain, or at least demand a hefty payment in exchange for his assistance. Instead, Old Neil agreed without hesitation, paying no attention to the distinction between Nighthawks and Mandated Punishers.

Looking at Old Neil's grave expression, Klein suddenly understood.

Whether they were Nighthawks, Mandated Punishers, or members of the Machinery Hivemind, their purpose was the same: to prevent supernatural powers from harming innocent people and to preserve the peace and stability of Tingen. Faced with an urgent and dangerous situation, their sense of duty compelled them to help without hesitation.

Swain answered tersely, "Back me up!"

He did not explain how the person had lost control or where the Rampager was. He simply turned and hurried toward the exit.

The former captain of the Mandated Punishers was plainly an aging alcoholic, yet Klein discovered that he could barely keep pace. He had to break into a jog to avoid falling behind.

Glancing back, Klein saw the elderly Mystery Pryer running after them as well.

The three ignored the guards' curious stares as they passed. Swain had an old naval uniform draped over his shoulders, Old Neil wore a classical black robe, and Klein was dressed in a black trench coat. Together, they rushed through the billiards room and into the Evil Dragon Bar.

The patrons drinking inside turned away from the dog-and-rat contest to stare at them.

"Was that Boss Swain?"

"Where's he going in such a hurry?"

"Did someone default on a loan?"

Amid the quiet murmurs, most patrons soon returned their attention to the cage. Once again, they erupted into cheers and jeers, venting the frustrations of the day. A few of the more perceptive customers, however, felt a vague sense of unease.

Tap! Tap! Tap!

Klein, Old Neil, and Swain raced across the road and entered the harbor district.

"That ship." Swain slowed and pointed toward a cargo vessel nearby. "Two Mandated Punishers are keeping the Rampager occupied and preventing him from reaching the Tussock River. Help me impair and restrain him. Leave the rest to me."

Panting for breath, Old Neil said, "All right, b-but give me a minute. Phew... One minute to recover."

Swain nodded without another word. He charged aboard the ship and joined the battle.

As sounds of fighting rang out from the vessel, Old Neil looked at the somewhat nervous Klein. From a concealed pocket near his waist, he withdrew a silver charm about the size of an infant's palm and handed it over.

"A Slumber Charm. The activation incantation is 'Evernight' in ancient Hermes. After reciting it, inject your spirituality into the charm, wait three seconds, and throw it at the target."

"All right!"

Klein accepted the charm, touched by the gesture.

Both sides of the silver piece were engraved with Hermes incantations, corresponding symbols, pathway numbers, and the characteristics of the spell. Even without activating Spirit Vision, Klein could feel the profound, tranquil power flowing within it.

Old Neil straightened, took out a similar charm, and held it in his palm. As he walked toward the cargo ship, he joked, "Don't be so nervous. Relax and think about something else. For example, remember that I'm only lending you that charm. If you use it, you'll have to make another one for me. Of course, you can wait until next month, when you receive a fresh allotment of materials."

This... He really is an old hand at this...

Klein slipped the charm into his left pocket, reached into his shoulder holster, and drew his revolver. He checked the cylinder and adjusted the hammer.

"I'm not nearly as nervous now..."

Holding the gun in one hand and his cane in the other, Klein followed Old Neil up the gangway and boarded the cargo ship.

The vessel showed obvious signs of age. Although it had been converted to steam power and possessed a smokestack, its original mast and sails remained. Only the exterior and a few other sections had been plated with metal; most of the ship was still made of wood.

The sounds of battle intensified as Klein and Old Neil searched for a way into the cabin. Suddenly, a deafening crash rose above the din.

The wooden wall of the cabin burst apart, sending splinters flying in every direction. A man hurtled through the opening and slammed into the ship's railing.

Klein had no time to assess the man's injuries. His gaze locked on the monster charging toward the shattered wall.

It stood more than 1.8 meters tall and wore a tattered shirt and trousers. Dark green scales covered its ankles, while membranes of skin stretched between its fingers and toes like the webbing of an aquatic creature.

Its wrinkled head still barely resembled that of a human. Thick slime coated its scales and dripped continuously onto the deck.

Sizzle!

The dark green fluid corroded the wood, leaving visible scars behind.

Bam!

Swain struck the monster from the side, forcing it to stagger two steps.

Bam! Bam! Bam!

Even with his formidable physique, Swain was clearly weaker than the creature. His punches and kicks landed, but could neither break through its scales nor inflict any meaningful injury. The monster's counterattacks soon left him staggering in a sorry state.

Were it not for Swain's extraordinary balance and the covering fire of the other Mandated Punishers, Klein suspected the blue-eyed old man would already have been beaten to death.

Thud! Thud! Thud!

Swain retreated several steps, then threw himself forward again like a moth plunging into a flame.

Yet Klein sensed that he was building something up, patiently awaiting the proper moment.

Bam!

The monster forced Swain backward, positioning his body between it and another Mandated Punisher and blocking the latter's line of fire.

Seizing the opportunity, the creature charged toward the opening.

It meant to escape the ship and leap into the Tussock River!

Klein raised his right hand, aimed at the monster's wrinkled, slime-covered head, and pulled the trigger.

Bang!

As he had foreseen, the silver demon-hunting bullet struck its target. However, it hit one of the creature's scales and failed to penetrate deeply.

The monster released a piercing shriek, planted its feet, and sprang at Klein.

A foul, fishy stench washed over him. Klein instantly ducked and rolled aside.

Crash!

The ship shuddered under the impact as fragments scattered across the deck.

At the same moment, an aged but resonant voice pronounced an incantation in ancient Hermes.

"Evernight!"

Klein rolled twice more. Forgetting his cane, he raised his head and revolver in alarm. He saw Old Neil calmly throw the charm despite standing terrifyingly close to the monster.

Dark crimson flames instantly consumed the silver piece, accompanied by the faint sound of an explosion.

A profound, tranquil power spread outward. The monster, which had nearly smashed through the side of the ship, swayed as its movements became sluggish.

Swain charged out of the cabin. Closing the distance, he drew back one arm and pummeled the monster like a jackhammer, landing blow after blow on its head.

Yet he could barely wound the creature, much less inflict fatal damage. Nevertheless, Klein sensed that whatever the blue-eyed man had been accumulating was finally nearing its peak.

Boom!

The monster appeared to recover. It swung one arm and sent Swain stumbling back five steps, each footfall cracking the deck beneath him.

As the creature turned to leap from the cargo ship, Klein hurriedly pulled the Slumber Charm from his pocket.

He expertly spoke the ancient Hermes incantation.

"Evernight!"

The silver charm abruptly turned icy cold in his hand, as though it had been carved from snow.

Without pausing to think, Klein injected his spirituality into it. He drew back his arm and hurled the charm toward the monster.

At that instant, the murloc-like creature sprang into the air.

Dark crimson flames illuminated the gathering dusk. The soft explosion resembled a prelude to sleep as its influence swiftly radiated outward.

Thud!

The monster fell onto the dock and curled in on itself, trapped temporarily in a half-conscious state.

Klein was about to rush to the ship's side and fire into its head when Swain sprinted forward and leaped over the railing, his naval uniform long since lost.

He adjusted his posture in midair, every muscle in his body drawing taut.

Through his spiritual perception, Klein sensed something long suppressed erupt within Swain. The older man dropped from above and crashed onto the creature, straightened his back, and drove a heavy fist into its head.

Crack!

The monster's skull shattered. Dark red blood and gray brain matter, streaked with sticky green fluid, splattered across the ground.

"Is that one of the Folk of Rage's abilities?" Klein murmured from beside the broken hull.

Old Neil clutched his left arm and leaned over to look down.

Swain stood erect below, staring at the lifeless monster beneath his feet.

He took out a metal flask, removed the cap, and drank half the liquor in a single long swallow. Then he tipped the flask and poured the rest over the corpse.

When he finished, Swain seemed to have aged years. His shoulders sagged, and his back bent slightly.

Watching from above, Old Neil sighed and whispered to Klein, "I knew the Mandated Punisher who lost control. He followed Swain for almost thirty years. Together, they eliminated Water Ghosts that preyed on people along the riverbank and captured evil Beyonders attempting to escape through the Tussock River..."

He did not continue, but Klein understood.

A guardian who had rendered distinguished service and slain countless monsters had ultimately become a monster himself.

This was no isolated tragedy. It was a fate that might one day befall many members of the Nighthawks, Mandated Punishers, and Machinery Hivemind.

Chapter 130: Backlund's Secret Gathering

Klein looked at Swain standing over the monster's corpse, then glanced at the Mandated Punisher helping his semiconscious companion to his feet. An indescribable sadness suddenly welled within him.

Members of the Nighthawks, Mandated Punishers, and Machinery Hivemind could almost never be celebrated as heroes. Their deeds remained unknown to the public, sealed away in confidential files, while the danger and pain they endured were all too real.

Perhaps one day, my enemy will be one of my own teammates...

Klein sighed inwardly, feeling the heavy burden borne by every Nighthawk, Mandated Punisher, and member of the Machinery Hivemind.

At that moment, Old Neil sighed as well.

"Let's go. We shouldn't disturb them."

"All right."

Klein retrieved his cane. Just as he began to walk away, he noticed that Old Neil was still clutching his left hand.

"Are you injured?" he asked with concern.

Old Neil chuckled. "A splinter struck me earlier. If I were still young, I certainly would've dodged it. Fortunately, it's only a small cut."

He shifted his right hand, revealing a tiny wound on the back of his left hand that was still bleeding faintly.

After confirming it was nothing serious, Klein followed him down the gangway and sighed.

"Mr. Neil, you were much calmer than I expected. Even with the monster less than two meters away, you recited the incantation and used the charm without hesitation."

Although the rampaging Mandated Punisher had transformed into a monster and leaped at Klein, Old Neil had been dangerously close the entire time.

Old Neil chuckled at the compliment.

"I'm an experienced Nighthawk. Among all the dangerous situations I've faced, that one wouldn't even rank in the top ten. Once, while Dunn and I were patrolling Raphael Cemetery, a corpse turned into a zombie, climbed out of its grave, and hid among the trees. I passed right by without noticing because I was searching for a secluded spot. Heh, you know what I mean. Anyway, it jumped onto my back and seized me by the throat."

The recollection filled Klein with dread.

"And even in that situation, you remained calm enough to use a charm? Or did you cast some spell a Mystery Pryer could activate quickly?"

Old Neil glanced sideways at him and laughed. "No. Dunn put the zombie to sleep in time. The point of the story is that, as a Nighthawk, you must not only believe in yourself. You must trust your teammates as well."

Klein was silent for several seconds. Then he replied with equal parts sincerity and humor, "Mr. Neil, you're remarkably wise today."

Old Neil hopped lightly from the gangway and landed on the pier.

"That's because you usually see only the most frivolous side of me," he replied disdainfully.

The two left the harbor and walked back toward the Evil Dragon Bar.

Klein put away his revolver, set down his cane, and removed his coat. Beneath the light of a gas streetlamp, he carefully inspected it for damage.

"How fortunate. Just a few splinters and one dirty patch..."

He plucked away the bits of wood, brushed off the dust, and put the coat back on.

Old Neil watched with a smile and imitated Klein's tone.

"What a pity. There's no way to claim compensation."

Klein was momentarily speechless.

I'm not that kind of person! he protested inwardly.

When a public carriage arrived, Klein took out his silver, vine-patterned pocket watch and flipped it open.

"If there's nothing else, I should head home," he told Old Neil.

Old Neil nodded. "Go enjoy dinner with your family. You don't need to worry about the Slumber Charm. I'll have Swain compensate me. He's a wealthy man, after all. Of course, I won't ask him tonight. I have to take his feelings into account."

Klein opened his mouth, but in the end all he said was, "...Thank you for your generosity."

He quickly boarded the carriage and returned to Daffodil Street. It was already past seven, and darkness had fallen.

Klein unlocked the front door and saw Melissa removing her veiled hat and placing it on the coat rack.

He smiled and greeted her casually. "You just got back too?"

The complicated emotions weighing on him abruptly faded, replaced by a sense of warmth and ease.

"We had a practical lesson at school today," Melissa explained seriously.

Klein sniffed and caught the aroma of food. Startled, he instinctively asked, "Then who's cooking dinner?"

The moment the question left his mouth, he and Melissa answered in unison.

"Benson!"

There was a trace of alarm in both their voices.

Having overheard them, Benson emerged from the kitchen, wiping his hands on an apron.

"Do you have so little confidence in my cooking? Before Melissa learned to cook, I remember both of you waiting eagerly for me to come home and prepare dinner. Cooking is actually quite simple. If you want beef stew with potatoes, put in the beef first, then the potatoes, followed by a little seasoning..."

Klein and Melissa exchanged a look and remained silent.

Klein set aside his cane and removed his hat. Turning around, he smiled.

"I think it's time we hired a maid. Eating dinner late is very unhealthy."

"But I don't want a stranger beside us while we're talking. It would make me uncomfortable," Melissa objected, instinctively searching for an excuse.

Klein began removing his coat as he replied with a smile, "I don't mind--"

His expression suddenly froze, and his hands stopped moving.

I nearly took off my coat. My revolver is still in the holster under my arm...

"Ahem."

He cleared his throat and pretended nothing had happened.

"Don't worry about her. Once we come home, we can let the maid retire to her own room. I doubt any maid would object to resting. Hmm, but we must find one who's willing to learn how to cook."

He had no desire to endure any more meals whose ingredients he could only guess at.

Standing at the kitchen door, Benson nodded in agreement.

"When we have time, we can visit the Tingen Family Servant Assistance Association. They have extensive experience and plenty of resources in this area."

"All right, then it's settled!" Klein declared, ignoring Melissa's reluctant expression.

Backlund, Empress Borough, Viscount Glaint's residence.

Accompanied by her personal maid, Annie, Audrey Hall withdrew from the party. They went upstairs and entered the bedroom the Viscount had prepared for her.

With Annie's assistance, Audrey slowly removed her glamorous dress and lightweight dancing shoes. Then she donned a black hooded robe she had prepared in advance.

Pulling up the hood, Audrey stood before the full-length mirror and examined herself.

Shadows concealed more than half her face, leaving only her beautiful lips clearly visible.

A long black robe, my face hidden in shadow, such an air of mystery... I've always dreamed of wearing something like this!

Audrey delighted in the image for a moment, then cautiously added a soft blue boat-shaped hat beneath the hood. Its fine checkered veil fell across her face, obscuring her features even further.

"Not bad. This will do!"

Audrey slipped her feet into leather ankle boots, turned to Annie, and instructed, "Wait for me here. No matter who comes, don't open the door."

Annie looked at her helplessly. "But you must promise not to be gone for more than an hour."

"You should trust me. I've always kept my promises."

Audrey smiled, leaned toward her personal maid, embraced her, and kissed her cheek as etiquette required.

Then she strode to the hidden doorway, adjusted her hood, and slipped out of the bedroom.

She followed the concealed passage downstairs and emerged near a side entrance of the Viscount's residence, where a carriage was already waiting.

Glaint stood in the shadows. After looking Audrey over, he offered a sincere compliment.

"Dressed like that, you look very... yes, to borrow one of Emperor Roselle's favorite expressions--very cool."

"Thank you."

Audrey lifted an imaginary skirt and performed an elegant curtsy.

The two entered the carriage and left the villa, arriving at another house roughly ten minutes later.

Outside, Audrey found Apprentice Fors Wall and her friend Arbiter Xio Derecha, whom she had met several times recently.

Fors's slightly wavy brown hair and pale blue eyes gave her an air of natural indolence. Gesturing toward Xio, she said, "She's an excellent persuader. She can help you obtain whatever you need."

Xio Derecha was quite short, no more than 150 centimeters tall. Her delicate features made her look young and somewhat immature.

Although her shoulder-length blond hair was messy and unkempt, and she wore a traditional knight's training outfit, she possessed an indescribable dignity and a naturally convincing presence.

Audrey had already met her several times. With a faint smile, she asked, "Miss Xio, can I trust you?"

"You have nothing to worry about." Xio smiled and gestured reassuringly.

Just as she stepped forward to follow Audrey and Viscount Glaint, something struck the ground with a sudden clang.

Audrey turned toward the sound and saw a triangular blade gleaming coldly beside Xio's leg.

She and Xio exchanged a look, both momentarily speechless.

After nearly twenty seconds, Xio hurriedly crouched down, retrieved the blade, and concealed it on her person.

"We must be prepared for accidents. Some people lack reason and aren't easily persuaded," Xio explained with complete seriousness.

Audrey nodded and replied in a clear voice, "I believe you..."

"Those are tools for persuading the bastards to speak with us calmly," Fors added, gazing off toward the lawn.

The four ended their conversation and walked several steps to the house. They knocked on the wooden door with three long raps followed by two short ones.

The door creaked open.

Maintaining her Spectator state, Audrey calmly surveyed the interior. Numerous people sat around the room without any apparent order. Most concealed their appearances with hoods or masks, though a few made no effort at all and openly displayed their faces.

Almost immediately, Audrey noticed a black-robed man seated alone on a sofa.

He, too, wore a hood, his features hidden in shadow.

He silently observed every guest, exuding the impression that he occupied a position of command.

He's very confident, but his gaze is revolting. His eyes travel over my body like two slippery tentacles trying to strip away my clothes...

Audrey's senses were keen. She calmly observed and assessed him, though goose bumps nearly rose across her skin.

Fors introduced him in a low voice.

"That's Mr. A, a powerful Beyonder and the organizer of this secret gathering."

Chapter 131: Transaction

Mr. A? That sounds more like the alias of a criminal than the name of a powerful mystic. It can't compare to The Fool at all... No, only gods and demigods are worthy of comparison with Mr. Fool...

The thought filled Audrey with a subtle sense of superiority.

She calmly studied Mr. A, then lowered her voice and asked Fors and Xio Derecha, "Are there any stories about this man?"

The hooded Viscount Glaint was equally curious.

Xio Derecha replied solemnly, "There have been several incidents in the past. Sequence 8 Beyonders, and even some at Sequence 7, have targeted Mr. A and attempted to deal with him. Every one of them disappeared under mysterious circumstances."

"Then he truly is a powerful Beyonder," Glaint marveled.

As they spoke, the group entered the room. The guards immediately closed the door behind them.

Once Audrey's eyes adjusted to the gaslight, she saw two blackboards directly ahead, each covered with lines of writing.

Fors, holding an unlit cigarette, whispered, "Those are requests submitted by the gathering's participants. As you can imagine, many people don't want others to know what they possess, lest they attract someone's greed. Instead, they anonymously write what they're seeking or selling, along with an approximate price, on the blackboards."

Audrey nodded. Rather than observing the people around her, she turned her attention to the writing on the board to the left.

"Seeking a pair of eyes from a mature Manhal Fish."

"Dust left behind by a wraith, 165 pounds."

"Three pages from Emperor Roselle's notebook, 20 pounds."

The last entry shocked Audrey out of her Spectator state. Surprise and excitement surged through her.

These prices... They're far too... too cheap!

As she continued walking, her gaze passed over several more notices.

"Tears of an Infant Flower, 200 pounds."

"Mummy Powder, 10 grams, 5 pounds."

"Murloc Slime, 30 milliliters, 29 pounds."

"Sequence 8 Sheriff potion formula, 450 pounds."

So cheap... Everything is so cheap! Even the Beyonder ingredients cost less than three hundred pounds!

Audrey's eyes sparkled as she found seats together with her companions.

Xio Derecha leaned closer and whispered in her ear, "Is there anything you want?"

Audrey's breathing quickened. A famous quotation from Emperor Roselle flashed through her mind:

"I want them all!"

She had two older brothers, which excluded her from inheriting the family title and the principal estate. However, as a daughter dearly loved by her parents and brothers, she possessed property, farmland, pastures, mines, jewelry, stocks, and bonds in her own name. Together, they were worth approximately three hundred thousand pounds.

This formed part of her inheritance, but until her father, Earl Hall, passed away or she married, she owned those assets in name only. Each year, she received a corresponding allowance from a trust.

Even so, her annual income ranged from fifteen to twenty-five thousand pounds, making her one of the wealthiest noblewomen in the entire Loen Kingdom.

Naturally, life as a noble brought unavoidable expenses. And now that she received an annual allowance, she could no longer pester her parents for money whenever she pleased.

Restraining herself, Audrey replied, "For now, I'm interested in Emperor Roselle's notebook. I greatly admire him, and I believe the special symbols and script he created possess a mysterious power. We simply haven't discovered the correct way to decipher them."

Audrey, you're becoming more hypocritical by the day... she added inwardly.

No sooner had she spoken than a young man in a white shirt, seated nearby, sprang excitedly to his feet.

"Yes! Exactly! At last, I've met someone who shares my opinion!

"I'm the one selling those three pages. I can give them to you right now!"

Audrey was briefly taken aback, but quickly smiled.

"Then please accept my gratitude."

She took out two ten-pound notes and handed them to the young man in exchange for three pages of Emperor Roselle's diary. Of course, no one present knew they were pages from a diary, so everyone referred to them as his notebook.

Audrey leafed through the pages and confirmed that the script resembled the pages she had encountered previously.

After putting them away, she quietly asked Xio and Fors, "If these pages turn out to be counterfeit, whom should I approach? Mr. A?"

"Yes. Mr. A permits no fraud at his gatherings. I can also help mediate the matter privately," Xio Derecha replied eagerly.

"I understand."

Audrey returned to her Spectator state and surveyed the Beyonders and aspiring Beyonders around her.

The young man's excited outburst had drawn considerable attention. Many participants were now observing Audrey and Glaint, some openly and others with greater discretion. Fortunately, their hoods concealed their features well.

Sofas and chairs have been arranged haphazardly around the room, all facing the blackboards. The furniture is made from ordinary materials, suggesting that Mr. A, the organizer, isn't a noble and places little importance on the venue... Yes, given his obvious confidence, he has no need for an ostentatious setting...

Audrey calmly studied the room.

Mr. A watches every woman present. His gaze lingers especially on those who are more attractive than average... He's a lecher... Why does he look at me so often? Can he see through my robe?

The deduction startled Audrey. Disgust rose within her as though she had swallowed a fly.

Her concern quickly eased, however, when she noticed that Mr. A was not looking at her body, nor at the bodies of the other women.

That means he can't see directly through clothing. His eyesight must be exceptionally keen, almost as if he were examining me from close by. With such an ability, a hood offers little protection.

Audrey continued calmly observing the participants as they conducted their transactions, gradually forming a rough understanding of several people present.

At that moment, one of Mr. A's assistants approached and whispered to Audrey's group, "You may write your requests on a slip of paper and hand them to me. Alternatively, during the break, you may enter the small room and write anything you wish to sell directly on the blackboards."

Fors inhaled the scent of her unlit cigarette and cautiously surveyed the room.

"Have you decided which Sequence 9 formula you want?"

She had kept her promise and told Audrey and Viscount Glaint about every Sequence pathway she knew.

Audrey pretended to consider the question before replying, "Spectator. I want to become a Spectator. I also want the formula for Spectator's advancement, Telepathist."

She expected to maintain frequent contact with Fors and Xio Derecha in the future. Eventually, they would probably discover that she was a Beyonder--specifically, a Spectator. She had therefore chosen this opportunity to reveal that fact while completely concealing the existence of the Tarot Club.

Even if it means wasting some money, it'll be worth it...

Audrey silently praised her own foresight.

At the same time, she noticed Xio repeatedly glancing at the blackboards, her face showing both longing and dejection.

Xio told me that Arbiter's corresponding Sequence 8 is Sheriff. Is she looking at the formula priced at four hundred and fifty pounds? Well, it's obvious that she wants it...

She's already been an Arbiter for more than a year, and she's been unconsciously acting as one the entire time. Her potion should already be digested...

All these details point to one conclusion: Xio has no money!

While Audrey made her deductions, Viscount Glaint announced his choice.

"Apothecary. I want the Sequence 9 Apothecary formula!"

Feeling Audrey, Fors, and Xio turn their gazes toward him, he chuckled and explained, "For me, good health and freedom from serious illness or injury are the most important things in life!"

"A sensible choice. I once dreamed of becoming an Apothecary myself," Fors said with a languid smile.

Once their decisions were made, Audrey and the others wrote their requests on slips of paper and handed them to the assistant. They watched as he circled the room, collecting several more notes from other participants.

The assistant shuffled the slips before passing them to his partner at the blackboards, who transcribed their contents.

"Seeking the Spectator and Telepathist potion formulas. Price to be negotiated in person..."

After writing each request on the board, the assistant repeated it aloud three times. Anyone interested could apply to use a private room, where the assistants would help facilitate the transaction.

Audrey and Glaint waited for some time, but no one approached to negotiate. Both felt rather disappointed.

Then an assistant came to Audrey and handed her a folded slip of paper.

"This is from Mr. A," he said softly.

Audrey unfolded it and read:

"Would you be interested in any other Sequence 9 potion formulas?"

The corners of Audrey's mouth curled in disdain. In the blank space below, she wrote:

"I am interested only in Spectator."

She refolded the paper and returned it to the assistant, watching as he carried it back to Mr. A.

Mr. A glanced at the reply but said nothing, resuming his silent observation of the gathering.

Audrey's keen eyes noticed him discreetly burn the note and let its ashes fall to the floor.

Fifteen minutes later, Mr. A announced, "We will now take a break. You may speak freely with the other participants."

The young man who had sold Audrey the pages of Emperor Roselle's diary immediately approached her.

"I've already deciphered some of Emperor Roselle's special characters. I tattooed them on my body and gained remarkable abilities!

"Are you interested?"

Audrey recalled asking Mr. Fool whether the special characters in Emperor Roselle's diary possessed any inherent power. Mr. Fool had replied that they were useless unless a deity suddenly took an interest in them.

She considered the young man before her, then probed, "What remarkable abilities did you gain?"

He answered excitedly, "I've become stronger and healthier!"

Audrey looked at him with pity.

"I'm sorry. I have greater confidence in my own research."

For the remainder of the gathering, she continued observing the participants but obtained little additional information. She could only make rough deductions that some worked as doctors, lawyers, or in other ordinary professions.

Half an hour later, Audrey and her companions left and returned to Viscount Glaint's mansion, where they waited for the ball to end.

Audrey arrived home at around ten that night. She was about to ask her maid to prepare hot water when she saw her dog, Susie, shoot her a meaningful look.

My dog just gave me a look...

Audrey's feelings suddenly became very complicated.

Chapter 132: Encountering the Monster Again

Audrey found an excuse to send her maid away for a while. After locking the door, she turned toward her golden retriever, Susie--though she was no longer certain Susie could still be considered merely a pet.

"Did you hear... or perhaps discover something?"

Susie sat upright and barked, the sound reverberating through the room.

"Yes. I overheard the Earl speaking with several Members of Parliament in his study. They said the King and Prime Minister had reached an agreement. For now, they will abandon their plans to retaliate against the Feysac Empire on Balam's eastern coast. Where is Balam's eastern coast?"

The terrifying speed with which Susie was mastering Loen left Audrey with complicated feelings. After several seconds of silence, she replied, "I'll give you a map tomorrow..."

"All right!" Susie answered happily. "The King and Prime Minister believe the most urgent task is to advance the reform that will select civil servants through examinations. They hope to pass the bill through both the House of Lords and the House of Commons before October."

"Really?" Audrey asked in pleasant surprise.

This was the first issue she had attempted to guide from behind the scenes after becoming a Spectator. Seeing it become reality would give her a genuine sense of accomplishment.

Susie answered honestly, "I can't say for certain. That's only what I heard, and I couldn't fully understand what they meant. After all, I'm a dog who has only just begun learning."

Audrey stared blankly for a moment, then broke into a brilliant smile.

"Susie, you did very well! Here's your reward."

She took a bag from an ornate cabinet, tore open the seal, and placed it in front of Susie.

It contained dog biscuits made by the Backlund Pet Care Company from flour, vegetables, meat, and water--one of Susie's favorite treats.

Sitting perfectly straight, Susie sniffed the bag and waved one paw, seemingly contemplating the manner of eating most appropriate to her newfound status.

Several seconds later, she abandoned the effort and surrendered to instinct. Lunging forward, she seized the bag and ran outside.

She rose onto her hind legs, opened the door with one paw, then dashed into the shadows to enjoy her reward.

On Sunday, Klein did not wake until the afternoon because he had spent the previous night on duty at Chanis Gate. After getting ready, he boarded a trackless public carriage and traveled to the Evil Dragon Bar.

He had originally intended to use divination to locate Monster Ademisaul and determine the cause of his recent strange behavior. The Mandated Punisher's loss of control had interrupted those plans, forcing him to postpone the matter until today.

Klein passed through the billiards room and entered the underground market. There was no need to search; he immediately spotted Ademisaul trembling in a corner.

Sensing Klein's approach, the pale young man with greasy, disheveled black hair suddenly covered his eyes. Pressing himself against the wall, he tried to edge toward a side door.

Klein quickened his pace and blocked his escape, discreetly clicking his left molars twice.

Under Spirit Vision, Ademisaul's aura appeared unhealthy. All its colors were dim. Although he suffered from no serious illness, his body was extremely weak.

At the same time, Klein saw vivid fear and anxiety dominating the Monster's emotional colors. Almost none of the blue that represented rational thought remained.

His Astral Projection extends outward from deep within his Ether Body. It's uniformly transparent and colorless, like pure light. Is this a unique characteristic of a natural-born Monster?

Klein gave an almost imperceptible nod. Staring at Ademisaul, he asked, "What have you seen recently? What happened to you? Why do you hide in a corner, trembling and saying that everyone is a corpse and everyone will die?"

Ademisaul lowered his head and stared at his toes, seemingly afraid to look directly at the man before him.

Clad in blue-gray trousers and a ragged linen shirt, he trembled so violently that his whole body shook.

"No, I didn't see anything. N-no, I only had a dream. There was blood everywhere, and corpses were scattered all around. Haha! Boohoo! I was among them! I was there! I'm going to die! I'm going to die! I don't want to die! I don't want to die!"

He laughed and wept by turns, leaving Klein thoroughly confused.

Klein massaged his temples, then lowered his voice and asked, "Why are you afraid of me?"

Ademisaul froze for several seconds. Then he abruptly crouched down and screamed in abject terror.

"No!

"No!"

Everyone nearby turned to stare, and Klein suddenly felt extremely awkward.

I didn't do anything to you... Why are you screaming as if I had?

He gave a dry laugh. Seeing Ademisaul curl into a trembling fetal position and refuse to say anything beyond pleas for mercy, Klein had no choice but to move away and pretend he had merely been passing by.

Hmm, perhaps I should ask Mr. Azik for advice. But he left for northern Feysac on vacation last week and won't return until Thursday or Friday. Before that, I need to report this to the Captain...

Klein covered his mouth as he yawned, then turned and left the underground market.

After receiving that week's salary, his private savings had risen to eight pounds and ten soli. Unfortunately, truly rare Beyonder ingredients were so expensive that he could do little more than browse. Of course, he could obtain a short-term loan from Swain if he were willing to accept the high interest.

As he waited for a public carriage outside the Evil Dragon Bar, Klein considered his future plans.

In another week, I'll have repaid the twelve-pound salary advance I received at the beginning. At last, I'll be able to bring home three pounds every week. Melissa will no longer have any excuse to delay hiring a maid... The other three pounds will remain secret, allowing me to rebuild my private savings...

I also need to obtain the Telepathist formula, or at least some useful clues, from Daxter Guderian as soon as possible. I can claim that one of my subordinates needs funding and exchange it for money from Miss Justice... We can use an anonymous bank transfer, and I'll employ divination to interfere with any attempt to trace it. That should be safe and won't expose my identity...

After boarding the carriage, Klein did not head directly to the Blackthorn Security Company. Instead, he planned to spend two hours at the Divination Club.

It was part of the groundwork necessary to explain his potion's digestion.

Moreover, Klein had become quite well known in Tingen's divination circles. Former clients returned, and others came through their recommendations. On average, he performed more than ten divinations in a single afternoon.

Thus, even though he visited only twice a week, he could still earn half a pound. For the impoverished Mr. Fool, every little bit helped.

Sigh. It's a pity I made myself sound so noble in the beginning and cultivated such a perfect image. I can't simply raise my fee whenever I please...

Seated in the Divination Club's meeting room, Klein helplessly reflected on the matter while sipping Sibe black tea.

Given his current reputation, customers would still seek him out even if he charged four soli.

But as a Seer who respected fate, he could only continue charging eight pence.

Although Klein had completely digested the potion, he was unwilling to risk violating the principles of a Seer he had previously summarized. That included the rule against obtaining excessive benefits through divination. After all, he did not know whether doing so might cause him to lose control or produce some other adverse effect.

The Nighthawks' confidential records did not mention "digestion" at all. Klein therefore could not determine whether danger persisted after a potion had been completely digested, or whether he could now act contrary to its principles without consequence.

As he considered these questions, the beautiful attendant named Angelica entered and approached him. Bending slightly, she said in a soft voice, "Mr. Moretti, someone would like a divination. The Red Agate Room."

"All right."

Before coming, Klein had divined whether today was suitable for visiting the Divination Club and received a definite positive result.

He collected his silk top hat, left the meeting room, and found his client waiting outside the Red Agate Room.

She was a girl of about sixteen, dressed in a pale blue ruffled dress and holding a matching gauze hat. She had curly brown hair, a sweet face still softened by baby fat, and beautiful pale blue eyes.

"Elizabeth?"

Klein recognized his sister's close friend, a student at Ivos Public School.

He had once helped her choose an amulet and, with her assistance, resolved the incident involving Selena's magic-mirror divination.

Elizabeth likewise recognized him and exclaimed with pleasant surprise, "Mr. Moretti, it really is you! When I saw the name, I wondered whether it might be."

"I am a mysticism enthusiast, after all," Klein explained helplessly. Then he added, "Don't tell Melissa. Or Selena."

My divination clearly said today was suitable for visiting the club! Then why did I run into Elizabeth?

Shaking his head, Klein turned and opened the door to the Red Agate Room.

At the same time, he clicked his left molars twice.

They entered the room. After sitting in the diviner's chair, Klein raised his head and looked at Elizabeth.

One glance was enough to make him frown.

A faint layer of gloomy green tinged the girl's energy field.

The mark of being haunted by a spirit or wraith...

Klein calmly reached his conclusion and asked directly, "Have you recently experienced recurring nightmares?"

Elizabeth had only just locked the door and had yet to sit down. She stared at him in astonishment, taking a long moment to answer.

"Yes... That's why I came to see you."

Leaning back, Klein asked, "What happens in the dream? And when did it begin?"

"It started during the last two days of my vacation in Lamud Town. Oh, my family owns an estate there." As something of a mysticism enthusiast herself, Elizabeth remembered such details clearly. "In the dream, I always encounter a knight in full black armor. He carries an enormous broadsword, and a helmet completely covers his face. All I can see are two glowing red eyes. He keeps trying to approach me. I become frightened and run away, but every time I have the dream, the distance between us grows shorter..."

Klein considered her account, then asked, "During the two or three days before the dreams began, did you come into contact with any antiques, ancient ruins, burial objects, or tombs?"

Elizabeth searched her memory. "I-I visited a mountain near Lamud Town. There was an abandoned ancient castle."

That's the standard beginning of a ghost story...

Klein lampooned inwardly, then pressed on. "Did you leave anything behind in the castle, or take anything away from it?"

Elizabeth knitted her delicate brows. After a moment, she replied uncertainly, "Some brambles cut me, and I bled... Does leaving blood behind count?"

Klein nodded, his expression solemn and his voice grave.

"Yes."

Chapter 133: Expensive Charms

Elizabeth immediately grew anxious at Klein's answer, and her words instinctively quickened.

"Can you divine the exact cause? Better yet, could you divine a way to solve it...?"

Divination can provide only a general direction. The revelation will be vague and symbolic, making it difficult to interpret correctly... Fortunately for you, I'm no ordinary Seer. I'm a genuine scholar of mysticism!

After lampooning her request inwardly, Klein said with due solemnity, "Since this matter concerns dreams, I suggest using a corresponding method of divination."

"All right, all right." Elizabeth nodded like a famished woodpecker.

Maintaining his professional demeanor, Klein explained, "You'll need to sleep here and allow the dream to manifest. Will that be a problem?"

"No. I trust you," Elizabeth answered without hesitation, pursing her lips.

She quickly added with a stammer, "B-but I can't guarantee that I'll... that I'll have the dream."

"We're only making an attempt," Klein reassured her with a gentle smile.

He gestured toward the long sofa at the side of the Red Agate Room.

"Please."

"No, that won't be necessary. I'll sleep here." Elizabeth gently shook her head, crossed her arms, and said, "This is how I sleep at school when I'm tired after class."

Using her arms as a pillow, she leaned forward against the edge of the table.

"Very well. Pretend I'm not here."

Klein smiled as he observed the colors of her aura and emotions, using them to determine when she had fallen asleep.

"All right."

Elizabeth closed her eyes and buried her face in her folded arms, deliberately slowing her breathing.

Klein said nothing and leaned back in his chair. The room soon became unusually quiet.

It was a peaceful silence, one that made it easy to forget one's troubles.

After confirming that Elizabeth was asleep, Klein took a semicircular piece of silver from his pocket. Its surface was covered with indecipherable Hermes phrases, symbolic images, and numbers.

It was a Dream Charm, successfully crafted by Klein the previous morning.

He had also completed two Slumber Charms and two Requiem Charms. The former were made from rectangular pieces of silver and the latter from triangular ones, allowing him to distinguish between them by touch alone in the middle of an intense battle.

"Crimson," Klein softly intoned in ancient Hermes.

This was the activation incantation he had chosen. Since the charm also required an injection of spirituality, its spoken command did not need to be unique. It merely had to be short and easy to remember.

The mystical word reverberated through the room. The Dream Charm became almost weightless in Klein's hand.

After infusing it with his spirituality, he immediately placed it on the table before him.

A translucent flame silently sprang to life, enveloping the charm and turning a deep, tranquil black.

The black fire spread rapidly, surrounding both Klein and Elizabeth.

Seizing the opportunity, Klein entered Cogitation. Through his spirituality, he perceived an illusory sphere of light ahead.

Boundless darkness surrounded the luminous sphere, making it appear exceptionally lonely.

Klein did not dare delay. He extended his spirituality until it touched the illusory light.

Without a sound, the scene around him shimmered and distorted, then settled into a yellow-brown plain. Human and equine corpses littered the ground, with fresh blood and discarded weapons everywhere.

Elizabeth stood amid the carnage, dressed in a regal gown with lace cuffs and a veiled hat. She looked around in confusion.

Upon spotting Klein, she immediately brightened with surprise and delight.

"Mr. Moretti, we meet again! When Selena and I visited for a divination, I saw your name in the register and suspected it might be you. I came back several times, but I always missed you because I had classes during the day...

"Then, once summer vacation began and I finally had free time, my parents dragged me away to Lamud Town...

"You can help me, can't you?"

The girl's sudden talkativeness left Klein momentarily stunned.

So Elizabeth had long suspected I was working part-time at the Divination Club and tried to find me several times...

Yet she never behaved strangely around me!

Hmm, the surprise she showed was genuine, but it concealed her true thoughts...

Indeed, everyone reveals their most honest self in dreams--everyone except me, Mr. Fool.

While Klein's thoughts wandered, Elizabeth's dreamscape shifted.

A towering knight, nearly 1.9 meters tall, approached them while dragging a broadsword along the ground.

Black armor covered him from head to toe. Metal clanged with every step, and two flame-like points of red light shone through the slit in his visor, fixed intently upon Klein and Elizabeth.

The lingering will of a wraith... It hasn't yet reached the level of an evil spirit.

In his spiritual state, Klein had no need to activate Spirit Vision.

According to the Nighthawks' confidential records, the lingering resentment and grievance left by ordinary spirits formed the weakest and most manageable type of soul. Shadows and wraiths came next, while evil spirits were the most difficult incorporeal entities to confront. The most terrifying among them were said to rival High-Sequence Beyonders.

With that knowledge in mind, Klein stepped forward and shielded Elizabeth behind him. Then he stamped one foot down and shattered the dream.

Countless motes of light scattered like fireflies. Klein's spirituality returned to his body, and his eyes gradually readjusted to the dimness of the Red Agate Room. He saw the divination implements arranged around the table and the Dream Charm, now almost entirely consumed.

The sight made his heart ache. Charms belonging to the Evernight Goddess's domain were all made from pure silver, which made using one a painful expense.

Using charms is the same as burning money! Even without counting the cost of my labor, the materials alone average six to eight soli per charm!

He felt slightly better when he thought of Beyonders from the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun. They literally burned gold, since gold was the metal corresponding to the Sun.

Elizabeth gave a soft groan, slowly awakened, and straightened in her chair.

She stole a cautious glance at Klein.

"Mr. Moretti, did your divination reveal anything?"

"Yes." Klein nodded solemnly. "Your nightmares should disappear within a week."

I'll report this to the Captain and ask him to send someone to Lamud Town to deal with it... he added inwardly.

"Really? That's wonderful! Thank you, Mr. Moretti!"

Elizabeth became visibly excited, but then suddenly frowned.

"What's wrong?" Klein asked with concern.

"Nothing. I only remembered that I need to go home now."

She slowly took out the one-soli note she had prepared and placed it on the table. Then she picked up her hat and somewhat reluctantly bade Klein farewell.

After leaving the Red Agate Room, Elizabeth headed toward the stairs. Once she was certain no one was watching, she flailed her arms and groaned softly.

"Pins and needles! They're completely numb..."

At the Blackthorn Security Company, Dunn rubbed his forehead as he looked at Klein.

"Did you suddenly return because you've encountered another supernatural incident?"

Captain, what's with that exasperated tone...?

Klein cleared his throat and answered without hesitation, "Yes."

"What happened this time?" Dunn asked, rubbing his forehead again.

Klein organized his thoughts before replying, "There are two matters. First, when I visited the underground market to purchase materials for my charms, I found Monster Ademisaul huddled in a corner and trembling with fear."

He placed particular emphasis on having purchased the charm materials, hoping to hint that he deserved reimbursement.

Klein could not mention the fees paid to the detective investigating Daxter Guderian, since that matter involved the red chimney. He deeply regretted not hiring separate detectives for the two assignments.

Dunn failed to notice the hint and merely nodded.

"What happened to Ademisaul?"

Klein exhaled silently and gave a detailed account.

"Ademisaul had a dream in which corpses and blood covered everything. His own body was among the dead, and the sight terrified him."

Dunn considered this before slowly asking, "As a Seer, what do you believe it symbolizes?"

"A disaster--one affecting a very large area. But I have no information beyond that, and not everything in Ademisaul's dream necessarily carries symbolic meaning," Klein replied after carefully choosing his words.

"I'll report it to the Holy Cathedral and see what they have to say." Dunn shook his head with a self-deprecating smile. "This isn't my area of expertise."

Klein had no further ideas either, so he changed the subject and described the wraith haunting Elizabeth.

"Lamud Town... Is the young woman a believer in the Goddess?" Dunn asked.

"Yes," Klein confirmed.

"Then there should be no problem. We'll leave for Lamud Town now and try to have dinner there. Oh, bring Frye as well. His abilities should prove useful if the incident involves corpses and ghosts."

Dunn massaged his temples, straining to determine whether he had forgotten anything.

Had Elizabeth not believed in the Evernight Goddess, they would have needed to hand the matter to the Mandated Punishers or Machinery Hivemind according to her faith. If she followed none of the three major Churches, responsibility would fall to the Machinery Hivemind, which handled the surrounding countryside.

Klein remained silent and waited. After a while, Dunn finally added, "There will be three of us on the mission, so we can request permission to use Sealed Artifact 3-0782."

"3-0782?"

It took Klein a moment to remember that this Sealed Artifact was called the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem.

The Sacred Emblem could sustain its Beyonder influence for a long time, continuously purifying corpses and spirits within a fifteen-meter radius. Unfortunately, it also purified the souls of ordinary humans. Research indicated that a normal person exposed to it for one hour would become an imbecile capable only of praising the Sun. For Beyonders, the limit was six hours.

Ghosts and corpses, meanwhile, would be dispersed in less than a minute.

Hmm, to think the Captain remembered this Sealed Artifact's designation... Damn it, I feel as though my memory is worse than his...

Klein abruptly froze, nearly overwhelmed by the desire to hang himself.

Dunn Smith leaned back and regarded Klein with his deep gray eyes.

"You visited the Divination Club again today? Have you noticed any changes over the past two days?"

Chapter 134: It's Been More Than a Minute

Captain, that's exactly the question I wanted you to ask!

Klein nodded solemnly.

"I feel even better. I believe I could pass the Holy Cathedral's examination right now. It's a kind of sensation and confidence that I can't put into words."

Realizing his answer was somewhat vague, he added, "Perhaps a potion's name truly is crucial. Once I began strictly following the principles I'd derived for a Seer and genuinely acted as a fortune-teller, everything became smooth and natural. Yes, I can even activate Spirit Vision in a much less conspicuous way now."

Dunn frowned slightly, the light in his eyes drawing inward as he murmured thoughtfully, "The name of the potion..."

Roughly ten seconds later, he looked at Klein again.

"Do you need to go home and inform your family? Sunday is your second day off after standing watch at Chanis Gate. You're supposed to be resting."

Considering that Elizabeth was his sister's close friend, and that he had promised to resolve her problem within a week, Klein answered without hesitation, "There's no need to waste time. Once we set out, we can have the carriage make a detour past Daffodil Street."

"All right. Fetch Frye while I complete the application for Sealed Artifact 3-0782." Dunn pointed toward the recreation room diagonally across the corridor.

As a Corpse Collector, Frye lacked the abundant energy of a Sleepless. Whenever he had free time, he tended to take a nap.

Filling out the application yourself, approving it yourself, and retrieving the artifact yourself... Captain, our management system has some serious flaws...

Lampooning silently, Klein picked up his hat, left Dunn's office, and knocked on the door opposite.

After the third knock, Frye opened the door and regarded Klein with undisguised confusion.

"What is it?"

Having just awakened, his hair was disheveled and his shirt untidy, softening much of his usual cold and gloomy air.

Even so, he still looks like a corpse that just climbed out of its coffin...

Hiding his amusement, Klein answered seriously, "There's a case involving a wraith. The Captain wants your assistance."

"All right."

Frye instinctively raised a hand and smoothed his tousled hair, once again becoming the forbidding man who kept the living at a distance.

Once he was properly dressed, the two waited beside the sofa in the reception area. Seven or eight minutes later, the surroundings gradually grew warmer, as though bathed in sunlight.

Immediately afterward, Dunn Smith emerged from behind the partition holding an ancient badge roughly half the size of his palm.

The badge gleamed with a dark golden luster. Symbols representing the Sun were engraved across its surface, with lines radiating outward to its edges. It was Sealed Artifact 3-0782 from the Intis Republic, originally known as the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem.

The Intis Republic was the nation Roselle had transformed from an empire into a republic, then back into an empire. It had since established itself as a stable republic along the western coast of the Northern Continent. Its border with the Loen Kingdom ran through regions such as Midseashire and the Hornacis mountain range.

Since Intis's founding, the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun had suppressed the Church of the God of Craftsmanship, later known as the Church of the God of Steam and Machinery. With the Eternal Blazing Sun as its principal faith, Intis was also called the Kingdom of the Sun.

"Let's go. Frye, you'll drive. Cesare can't withstand the Sacred Emblem's purification for very long," Dunn reminded them calmly.

Cesare Francis was the clerk responsible for purchasing and collecting supplies, and he also served as their driver. But as an ordinary person, he could remain within fifteen meters of Sealed Artifact 3-0782 for no more than an hour. Based on Klein's understanding, the journey from Zouteland Street to Lamud Town would take at least two and a half hours, not including their detour through Daffodil Street.

"All right." Frye raised no objection and checked that he had all his personal equipment.

By the time the setting sun painted the spire of the local cathedral, the Nighthawks' carriage finally arrived in Lamud Town.

Located at the northwestern edge of Tingen, the town retained many architectural features from before the Age of Steam. There were almost no factories, and commerce largely depended on the surrounding villages.

After they stopped the carriage, Dunn glanced at the hair salon across the street and said, "I asked a local for directions. The castle ruins are only a fifteen-minute walk from here, up the mountain. Supposedly, the castle belonged to a feudal lord who ruled during the Fourth Epoch, though no one knows what happened afterward. Naturally, that's only a local legend."

"Then let's go now and deal with the wraith before nightfall. Afterward, we can take turns guarding 3-0782 and keeping it away from ordinary people," Klein suggested.

Three hours had already passed since Dunn retrieved the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem. They were drawing ever closer to a Beyonder's exposure limit. Before long, they would need to separate and give one another time to recover.

"All right," Frye replied tersely.

"No objections here." Klein touched the Slumber and Requiem Charms in his pocket.

The three Nighthawks, dressed in thin black trench coats, crossed the town and turned toward the mountain at a fork in the road. Weeds and dense shrubs encroached upon the path, but it remained broad enough for two carriages to pass side by side.

Before long, they saw the collapsed outer wall of an ancient castle. Green vines covered the sections still standing, while the exposed masonry was mottled with age.

As they drew closer, Klein felt a penetrating chill, and goose bumps rose along his arms.

"There truly is a wraith," Frye said tonelessly as he stared at the castle.

Dunn glanced sideways at the newly initiated Nighthawk and laughed.

"Don't worry. We have both 3-0782 and Frye. The wraith won't pose much of a threat."

Holding his custom revolver in one hand and the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem in the other, Dunn took the lead toward the ruined castle.

Klein followed close behind, ready at any moment to pull the trigger, swing his cane, or use one of his charms.

Whoosh! Whoosh! Whoosh!

When Dunn came within five meters of the castle, a broken stable, an old well, and other abandoned structures entered Klein's view. A freezing wind suddenly shrieked around them, as if the ruins themselves were rejecting the uninvited guests.

The three Nighthawks did not stop. The Sacred Emblem's warm, pure aura gradually dispersed the cold and pushed into the castle grounds.

They climbed over a mound of rubble, passed through the collapsed outer wall, and cautiously entered the castle, whose main entrance was long gone and whose floor was littered with broken tiles.

Fallen stone pillars filled the great hall, and moss blanketed the floor. Although the chamber was spacious, its windows were narrow and positioned high along the walls, allowing little light inside. The interior remained dim and oppressive.

That's another feature of architecture from the end of the Fourth Epoch and the beginning of the Fifth...

Klein instinctively made the assessment as a historian, then activated Spirit Vision.

At that instant, an illusory yet piercing roar erupted through the hall. Thick black fog appeared from nowhere, flooding the air and resisting the encroaching warmth and purity.

A towering figure emerged within the darkness. He wore full black plate armor and carried a broadsword so massive an ordinary person would have struggled even to lift it.

The wraith was identical to the one Klein had seen in Elizabeth's dream. Two flame-like points of red light shone through the slit in its visor. Their glow was cold, yet they glared furiously at the three Nighthawks.

"You have disturbed my slumber! You will pay with your flesh and blood!"

The knight abruptly lunged forward, instantly closing the distance to Dunn, and brought the broadsword crashing down.

Dunn swiftly retreated and raised his revolver.

Bang!

The silver demon-hunting bullet failed to penetrate the illusory black armor. It produced only a crisp, unreal clang.
