Klein and Frye simultaneously moved apart. Klein aimed his revolver at the twin red flames within the knight's helmet, while Frye's eyes turned a tranquil gray-white as he focused on the wraith.

The black-armored knight roared again, took another enormous stride toward Dunn, and swept its broadsword horizontally.

Bam!

The blade did not cut Dunn, but the sheer force hurled him aside. He crashed heavily near the doorway and coughed up a mouthful of blood.

With a metallic thud, 3-0782 fell to the floor. The wraith eagerly kicked it with one armored boot, sending the dangerous badge flying through the castle entrance and more than fifteen meters away.

Klein grew nervous and confused at the sight. Yet it also felt as though another part of him were calmly and rationally observing the unfolding changes from a distance.

Bang!

He fired again. The silver demon-hunting bullet struck the wraith's helmet and produced a burst of sparks, but caused no visible damage.

"The right gauntlet!" Frye shouted. His normally cold, somber voice was now filled with anxiety.

No sooner had he spoken than he raised his own revolver and aimed at the wraith's right metal glove.

Bang! Bang!

Acting instinctively on Frye's instruction, Klein fired almost simultaneously with him.

This time, the wraith did not rely upon its armor. It raised its broadsword and knocked both bullets aside.

Bam!

It took one great stride and slammed directly into Klein.

As he flew backward, Klein saw his chest collapse and blood spray from his mouth, yet felt no pain or discomfort whatsoever.

Then he abruptly emerged from his daze, struck the floor, rolled several times, and screamed.

Without warning, the ancient castle, the wraith, the fallen pillars, and the moss-covered floor shattered into fragments. Everything dissolved back into the black fog from which the armored knight had first emerged.

Only one thing had changed: Dunn stood with both fists tightly clenched and his body slightly bowed, his gray eyes dark and profound.

As expected, it was all a dream. The Captain pulled the wraith, Frye, and me into it at the same time. But because I'm special, I can remain lucid and rational...

Klein realized that he was still standing two meters to Dunn's right. He had neither coughed up blood nor screamed.

At that moment, Dunn straightened and faced the wraith, which was preparing to swing its sword.

"It's been more than a minute," he said calmly.

The wraith froze, then released a piercing shriek. Black vapor began pouring from its body, as though it had just received a death sentence.

No zombie or spirit below the level of an evil spirit could remain within fifteen meters of the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem for longer than a minute!

Holy hell, Captain, that was incredible!

Watching from the side, Klein nearly cheered aloud.

Dunn had used his dream ability not to attack the wraith within its own domain, but simply to delay it!

Under the Sacred Emblem's warm and pure influence, the black vapor rapidly evaporated and the chill gradually receded. Before long, the armored knight turned translucent and dissolved into the void.

Clang!

A black metal gauntlet fell to the floor, its surface coated in white frost.

Klein was about to ask the Captain for permission to collect the spoils when a sudden disturbance rippled through his spirituality.

Near the stairs separating the great hall from the dining room, something steeped in intense, illusory misery and corruption was calling to him!

Chapter 135: Portrait of a Baron

"There's something wrong over there," Klein said gravely, pointing toward the steps separating the great hall from the dining room.

He had read in the Nighthawks' confidential records that when spiritual perception produced a sensation like this, it usually indicated that something evil and corrupt was concealed nearby. Unless one was confident of handling it, the wisest choice was to avoid all contact. Sometimes, a single glance could cause irreversible harm.

Dunn looked in that direction. With his keen spiritual perception, he immediately sensed the anomaly as well. Turning to Klein, he calmly instructed, "Divine whether our investigation will be successful."

The Captain didn't ask me to perform a divination before we entered the castle. He was quite confident then... That means he believes whatever is hidden here may be more dangerous than the wraith.

Klein nodded silently, holstered his revolver, and handed his cane to Frye.

He loosened the topaz bracelet concealed within his sleeve, held the silver chain in his left hand, and silently formulated an appropriate statement.

His eyes darkened, and a faint breeze began circling him.

"The investigation of the hidden area within the ancient castle will be successful.

"The investigation of the hidden area within the ancient castle will be successful."

After reciting the statement seven times, Klein's eyes returned to their normal color. The suspended topaz was rotating clockwise.

The movement was subtle, but unmistakable.

Their investigation would be successful.

Now a fully fledged Seer, Klein immediately nodded to Dunn and Frye.

"Any danger should be within our ability to manage. It's also possible there will be no danger at all."

Dunn pinned the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem to the left side of his chest, pressed down his hat, and strode toward the stairs. He crouched and expertly began searching for a hidden mechanism.

Frye, who had already retrieved the gauntlet, returned Klein's cane. Then he gripped his revolver and vigilantly surveyed their surroundings, as though expecting an enemy to appear at any moment.

I'm still not professional enough as a Nighthawk...

Klein collected himself, drew his revolver, and likewise went on guard.

Several minutes later, Dunn triggered something while kneeling beside the stairs. A series of heavy grinding noises rose from beneath the floor.

The flagstones split apart, revealing a staircase descending into darkness. A cold, corrupt aura surged upward, so dense that it seemed almost tangible.

Dunn glanced into the opening, unpinned Sealed Artifact 3-0782, and tossed it directly through the trapdoor.

After several metallic clatters, the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem came to rest somewhere below.

If there are spirits down there, they'll probably throw 3-0782 straight back out... That would be interesting...

Klein stared into the opening and waited patiently.

The lingering aura of cold and corruption soon melted away like snow beneath the sun. Warmth and purity spread throughout the entrance.

"Klein, come down with me. Frye, remain here and prevent any other enemies from damaging the mechanism," Dunn ordered with practiced decisiveness.

"All right."

Klein did not hesitate. He stepped forward and joined Dunn, while Frye nodded and maintained his vigilance.

Dunn descended first, his footsteps echoing through the silence.

He had prepared no source of illumination. For a Beyonder of the Sleepless pathway, darkness was not an obstacle but an advantage, and his vision remained unhindered.

After descending several steps, Dunn abruptly turned to Klein.

"I forgot you can't see in the dark. I'm not accustomed to carrying anything for illumination..."

"Don't worry about me, Captain. I have Spirit Vision."

Klein found himself entirely unsurprised.

That impressive version of the Captain from a moment ago really wasn't normal!

Through Spirit Vision, a gray film seemed to cover the darkness ahead. Although everything remained blurred, Klein could at least distinguish the individual steps.

Well, the Captain is certainly healthy, and his mental state looks fine too...

Klein cautiously extended one foot at a time and slowly followed him down.

The staircase was short, reaching the floor after only fifteen steps.

Sealed Artifact 3-0782 lay nearby, radiating purity and warmth along with a faint golden glow.

With its light, Klein could see the chamber more clearly. The basement was not particularly large. Its cold and sinister atmosphere had vanished, though the air remained damp.

A black coffin stood at the center of the room, its lid secured by dark red nails.

The lid had been pushed slightly aside, revealing a headless, skeletal corpse within.

Dunn looked around, then bent down and retrieved the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem.

"Captain, this coffin... It was designed to prevent the body inside from becoming a zombie or a wraith."

Klein studied the dark red nails and their arrangement. Drawing upon his respectable knowledge of mysticism, he identified them as part of an ancient ritual meant to keep a corpse from reanimating.

At the same time, he wondered inwardly, Under normal circumstances, who would go out of their way to prevent a loved one from rising again? Hmm, the people who buried this person probably weren't family... And hiding the coffin in a basement instead of a tomb suggests they were afraid someone might discover the body...

Having pinned Sealed Artifact 3-0782 to his chest once more, Dunn approached the coffin and inspected it.

"The deceased was probably poisoned."

"Then the murderer must have used ritualistic magic to prevent him from reanimating and taking revenge. This happened around thirteen hundred years ago, yet he still became a wraith in the end... His resentment must have been extraordinary!"

Klein stepped up beside the coffin.

"But where is his head? The ritual doesn't require decapitation..."

Dunn considered the question before replying, "I have a theory. This wraith didn't always exist. It appeared only recently. The castle is a mere fifteen-minute walk from town, so local troublemakers must have visited it many times over the years. Yet before this incident, there were no rumors of a wraith haunting the ruins."

Klein gave a barely perceptible nod.

"You mean someone came here recently, opened the coffin, and took the dead man's head?"

"Yes. The ritual prevented the corpse from reanimating, but it also sealed and preserved its resentment within the coffin. Once someone opened the coffin and broke the ritual, that resentment rapidly developed into a wraith with the gauntlet's assistance..."

Klein frowned. "There's no corpse belonging to whoever opened it, so that person couldn't have been ordinary... But why take the head?"

Dunn stared at the skeleton. "For resentment to survive so long, the ritual alone probably wasn't enough. The deceased may have been a Beyonder, or perhaps a first- or second-generation descendant of a Mid-Sequence Beyonder. By Mid-Sequence, I mean the old definition--Sequence 5 or Sequence 6.

"Such bodies often possess unusual properties. His head may have been useful in a ritual or for some other purpose."

Dunn paused, then continued, "Everything I've said is conjecture, but we can test part of it. Once we're back in town, we'll split up and ask whether anyone was injured while playing here in their youth. If those people are still alive, it will prove that the wraith appeared only recently."

"A sound line of reasoning," Klein praised.

He quickly searched the basement but found nothing else.

Klein attempted to use ritualistic magic to sketch the "visitor" who had entered the chamber. Unfortunately, more than a month had passed, and the wraith's repeated appearances had severely disturbed the environment. His attempt produced no meaningful result.

He then relieved Frye at the entrance, allowing the expert in the dead to conduct a more thorough examination.

Fifteen minutes later, as the sun sank below the horizon, Dunn and Frye climbed the steps and returned to the castle's great hall.

While Dunn reached for the mechanism controlling the trapdoor, Frye gave a concise report.

"The deceased was indeed poisoned. The marks around the neck are recent--no more than three months old."

That makes it highly likely someone came here not long ago...

Klein nodded thoughtfully.

The three Nighthawks returned to Lamud Town before nightfall and rented two rooms at an inn. Whoever carried Sealed Artifact 3-0782 would take the dangerous item outside town, far from ordinary people, and walk around with it. They would change shifts every two hours, so only two rooms were necessary.

After a simple dinner, Klein, Dunn, and Frye immediately separated. They divided the town among themselves and questioned longtime residents.

In situations like this, their police identification proved extremely useful.

"Officer, why are you asking about that? I used to play in the abandoned castle when I was young... Was I ever injured? Of course. What child never falls while playing? I remember--yes, once I cut myself on a sharp stone in the castle's outer wall..."

The blond man in his forties looked at Klein in confusion but answered honestly.

He was the fourteenth person Klein had questioned. Two of them clearly remembered being injured at the castle as children.

The Captain's deduction was correct...

Klein put away his identification and smiled.

"Thank you for your cooperation. I have no further questions."

He was about to leave when the man called after him.

"Officer, are you interested in the old castle? I have an oil painting of the first baron who lived there. He was my grandfather's grandfather's grandfather... Well, it was a very long time ago. One of my ancestors took the painting from the castle and said it depicted the first Baron Lamud.

"Would you like to buy it? It's a genuine antique!"

If it were a genuine antique, your family would've sold it long ago... This man has some nerve, trying to swindle a police officer. Should I frighten him with my revolver?

Lampooning inwardly, Klein adopted the casual attitude of a window-shopper.

"Who knows whether it's genuine? I'll trust my own judgment.

"Bring it out and let me see it."

The blond man smiled, went back into the room, and rummaged around.

Some time later, he emerged carrying an oil painting.

Klein gave it a casual glance. The baron had gentle features and bronze skin, while his eyes held an indescribable depth of experience. A white, curled wig covered his head.

Wait... He looks remarkably like Mr. Azik!

Klein's eyes widened. His gaze instinctively shifted to the area below the baron's right ear.

There, he saw an otherwise unremarkable mole.

It was in exactly the same position as Mr. Azik's.

Chapter 136: Klein Is Stumped

This can't be right... How could Mr. Azik be the first of the so-called line of barons--Baron Lamud? That man lived fourteen or fifteen hundred years ago! No, wait. How do I even know the portrait depicts the first Baron Lamud?

Klein stared at the oil painting, his thoughts buzzing with confusion. He felt as though everyone around him had suddenly become a monster, or as though he had fallen into a dream where the entire world was populated by gods.

He raised his head and fixed the blond, middle-aged man with a hard stare. Reaching toward the revolver in his shoulder holster, he said in a deep voice, "This isn't an antique. Unless you explain yourself, I'll arrest you and charge you with fraud!"

He did not care whether prosecution actually fell within the police department's jurisdiction. His sole purpose was to frighten the man into telling the truth.

At the same time, Klein clicked his left molars twice to activate Spirit Vision, then watched the changing colors of the man's emotions.

The blond man jumped in alarm and stammered in a muffled voice, "No, I'm not certain it's an antique either. No--I heard it was one, but I don't understand such things. I truly have no idea. I can hardly even read. Y-yes, read..."

His eyes darted about anxiously, as though he were preparing to call for help.

Then he saw Klein adjust the cylinder and hammer of his revolver, looking ready to shoot a resisting suspect.

The man immediately straightened and stopped glancing around.

"Where did you get this painting?" Klein demanded.

The man's lips trembled as he gave a fawning smile.

"Officer, my grandfather found it in the old castle more than forty years ago. Part of the outer wall and a room on the second floor had collapsed, exposing some things no one had been able to find before. One of them was this painting. No, no, not this exact painting. The original was torn and couldn't be preserved, so my grandfather hired someone to make a copy. Mm, that's the one you just saw. I didn't lie to you. A painting made forty years ago really can be considered an antique..."

"Are you certain this is a portrait of the first Baron Lamud?"

Klein rested a finger on the trigger and kept his gaze locked on the man.

The blond man chuckled awkwardly. "I'm not certain, but that's my guess."

"On what basis?" Klein nearly laughed at his shamelessness.

"Because the original painting didn't have any label," the man answered, suddenly serious. "It's like how people call me Scoundrel Gray and my father Curly-Haired Gray, while only my grandfather was simply Gray."

Klein exhaled silently.

"Where is your grandfather now?"

"In the cemetery. He's been buried there for almost twenty years. My father, who died three years ago, is buried beside him," the blond man replied honestly.

After asking several more questions from different angles, Klein deliberately adjusted the revolver's cylinder in full view of the man, then returned the weapon to his shoulder holster.

He put away his police identification, turned with his black trench coat swirling around him, and walked back toward the inn with his hands in his pockets. Dim light spilled from the houses lining both sides of the quiet street.

I can't confirm that the painting depicts the first Baron Lamud... I wonder whether the town has reliable historical records about the castle.

Regardless, the man in that portrait belongs to the distant past--at least a thousand years ago...

Aside from the hair, he looks almost identical to Mr. Azik. Could this be reincarnation?

Perhaps instinct was what drove Mr. Azik to decline positions at universities in Backlund and come to Tingen...

Hmm, there's another possibility. The man in the portrait is Mr. Azik, and Mr. Azik is him!

The thought sent a jolt through Klein, nearly causing him to stumble on the steps ahead.

He paced around a broken gas streetlamp and drew upon the knowledge accumulated in his information-saturated previous world, developing his theories further.

Could Mr. Azik have become immortal for some reason--perhaps because he's a vampire? Is that how he survived for so long?

No, that doesn't make sense. Who ever heard of a bronze-skinned vampire...?

Besides, when I shook Mr. Azik's hand, I could clearly feel his body heat and the living blood flowing within him.

Although he dislikes the heat of the south, he isn't afraid of sunlight. He once competed in a rowing race with the other teachers beneath the blazing sun...

Hmm, there's another possibility. Perhaps Mr. Azik's Sequence potion, or some other factor, granted him an unnaturally long life at the cost of his memories! Considering his various dreams, could his memory loss be cyclical? Every few decades, he forgets his past and begins a new life, while his dreams contain fragments of his previous lives... Heh, I think I've read a novel with a similar premise...

I can't verify this through divination alone. I need to search for traces of Mr. Azik's former lives--evidence that he had no childhood and simply appeared as an adult!

Klein found himself leaning toward the latter theory, though he could not yet eliminate the possibility of reincarnation.

He reined in his chaotic thoughts and carefully considered whether to inform Captain Dunn.

If Mr. Azik is a Beyonder who has lived for a thousand years, he must be far more powerful than I imagined...

He warned me out of kindness. But there's no guarantee he'll remain friendly once he realizes I've uncovered clues about his past.

On the other hand, Mr. Azik has always treated me well. Involving the Nighthawks could very easily place him in danger...

Sigh. It seems I must divine the matter above the gray fog. That's the most appropriate choice for a Seer!

Having made his decision, Klein quickly returned to the inn.

Dunn and Frye had not yet come back, so he took the opportunity to rent another room for one soli.

Once inside, Klein used Holy Night Powder to construct a spiritual wall. Then he took four steps counterclockwise, endured the familiar ravings, and entered the world above the gray fog.

The majestic palace remained lofty and silent. The ancient, mottled bronze table and twenty-two high-backed chairs stood unchanged.

Klein sat in the seat of honor and conjured a sheet of brown goatskin parchment and a black fountain pen.

He picked up the pen and carefully wrote:

"I should tell Dunn Smith about Mr. Azik."

Then he drew the topaz pendant from his left sleeve and performed a spirit pendulum divination.

The pendant rotated counterclockwise.

He should not tell Dunn!

Klein set down the topaz pendant and considered the result. To be safe, he decided to confirm it with dream divination.

He altered the divination statement to read:

"The outcome of concealing matters related to Mr. Azik from the Nighthawks."

Holding the parchment, Klein silently repeated the statement seven times. Then he leaned back and sank into a deep sleep.

Within the hazy, distant dreamscape, he saw himself struggling as he drowned in a sea of blood.

A hand reached down and pulled him from the crimson waters. It belonged to Azik, with his bronze skin and the small mole near his ear.

The scene shattered and reassembled.

Klein found himself standing inside a dark, gloomy mausoleum. All around him, coffins opened one after another.

Azik stood at his side, gazing ahead as though searching for something.

The dream abruptly ended, and Klein once again saw the boundless gray fog.

The first scene symbolizes that if I conceal Mr. Azik's affairs, he'll help me when I'm in danger... Heh, though that danger might arise precisely because I kept his secret. What does the second scene mean? Will I discover a mausoleum with Mr. Azik? Yes, the mausoleum could also have some other symbolic meaning...

Klein clasped his hands beneath his chin as he interpreted the dream.

Combining it with the result of his spirit pendulum divination, he decided not to report his deductions to the Captain. He would mention only that a resident had shown him a purported portrait of the first Baron Lamud, and that the man in the painting resembled a history professor at Khoy University. Since Dunn might hear about the painting from another source, Klein needed to acknowledge it at least in passing.

Of course, Dunn was not acquainted with Azik and knew nothing of his strange dreams or personal account. He would have difficulty making any connection. Klein even suspected the Captain could not clearly remember what Azik looked like.

Having settled the matter, Klein prepared to leave the world above the gray fog. Just then, he noticed that the crimson star which had long remained silent was once again flickering faintly.

Intrigued, Klein extended his spirituality toward it. Once more, he saw the young man who spoke Jotun kneeling before a flawless crystal sphere.

The young man still wore tight black clothing unlike anything found in the nations of the Northern Continent. His features were blurred and distorted, but Klein could faintly distinguish his brownish-yellow hair.

He knelt and prayed, his voice filled with extraordinary anguish.

Klein leaned closer and listened. With his rudimentary command of Jotun, he could barely understand the words.

"O Magnificent Deity, please cast Your gaze upon this land You have forsaken.

"O Magnificent Deity, please free us, the People of the Dark, from the curse of our fate.

"I am willing to dedicate my life to You and use my blood to earn Your pleasure."

A forsaken land... People of the Dark... Magnificent Deity...

As Klein repeated the key phrases, he suddenly remembered a place The Hanged Man had once mentioned.

The Forsaken Land of the Gods!

It appeared in Roselle's diary as well! He even dispatched a fleet to search for it, but they found nothing...

Klein narrowed his eyes, wondering whether his guess was correct.

He tapped the edge of the long bronze table with one finger. After the third tap, he reached a decision.

Klein extended his right hand and touched the illusory crimson star.

The crimson light erupted at once, surging toward him like a flood.

Chapter 137: The City of Silver

The City of Silver, the mortuary.

Derrick stood before a short flight of stairs, staring ahead with reddened eyes. Two coffins lay before him, containing his parents.

A simple silver sword had been embedded in a stone slab nearby. Each booming peal of thunder shook the building and set the sword trembling.

The Bergs were not yet completely dead. They struggled to keep their eyes open and made feeble attempts to draw breath, but to those who knew what to look for, the radiance of their lives could no longer suppress the irreversible darkness spreading through them.

"Derrick, do it!"

An elder in a long black robe stood nearby with a staff in hand, addressing the visibly anguished young man in a deep voice.

"No, no, no!"

Derrick, whose hair was brownish yellow, shook his head again and again. He retreated with every word before finally releasing a piercing scream.

Thump!

The elder struck the floor with his staff.

"Do you want the entire city to be buried alongside your parents?

"You know that we are the People of the Dark, forsaken by God. We can survive only in this cursed land, where every dead person will become a terrifying evil spirit. Nothing can prevent it--nothing except having a family member end their life!"

"Why? Why?" Derrick asked in despair, still shaking his head. "Why is every child born in the City of Silver destined to kill their own parents...?"

The elder closed his eyes as though recalling his own past.

"This is our destiny. This is the curse we must bear. This is the will of God..."

He paused before continuing.

"Draw the sword, Derrick. This is how you show respect to your parents.

"Once it is done and you have regained your composure, you may attempt to become a Divine Blood Warrior."

Inside one coffin, Berg tried to speak. His chest heaved several times, but only a faint groan emerged.

With immense difficulty, Derrick stepped forward until he stood beside the silver sword. His right hand reached out, trembling violently.

The cold metal beneath his fingers reminded him of the Blood Ice his father had once brought home from a hunt. A piece no larger than a palm had kept their home cool for several days.

Images flashed before Derrick's eyes: his stern father teaching him swordsmanship; that same father kindly brushing dust from his back; his gentle mother mending his clothes; his courageous mother shielding him when they encountered a mutated monster; and finally, the entire family huddled around a flickering candle as they shared a meal.

Despite his desperate efforts to suppress it, a strangled sound escaped his throat. With a low growl, Derrick tightened his grip and pulled the sword free.

Tap! Tap! Tap!

Head bowed, he rushed forward, raised the blade, and drove it down with all his strength.

"Ah!"

A cry of pain rang out as blood splashed across Derrick's face and into his eyes.

His vision turned crimson. He tore the sword free and plunged it into the coffin beside the first.

The blade pierced flesh. Derrick released the hilt and staggered upright.

Without looking at the people inside the coffins, he stumbled from the mortuary as though pursued by evil spirits. His fists and teeth were clenched, and the blood on his face ran downward in streaks.

The elder, who had watched everything in silence, gave a heavy sigh.

Stone pillars lined the City of Silver's main streets. Lanterns sat atop them, each containing an unlit candle.

There was no sun in the sky, no moon and no stars--only eternal darkness and lightning that threatened to tear the world apart.

The citizens of the City of Silver traveled its dark streets by the light of those flashes. The rare hours when the lightning subsided were what they called true night, as described in ancient legends. During those periods, they had to light candles throughout the city, driving back the darkness and warning off the monsters that lurked within it.

Derrick wandered through the streets with no destination in mind. Eventually, he realized he had arrived at his own front door.

He took out his key and unlocked it. Familiar sights greeted him, but there was no concerned call from his mother and no rebuke from his father for running about outside.

The house was cold and empty.

Derrick clenched his teeth again, hurried to his room, and searched until he found the crystal sphere. His father had told him it came from a city destroyed long ago, where it had been used to worship a deity.

He knelt before the crystal sphere and prayed without hope, pleading bitterly:

"O Magnificent Deity, please cast Your gaze upon this land You have forsaken.

"O Magnificent Deity, please free us, the People of the Dark, from the curse of our fate.

"I am willing to dedicate my life to You and use my blood to earn Your pleasure."

He repeated the prayer again and again. Just as despair completely overwhelmed him and he prepared to rise, dark crimson light erupted from the flawless crystal sphere.

It surged outward like flowing water and swallowed Derrick in an instant.

When his senses returned, he found himself standing in a magnificent palace supported by towering stone pillars. An ancient table stretched before him, and at its far end sat a human figure obscured by dense fog.

Nothing else surrounded him. The palace was empty and ethereal, while boundless gray mist and incorporeal crimson lights lay beneath his feet.

A spark of hope ignited in Derrick's heart. Confused and bewildered, he stared at the figure seated at the head of the table.

"Are... are You God?"

The moment he asked, he remembered a passage from one of the City of Silver's books and hastily lowered his head.

The passage read: "Do not look directly at God."

Klein leaned back, folded his hands, and adopted a relaxed posture. Speaking in Jotun, the language of giants, he replied, "I am not God. I am merely The Fool, one who takes an interest in the long history of this world."

Klein had already clicked his left molars to activate Spirit Vision. He saw that no additional colors covered the surface of the young man's Astral Projection or the depths of his Ether Body.

This meant the youth was not a Beyonder.

The Fool...

Derrick silently pondered the title. After a long pause, he said with difficulty, "I don't care whether You are God or The Fool. My prayer will not change. I want the people of the City of Silver to be freed from the curse of our fate. I want the sun and blue sky described in our books to appear above us. And if it's possible--if it's possible, I want my parents to live again."

Hey, I'm not a wishing well...

Klein lowered his folded hands and chuckled.

"Why should I help you?"

Derrick froze. After thinking for some time, he answered, "I will offer You my soul. I will use my blood to please You."

"I have no interest in the soul or blood of a mortal."

Klein smiled and shook his head, watching the colors of the youth's emotions gradually sink into despair.

Before Derrick could speak again, Klein continued in a casual tone, "However, I can offer you an opportunity.

"I am a Fool who favors fair and equal exchange. You may trade what you can obtain with me--or with others like yourself--for the things you desire. But remember, both sides must offer something of equal value...

"This may allow you to grow stronger. Perhaps one day, you can rely upon your own power to free the City of Silver from its curse and bring the sun back to your sky."

Based on Derrick's description, Klein was fairly certain that the City of Silver lay within the so-called Forsaken Land of the Gods.

He could not yet be completely sure. Religious texts claimed that the world had existed without a sun during the First Epoch, the Chaos Epoch. Beyond the Forsaken Land of the Gods, there might be other strange regions unknown to the nations of the Northern Continent.

Derrick listened in silence. He lowered his head and, after a while, replied, "I want to become a Sun. I wish to obtain the formula for that pathway's initial Sequence potion."

Sequence, potion, Sun... The pathway controlled by the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun... It seems we do inhabit the same world.

The term "Sequence" originated with the revelation of the first Blasphemy Slate at the end of the Second Epoch, the Dark Epoch... If the City of Silver truly lies within the Forsaken Land of the Gods, it must have been separated from the Northern and Southern Continents sometime after the Second Epoch.

Could that separation be related to the cataclysm of the Third Epoch? According to legend, the Evernight Goddess, Earth Mother, and God of Combat descended into this world and joined the Lord of Storms, Eternal Blazing Sun, and God of Knowledge and Wisdom in protecting humanity from the cataclysm...

Klein extracted a surprising amount of information from the young man's few statements.

Unfortunately, he struggled both to understand Derrick and to organize his own replies, since he was not fluent in Jotun.

Fortunately, ancient Feysac was directly derived from Jotun, and Klein could be considered an expert in that language. This allowed him to learn Jotun relatively quickly and avoid embarrassing himself.

Maintaining his composed posture, Klein replied calmly, "We can discuss that transaction later. For the next two days, do not leave your home. Avoid sharing a room with anyone else if possible."

He did not know what unit of time the City of Silver used, much less whether it shared the Loen Kingdom's time. All he could do was refer broadly to "tomorrow," then wait until after the Tarot Gathering to explain when their future meetings would occur.

Klein knew Jotun contained a word for "day," so he assumed Derrick would understand even if the City of Silver did not use it as a formal measure of time.

"All right. I will follow Your instructions," Derrick replied with his head bowed, raising no objection.

Klein quietly sighed in relief. Tapping the edge of the table with one finger, he said, "Before I send you back, we must complete our first equal exchange. I have given you an opportunity to become stronger, and you must offer me something of comparable value.

"As I said, I am The Fool, and I take an interest in the long history of this world. In return, I ask for the history of the City of Silver--everything you know about it."

Derrick considered the request before answering softly, "I will recount it faithfully.

"The City of Silver has existed since the omnipotent and omniscient God, the Lord who created all things, forsook this land. No--it existed even before then, but in those days it was called the Kingdom of Silver."

Chapter 138: The Giant Pathway

The omnipotent and omniscient God... The Lord who created all things...

Klein leaned back and maintained his inscrutable posture as he contemplated the words spoken by the young man from the City of Silver.

He was no stranger to the phrase "the Lord who created all things." The Creator described in The Book of Storms, The Revelation of Evernight, and various folk legends was addressed by similar titles. Secret organizations such as the Aurora Order also used them to refer to the True Creator.

But this was the first time Klein had heard anyone in this world speak of an "omnipotent and omniscient God." Neither the Evernight Goddess, the Lord of Storms, nor the God of Steam and Machinery claimed omniscience or omnipotence.

If the City of Silver truly lies within the Forsaken Land of the Gods, then that land unquestionably belongs to this world. Perhaps "the omnipotent and omniscient God" was the title ancient people used for the Creator...

Deep in thought, Klein looked at the young man across from him and observed the colors of pain and sorrow within his emotions.

Sensing The Fool's gaze, Derrick involuntarily lowered his head.

He recalled the legends his parents had told him and spoke slowly, sadness filling his voice.

"When the sun vanished from the sky, when the clouds were torn apart, when thunder and lightning became our rulers, monsters beyond imagination emerged from the darkness. They destroyed one city after another throughout the Kingdom of Silver. The Dark Ages of humanity had begun.

"The surviving experts of the City of Silver united their strength and relied upon two mystical items to repel the Things of the Dark. Gradually, they eradicated every monster within a day's journey and established a city-state that protected the final light of human civilization."

A standard textbook account...

Klein could not help commenting inwardly.

The young man's description made the City of Silver sound as though it existed in an entirely different world from the Northern Continent.

Perhaps that's simply a unique characteristic of the Forsaken Land of the Gods.

Klein kept his emotions hidden.

Derrick steadied his breathing and continued, "For the first several decades, no crops would grow. The City of Silver suffered a terrible shortage of food, and we survived only by hunting creatures of darkness and mutated beasts. Our population fell dramatically.

"Fortunately, we discovered Black-Faced Grass. It could survive in those conditions and became our only stable and dependable source of food.

"It is said to have been the final act of mercy left to us by the magnificent God. It allowed generation after generation to survive in the City of Silver, which has now endured the Dark Ages for 2,582 years.

"Successive Chiefs recorded the passage of time. Ordinary citizens call periods of frequent lightning 'day,' and periods when the lightning subsides 'night.' It's a confusing system, and determining precise dates is very difficult."

What an extraordinary place...

Klein was glad he had not specifically said "tomorrow," instead referring vaguely to the next two days.

Derrick briefly recounted several memorable events from the City of Silver's history before continuing, "Once the population recovered and the number of Beyonders increased, the six-member council began forming elite teams to explore the darkness.

"We have now explored all the territory once belonging to the kingdom, as well as the surrounding cities. Our expeditions continue deeper into regions that are darker and more terrifying. Along the frontier, we discovered ruined cities built in unfamiliar architectural styles. We suspect they were sanctuaries established by other surviving humans. Unfortunately, they ultimately fell to the Things of the Dark."

By "Things of the Dark," he must mean those unimaginable monsters that hide within the darkness.

Klein gave an almost imperceptible nod.

"The Kingdom of Silver was once ruled by the Giant King. Therefore, the Beyonder pathway we control is the Giant pathway, also known as the Divine Blood Warrior pathway," Derrick said, moving on to the city's present circumstances. "By killing certain monsters and exploring ruined cities, we obtained potion formulas belonging to other pathways. However, none of those pathways are complete."

Klein's attention sharpened at once, though his posture barely changed.

I love learning about Sequence potions! The Giant King... Does that mean the City of Silver and the Northern Continent share the same history--the history of the Second Epoch?

Hmm, killing monsters allowed them to obtain potion formulas? Is this some kind of game? No, there's another possibility. Those monsters may once have been humans--Beyonders...

A heavy feeling settled over Klein.

Seeing that The Fool offered no response, Derrick gritted his teeth, considered his words, and continued.

"The Giant pathway consists of Sequence 9 Beyonder Warrior, Sequence 8 Gladiator, Sequence 7 Weapons Master, Sequence 6 Dawn Paladin, Sequence 5 Guardian, and Sequence 4 Demon Hunter. Only the elders of the six-member council know the names of the higher Sequences."

Sequence 4 Demon Hunter... So that's the name of a High-Sequence potion! This is the first one I've ever learned!

Klein was delighted to have finally discovered the name of a High Sequence. Still, he suspected it was an ancient title that differed from the modern name used on the Northern Continent, much as Storm Priest differed from Seafarer.

Wait--Beyonder Warrior, Gladiator, Weapons Master... Those sound familiar.

Of course! They closely resembled the pathway controlled by the Church of the God of Combat: Sequence 9 Warrior, Sequence 8 Pugilist, and Sequence 7 Weapons Master. Klein's security clearance allowed him to know only the first three Sequences, but the similarity was unmistakable.

Their essential meanings are nearly identical. The complete pathway controlled by the Church of the God of Combat must be the so-called Giant pathway... According to legend, a god arose during the Third Epoch, the Cataclysm Epoch, and inherited the Giant King's legacy. Or could He be an ancient Giant Himself?

Klein calmly analyzed the information while maintaining his composed appearance.

Derrick continued, "Once we survived the initial hardships, the City of Silver came under the rule of a six-member council. The highest-ranking elder is called the Chief, while the other five hold equal status... The current council consists of three Demon Hunters, the two most promising Guardians, and one Shepherd."

The City of Silver has three High-Sequence Beyonders--three demigod-like experts! Those three alone could destroy the Tarot Club a hundred times over...

Klein felt a flicker of fear. He had never before attempted to recruit someone under the eyes of a High-Sequence Beyonder.

However, Derrick was still an ordinary person, not even a Sequence 9. The city's leaders were unlikely to pay him any attention for a long time, and Klein gradually relaxed.

Is Shepherd part of another pathway, perhaps one of the incomplete ones? The name sounds reminiscent of the Aurora Order. What was the name of the Aurora Order member who wrote to Mr. Z? He kept speaking of the "Lord's lambs"...

Maintaining his leisurely posture, Klein casually asked, "Shepherd?"

"Yes. We discovered that pathway in a city destroyed by the Things of the Dark. We possess formulas only as far as Sequence 5 Shepherd. But Elder Lovia is extremely powerful, strange, and frightening. It is said that she once defeated an evil spirit equivalent to a High-Sequence Beyonder without suffering any injury. Therefore, when a seat opened on the six-member council, they made an exception for her," Derrick explained, fear coloring his voice.

Klein considered this, then asked with a smile, "What are the Sequences preceding Shepherd? The name sounds familiar. As you know, the historical and modern names of a Sequence often differ."

"The names of potions have never changed in the City of Silver," Derrick instinctively objected. Then he lowered his head and answered, "Sequence 9 is Secrets Suppliant..."

Indeed!

Klein was pleased to see his theory confirmed.

That's the Aurora Order's Sequence 9!

"Sequence 8 is Listener, Sequence 7 is Shadow Ascetic, Sequence 6 is Rose Bishop, and Sequence 5 is Shepherd," Derrick recited.

Listener... Heh, I already know more than the records available to the Tingen Nighthawks.

In an excellent mood, Klein gestured for Derrick to continue.

Derrick gave a general account of the City of Silver's present situation. Eventually, he could no longer restrain himself.

"We bear a curse upon our destiny. Whether a citizen of the City of Silver is an ordinary person or a Beyonder, everyone becomes an evil spirit after death. The evil spirit left by a Beyonder is merely stranger, more terrifying, and far more difficult to destroy. On many occasions in the past, this curse nearly annihilated the City of Silver. The only way to prevent the dead from rising is for them to be killed by someone of their own bloodline."

"That is a cruel burden," Klein said. "I hope you can become strong enough to discover a way for the people of the City of Silver to escape this curse."

As The Fool was little more than an empty shell, all Klein could offer for free were a few encouraging words.

"That's why I want to become a Sun... When sunlight still shone upon this land, we never suffered from such a curse," Derrick murmured, his voice thick with pain.

Klein nodded slightly.

"You will have your opportunity. Remember, I may bring you here at any time during the next two days. Try to remain away from other people."

"All right," Derrick replied solemnly.

"Before then, you must choose a code name."

Klein smiled and pointed toward the deck of tarot cards that had appeared upon the table.

Certain that Derrick had never encountered tarot cards, he briefly explained them.

"Choose one card as your code name--anything except The Fool, Justice, and The Hanged Man."

Derrick stepped forward, examined the cards, and answered without hesitation.

"The Sun. I choose The Sun."

"Remember your choice. It will remain with you for the rest of your life," Klein replied in the manner of a charlatan.

At the same time, he extended one hand and severed the connection with deliberate restraint. The crimson glow receded, and the young man across from him became incorporeal before gradually dissolving from sight.

Chapter 139: Studying 3-0782

After the crimson light before him dissipated, Derrick Berg once again saw his room and the pure crystal ball in his hands.

Crack!

The crystal ball shattered from within. Part of it transformed into illusory beams of light that flew into the surrounding void, while the remaining crystalline fragments clattered to the floor.

Derrick stared in astonishment. In the bronze mirror, he saw the traces of blood on his face. He also noticed a crimson light spiraling across the back of his right hand, forming a circle with lines radiating from its edge.

The strange symbol sank into the back of his hand and vanished.

Derrick remained dazed for the span of several flashes of lightning before finally coming to his senses.

He looked at the fragments of the crystal ball on the floor, then at the back of his right hand. His gaze grew resolute.

He left his bedroom, returned to the living room, and opened the door to look up at the sky above the City of Silver.

An arc of lightning streaked across the heavens, bathing the city in a silver sheen. Rumbling thunder followed close behind. The world belonged to darkness, and the oppressive blackness, bereft of even a speck of light, left only despair in people's hearts.

Derrick clenched his fists. There was no joy in his eyes; they remained filled with lingering grief and pain.

But he was no longer lost.

Phew, it looks like I've managed to trick another person into becoming a member. No, I've managed to recruit another member...

Klein shook his head, privately mocking the Tarot Club's present strength.

Its leader, The Fool, was merely a Sequence 9 who had only just fully digested the Seer potion!

Meanwhile, the hopeless City of Silver described by The Sun had at least three Sequence 4 High-Sequence Beyonders!

After I mention the acting method one more time, I can begin explaining the details to the Captain and submit my special application. At the very least, once I become a Clown, I'll no longer be assigned only support duties.

Klein did not linger in the world above the gray fog. He extended his spirituality, wrapped it around himself, and initiated his descent.

Passing through the gray fog and the maddening ravings, he returned to his room and dispelled the wall of spirituality.

Klein then picked up the key and left the room. He first checked the two rooms Dunn had booked and confirmed that neither the Captain nor Frye had returned. Then he went downstairs and handed the key back to the proprietor.

The man glanced at the wall clock and gave him a thumbs-up.

"Well done!"

Hey, have you misunderstood why I rented a room by the hour?

Klein wanted to explain himself, but ultimately decided to let the misunderstanding stand.

Feeling unjustly accused, he tried to console himself.

Yes, this way, he won't mention to the Captain that I rented another room!

After leaving the inn and going through the motions, Klein performed a quick divination and returned when the result indicated it was safe. As expected, he found Dunn and Frye in one of the rooms on the second floor, discussing what they had learned from their investigations.

"We can confirm that the wraith appeared sometime within the past three months," Dunn summarized with a nod as Klein entered.

Klein immediately agreed. "My investigation confirmed that as well."

He recounted the main points of his inquiries, then concluded, "Heh, there's a local named Scoundrel Gray who claimed to own a portrait of the first Baron Lamud. According to him, it was an antique oil painting more than a thousand years old."

"Don't tell me you bought it?" Dunn asked, his eyes glinting with surprise.

Captain, do you really think I'm stupid enough to fall for that?

Klein gave a dry laugh.

"No, I didn't. Although I'm a history student, I've attended several archaeology classes and have some experience in this area. I can more or less tell whether something is fake. Heh, the man in the portrait looked a little like my history teacher, Mr. Azik."

He casually slipped in the most important piece of information.

As expected, Dunn did not pay it much attention. He massaged his temples and said, "This is a small town near a historical site. There will always be a great many 'antiques' here. I just saw a vendor selling silver wineglasses that supposedly belonged to Baron Lamud."

"Someone tried to sell me the Lamud family's insignia, claiming it had been excavated from the castle," Frye added.

Klein instinctively asked, "Did either of you buy anything?"

Frye and Dunn looked at each other and declined to continue the subject.

"Your next task is to take Sealed Artifact 3-0782 out of town to an uninhabited area. Otherwise, half the people in this inn will become idiots who do nothing but babble praises to the Sun. Will you go first, or should Frye?" Dunn fixed his deep gray eyes on Klein.

"I will." Klein raised his hand slightly and smiled. "It's still early, so I can come back and enjoy a good night's sleep afterward. We're taking two-hour shifts, right?"

"Yes. Frye, accompany Klein and decide where you'll exchange the Sealed Artifact." Dunn turned toward the Corpse Collector.

When they had split up to investigate, Dunn had already found an opportunity to hand Sealed Artifact 3-0782 to Frye. Otherwise, Dunn himself would have been purified and begun praising the Sun. Frye had not yet had enough time to recover, so he could carry the item for only another three hours.

"All right." Frye took the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem from the inner pocket of his black trench coat and handed it to Klein.

Klein accepted the item with considerable curiosity and interest. The metal was warm to the touch, as though hot water flowed within it.

Its warm, gentle radiance spread outward in rippling waves, carrying a pure fragrance with it. At the same time, Klein felt the dark golden Sacred Emblem, carved with the symbol of the Sun, cleansing his spirituality, removing its impurities and leaving it pure.

Of course, every Sealed Artifact has its dangers. A moment of carelessness could mean death. A fate worse than death is also possible...

Muttering inwardly, he placed Sealed Artifact 3-0782 in his inner pocket.

After checking his revolver, charms, and cane, he left the room with Frye. Together, they headed straight for the outskirts of Lamud Town.

The two circled an area beside a sparse, deserted forest and confirmed that no one was within dozens of meters.

"Drive away anyone who approaches," Frye reminded him coldly. "I'll return to relieve you in two hours."

"Sounds good," Klein replied with a smile.

After watching Frye head back into town, Klein approached a tall boulder he had noticed earlier. He gathered some leaves from a nearby tree and used them to wipe its surface.

He ran a finger over the top of the rock and examined it beneath the crimson moonlight.

After confirming that it was clean, Klein spread his black trench coat over it and sat down.

Why stand when I can sit?

After several minutes of silence, he glanced at the dark, quiet, rather frightening forest. Unable to help himself, he stood, took several metal bottles from his hidden pockets, and scattered their contents--powdered herbs and essential oils--around the boulder.

Klein recited an incantation in Hermes. With the aid of the materials, he created a wall of spirituality that sealed off the area around him.

He performed this simple ritual for two reasons. First, he did not want to rely too heavily on a Seer's premonition of danger to protect him from a surprise attack by corpses or spirits. The second reason was--was to keep the insects away...

This is a hundred times better than insect repellent!

Satisfied, Klein sat back down.

After another few minutes, curiosity prompted him to take out Sealed Artifact 3-0782 and carefully examine the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem.

I wonder if divination could reveal its origins and how it acquired its special properties...

He took out the pen and paper he always carried and wrote a divination statement:

"The origin of the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem in my hand."

As a qualified, genuine Seer, Klein had made all the preparations necessary to perform divination anywhere.

After reciting the statement seven times, he closed his eyes and entered Cogitation, using it as a springboard into a dream.

In the dream, he saw only fragmented points of light. Beyond that, he learned nothing.

Yes, the Church must have had other Seers attempt the same thing in the past. The fact that its origins aren't recorded means their divinations must have failed, just as mine did...

Klein sighed, then had another thought.

I wonder what would happen if I eliminated the interference?

The idea immediately consumed him, pushing his curiosity to its peak.

After hesitating for more than ten minutes, he stood. Since no one was nearby and he was in a secluded area by the forest, he decided it should be safe. Within his wall of spirituality, he took four steps counterclockwise and once again entered the world above the gray fog.

Klein sat in the seat of honor at the ancient table within the magnificent palace. He conjured several sheets of yellowish-brown goatskin, a black fountain pen, and the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem.

"It feels remarkably real..."

He rubbed Sealed Artifact 3-0782 between his fingers and found the tactile sensation identical to what he had felt in the outside world.

Was it instantiated from my sensory impression of it?

Klein muttered to himself, then wrote the statement he had used before:

"The origin of the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem in my hand."

After reciting it seven times, he held the goatskin and Sealed Artifact 3-0782 in his hands, leaned back, and entered a dream.

Within the hazy dreamscape, Klein saw a drop of glowing golden liquid. It was warm and radiant.

It hung above an altar before a man dressed in a classical white robe.

The man's back was turned to Klein. Devoid of all signs of life, he slowly collapsed toward the sacrificial altar.

At that moment, the Sun Sacred Emblem in his hand touched the golden liquid, which swiftly seeped into the emblem.

The dream rapidly dissolved, and Klein awoke.

So that golden liquid is what made the Sacred Emblem so effective and uncontrollable even today. Hmm, decades have passed since the emblem was discovered, yet its purifying power hasn't diminished. What could the golden liquid be? Some high-level Beyonder ingredient?

Klein toyed with Sealed Artifact 3-0782 as he sank into deep thought.

After several minutes of deliberation, he tried to reproduce the sensation he had experienced in the dream. He wanted to separate the golden liquid from the conjured Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem.

He succeeded almost as soon as the thought occurred to him. Klein stared in astonishment at the emblem, which no longer felt warm or pure, then at the drop of golden liquid hanging silently in the air. His admiration for this mysterious space above the gray fog grew even greater.

This is practically a miracle, even if the separation and instantiation here aren't real!

With great excitement, he wrote a new statement.

"The origin of this drop of golden liquid."

Chapter 140: Expert at Courting Death

"The origin of this drop of golden liquid."

After reciting the divination statement seven times, Klein held the goatskin and the illusory golden liquid, then leaned back in his chair.

He did not know whether he could perform divination with an object instantiated purely from a sensory impression. All he could do was form a bold hypothesis and seek careful confirmation.

Within seconds, Klein's eyes darkened from brown to black as he entered Cogitation.

His eyelids drooped, and he "saw" the illusory, hazy dream.

Within the shattered, indistinct world, a blinding golden sun suddenly appeared!

A deep grunt reverberated through the void. Pure, immaculate light instantly illuminated everything as golden, searing flames swept outward.

Boom!

Klein was abruptly expelled from the dream and flung onto his side, shuddering. His body seemed to transform into a tremendous bonfire, raging flames consuming him.

At that moment, his thoughts were in utter disarray. No coherent idea could emerge from the chaos in his mind.

Rumble!

The mysterious space above the gray fog shook violently. The lofty palace collapsed inch by inch, and the ancient, mottled bronze table broke into several pieces.

The terrifying upheaval lasted only three seconds before the world above the gray fog returned to tranquility, as though nothing had happened.

The golden flames enveloping Klein gradually died away. He rolled across the floor, his skin charred, groaning in pain until he finally regained the ability to think.

Supporting himself on the armrest of the high-backed chair, he struggled to his feet, terrified and bewildered by what had just occurred.

He had never imagined that a mere divination could have such consequences!

Panting, he raised his head and surveyed his surroundings. The lofty palace and ancient bronze table, both of which seemed to have stood unchanged since antiquity, had suffered extensive damage. Nothing abnormal had ever occurred in the world above the gray fog, making this destruction utterly unprecedented.

What happened? Did my divination point toward some unfathomable existence?

Klein gradually calmed down. As his burned flesh sloughed away, he began to speculate.

If this mysterious space above the gray fog hadn't protected me, there might not even be ashes left... Could that drop of golden liquid be the blood of a god? Did I see the Eternal Blazing Sun, or some mighty angel under Him? No, that was the sun itself, so it was probably the former... Damn it, did I just look directly at a god?

The more Klein thought about it, the more frightened he became. He felt as though he had narrowly escaped death.

Those who know nothing fear nothing, but those who don't court death won't die... I can't casually divine anything and everything in the future. Who knows what I might see!

If this happened again, I don't know whether this mysterious space could protect me from fatal harm... If it couldn't, I'd truly die...

Yes, I absolutely cannot continue experimenting with the golden liquid. The existence I saw was most likely the Eternal Blazing Sun. He must have sensed the sudden, concealed, and unexpected influence of a divination from above the gray fog and failed to react in time... If He were prepared, this mysterious space might not be able to withstand the consequences...

By the time Klein reached this conclusion, his body had already returned to normal. It was no longer charred, though he appeared dimmer and more incorporeal than before.

He raised a hand to massage his temples, then willed the palace and long table to restore themselves.

The palace, built as though it were the home of a giant, and the long table cast from bronze immediately returned to normal. Everything appeared exactly as it had before.

Klein sat down and leaned against the back of his chair, mocking himself inwardly.

Well, this wasn't entirely bad. At least I learned something about the limits of this mysterious space and now have a rough benchmark... Only powers approaching the level of the gods' angels can fully affect the area above the gray fog?

Sigh, I need to add another rule to my Seer principles: "Do not casually divine anything involving a high-level entity." Yes, I mustn't activate my Spirit Vision recklessly either. If I look directly at something that shouldn't be seen, it could be the end of me. In the outside world, I don't have this mysterious space to ward off most of the negative effects...

After a while, Klein's expression grew peculiar as fragments of knowledge reverberated through his mind.

Yes, knowledge!

Throughout his brief contact with what appeared to be the Eternal Blazing Sun, Klein had remained in a state of divination. As a result, his instincts had divined certain information and knowledge from the being he had gazed upon.

He quickly used dream divination to recall and organize the incidental knowledge he had acquired. Then he picked up the black fountain pen and wrote line after line.

"1. Do not look directly at God.

"2. White Angel.

"3. The method for creating a Flaring Sun Charm... It is a relatively high-level charm in the Sun domain. Its power can be preserved for one year before deteriorating... There is no need to perform a ritual praying to the Eternal Blazing Sun. Instead, Sealed Artifact 3-0782 takes the ritual's place, allowing the charm to draw power from the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem...

"4. Extremely hostile toward the Lord of Storms and the God of Knowledge and Wisdom.

"5. Bard potion formula:

"Main ingredients: one Crystal Sunflower, or the tail feather of an adult Flint Bird, or the tail feather of a Fire Bird... One piece of Siren Rock or one Singing Sunflower...

"Supplementary ingredients: one blade of Midsummer Grass, five drops of July Wine Juice, one blade of Elf Dark Leaf...

"6. Light Suppliant potion formula:

"Main ingredients: one piece of Brilliance Rock or Dazzling Soul powder, or... the blood of a Mirror Hedgehog or the heart of a Magma Titan...

"Supplementary ingredients: one Golden-edged Sunflower, three drops of Aconite Juice...

"7. Priest of Light potion formula:

"Information on the main ingredients is missing.

"Supplementary ingredients: five grams of Rosemary, seven drops of fingered citron juice, Rock Water...

"8. Sequence 4 Unshadowed potion formula: The main ingredient can be the golden blood of a god extracted from the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem. It can also be replaced by three tail feathers from adult Sun Divine Birds and one piece of Holy Brilliance Rock.

"Information on the supplementary ingredients is missing..."

After writing all eight entries, Klein could not help rapping his knuckles against the edge of the long bronze table.

He had gained far more than he had imagined!

He had already been satisfied simply to survive his reckless divination, yet now he had received an unexpected "survival reward."

From the confidential information available to the Nighthawks, he knew that the pathway controlled by the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun was called the Sun pathway, and its Sequence 9 was Bard. Through song, a Bard could instill courage and strength in themselves and their allies. It was a "profession" that inspired devotion and submission. Its slogan was "Let us praise the Sun!"

The corresponding Sequence 8 was Light Suppliant. They could cast spells and conduct rituals in the Sun domain, abilities particularly effective against corpses and spirits. Sequence 7 was called Solar High Priest, which greatly enhanced spells and rituals within that domain.

In other words, I've obtained the complete Sequence 9 and Sequence 8 potion formulas for the Sun pathway. Yes, unlike the formulas I've seen before, these even list substitute materials and ingredient names from different eras... As expected of formulas divined directly from the Eternal Blazing Sun!

Klein was deeply satisfied.

He had originally planned to see whether The Hanged Man could fulfill the request of the young man from the City of Silver. After all, the Church of Storms and the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun were the oldest orthodox faiths. They had fought each other for thousands of years, so it was only reasonable that each church would have learned the lower Sequences of the other's pathway.

The Hanged Man might never have cared about the Sun pathway before, but since he's very likely a Sequence 7 Seafarer, it probably wouldn't be difficult for him to gather the information if he truly tried. But I no longer need his help. I solved it myself through an unbelievable yet extraordinarily dangerous method... Miss Justice, Mr. Hanged Man, my friend The Sun, your Fool nearly turned himself into a charred corpse...

Klein lampooned himself inwardly, still gripped by lingering fear.

He lowered his head and studied the notes on the goatskin before him, then considered another formula.

Could Priest of Light be an ancient name for Solar High Priest? The Nighthawks' confidential records never mentioned it, and my divination didn't reveal its Sequence number... Is it Sequence 6 or Sequence 5?

Sequence 4 Unshadowed... This is the first High-Sequence formula I've obtained! What a pity the supplementary ingredients are missing. How can I fill in the blanks? I can't believe that drop of golden liquid truly is the blood of a god. Sealed Artifact 3-0782 is probably far more powerful than anyone imagines.

From what I can tell, it's powerful enough to be classified as a Grade 1 Sealed Artifact.

Yes, the Nighthawks who examined it before probably only determined whether it exhibited traits of a living being, how much danger it posed to nearby humans, how difficult its effects were to control, and whether it could be used against corpses and spirits. They had no way to discover its extraordinary origin.

The Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem could probably even contend with evil spirits... But where would an examiner find an evil spirit to test it against?

As an official Nighthawk, I can't become the owner of Sealed Artifact 3-0782. But, yes, perhaps I can find an opportunity to make a Flaring Sun Charm and draw some of its power? Sigh, I certainly can't do so now. I haven't prepared the required ingredients. Why would I, a Nighthawk of the Evernight Goddess, carry ingredients from the Sun domain?

Klein massaged his forehead regretfully. When he saw no further disturbances in the world above the gray fog, he finally relaxed, certain that the Eternal Blazing Sun had not managed to track him down.

Do not look directly at God. Do not look directly at a high-level entity. I must remember that!

Why would the Eternal Blazing Sun be so hostile toward the Lord of Storms and the God of Knowledge and Wisdom?

And what the hell is a White Angel?

As these jumbled thoughts filled his mind, Klein felt a hollow, aching pain in his head. Moreover, he sensed that too much time had passed. He had to return to the outside world before someone discovered something amiss.

He had initially assumed that performing two or three divinations in the mysterious space would take only a minute or so. In addition, a wall of spirituality isolated him from his surroundings, and his body in the world above the gray fog would sense if anything touched it. He had therefore believed himself entirely safe. He had not considered the possibility of an accident. In the end, he had nearly lost his life, wasting a considerable amount of time in the process.

Afraid that he might return only to be struck by a beam of Light of Purification, or that he would discover the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem had been damaged, he enveloped himself in spirituality and initiated his descent with his heart in his throat.

Crimson moonlight reflected in his darkened eyes. Klein saw the sparse forest and the weeds before him, as well as the intact Sealed Artifact 3-0782 in his hands.

Only after several breathless seconds did he finally believe he was safe.

Phew...

Klein exhaled in relief, exhausted by his mad exploration along the boundary between life and death.

Chapter 141: Nightmare

Exhausted, Klein dispelled the sealed wall of spirituality and allowed the cool breeze to wash over his face. The scent of grass and trees it carried revitalized him.

He rubbed the warm, antiquated Sealed Artifact 3-0782 with his hands and sighed inwardly.

Who would have thought there would be a drop of a god's blood in this emblem? I have to assume the experts from the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun searched for it in the past but failed to find it...

Klein stretched his neck. Not daring to attempt anything else, he tucked the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem into the inner pocket of his trench coat.

Pulling on the chain with one hand, he drew out his silver pocket watch, its case engraved with vine leaves. He opened it and saw that there was still about an hour before Corpse Collector Frye's shift.

I need two matches to prop my eyelids open... This is a side effect of that near-death experience! Unable to think of any other solution, Klein took a small metal bottle from a tiny hidden pocket. He uncapped it and held it beneath his nose.

A pungent odor, a mixture of mint and disinfectant, rushed into his nostrils and sent goosebumps across his skin. The shock jolted his senses awake, temporarily driving away his fatigue.

He had learned the formula from Corpse Collector Frye. It was called Quelaag's Oil, and it could help someone ignore the stench of rotting corpses while also refreshing and clearing the mind.

The next hour was torture. Klein paced back and forth from time to time and was bitten several times by mosquitoes in the forest.

At last, he saw the black-haired, blue-eyed Frye emerge from the town in a trench coat, a cane in hand.

Although Frye still looked like a living corpse, Klein felt as though he were looking at his savior. He covered his mouth and yawned, bringing tears to his eyes, then walked over and took Sealed Artifact 3-0782 from his pocket.

"What happened?" Frye asked, looking at his partner's pale face.

Klein sighed. "I was on duty at Chanis Gate the night before last and didn't sleep well yesterday morning, so I'm very tired."

He offered no further explanation and changed the subject. "Should I return for my next shift four hours from now?"

"Seven hours. The Captain doesn't need to sleep tonight." Frye accepted the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem.

I'm glad someone enjoys staying up late... Klein silently mocked the Captain. He bade Frye farewell and walked toward the town.

On his way back to the inn, he took out his pocket watch again and checked the time.

Hmm, ten minutes earlier than we agreed... What a nice person... Klein chuckled and quickened his pace. Upon returning to the inn, he pushed open the half-closed door. The proprietor watched him make his way upstairs and enter his room.

After locking the door, he removed his coat and shoes. Without even washing up, he collapsed directly onto the bed.

Within seconds, his breathing grew heavy, then long and peaceful.

In his dreams, Klein returned to Earth, where he was playing a game he had yet to beat. To his left sat a cup of soda and a plate of spicy chicken wings. To his right was a bowl of rice and a soup of sliced pork and bitter bamboo shoots.

He did not like bitter bamboo shoots on their own, but he loved them in soup with slices of meat. Their refreshing flavor and the slight richness of the pork were tantalizing and complemented the rice perfectly.

With a good dipping sauce on the side, he could eat an extra bowl of rice!

Just as Klein was about to enjoy his supper and continue playing, his dream changed again, revealing the interior of 2 Daffodil Street.

Klein abruptly became alert, aware that he was dreaming.

He saw himself seated at the dining table with a copy of the Tingen Daily Tribune in hand. Before him were a bowl of tomato oxtail stew, pan-fried lamb chops, mashed potatoes, and wheat bread.

He subconsciously turned toward the door and suddenly noticed a figure standing outside the living room window, silently staring into the house!

Klein was startled. He immediately recognized the gray-eyed Dunn, half his face pressed close to the window as he silently watched the people inside.

...Captain, could you please not frighten people in their dreams? Is this how you act as a Nightmare? Klein thought, finding humor amid his exasperation. He spooned up a mouthful of stew and put it into his mouth.

Ah, this is my cooking! he marveled inwardly. He now understood why he had abruptly become lucid in the dream and why the scene of him on Earth had vanished.

He would naturally become aware when someone barged into his dreams!

At that moment, Dunn left his place beside the oriel window and entered the house directly. Dressed in his black trench coat, he silently approached Klein.

He removed his hat and nodded before sitting down. Without standing on ceremony, he picked up the cutlery and swiftly polished off the stew, lamb chops, mashed potatoes, and wheat bread on the table.

Klein stared in bewilderment, unable to fathom what the Captain was doing.

Phew! Dunn exhaled in satisfaction and gave Klein a thumbs-up. He then took out his pipe and a match, lit it, and drew an intoxicated puff.

After exhaling a cloud of smoke, he rose, put on his hat, and bowed before leaving both the house and the dream.

"..."

Klein watched the Captain's retreating back, unable to collect himself for a long while.

He looked down at the empty plates and instinctively tried to conjure the food again.

But this time, the oxtail stew, lamb chops, and mashed potatoes did not reappear in his dream.

They were completely eaten? A Nightmare can do that? Klein's lips twitched in frustration. So the Captain's goal was to stop me from eating supper in my dream? That truly is a nightmare... What a creative way to act as a Nightmare...

He chuckled, slipped out of the dream, and once again fell into a deep sleep.

At about half past five the next morning, Klein had no choice but to wake early, drink his coffee, and eat his toast and bacon. He then hurried out of town to relieve Dunn.

At seven in the morning, they prepared to set off for Tingen.

It was not even ten when they arrived at 36 Zouteland Street. After Dunn, the most energetic of the group, returned Sealed Artifact 3-0782 to the area behind Chanis Gate, Frye sat down at the typewriter. Taking advantage of the clerks' absence, he began writing the mission report and listing the related expenses for reimbursement.

Klein watched from beside him, pleased to see that all the items he had expended were included on the list--even the materials he had used to repel insects and mosquitoes.

He did not return home immediately because, through a coded letter, he had arranged to meet Doctor Daxter from the asylum at one in the afternoon at their agreed location.

Then there's still the Tarot Gathering at three... Why is the leader of a secret society condemned to such an exhausting life? Klein thought. He took a two-hour nap in the Nighthawks' break room to catch up on sleep.

He had not forgotten the information he obtained the previous day. He was not worried about forgetting it, since he could recall the information through divination. What he feared was overlooking its very existence and thereby losing even the ability to divine it. Thus, before going to sleep, he reviewed each piece of information once more to reinforce his memory.

This was also why Klein insisted on reviewing and reorganizing everything he knew each week.

After lunch, he checked his pocket watch and left the Blackthorn Security Company for the Shooting Club at 3 Zouteland Street.

After pushing open the door, Klein entered the reception area, but he did not go directly to the shooting range reserved for the Nighthawks. Instead, he found a seat in the hall and waited patiently with his black cane in hand.

He had arranged to meet Daxter at the Zouteland Street Shooting Club!

They made their arrangements through handwritten letters. Whenever Klein needed to meet with him, he would write to Doctor Daxter Guderian under the guise of a patient's relative and inquire about an unusual condition called "dissociative identity disorder." In the letter, Klein would find various ways to mention the word Spectator and include a concealed ink mark to authenticate his identity. He would also casually suggest a meeting time.

As for the meeting place, they had decided upon it during their first encounter. If Klein felt it necessary to change the location, he would mention it when they next met in person.

When Daxter Guderian needed to meet regarding a nonurgent matter, he could send a letter to the Hound Pub or the Shooting Club. It would be addressed to Mr. Hornacis, and Klein would collect it at scheduled intervals.

In an emergency, Daxter could hand the letter directly to Wright, the proprietor of the Hound Pub, and mention that he was "looking for mercenaries." Wright, an associate of the Nighthawks, would then immediately deliver the letter to the Blackthorn Security Company.

After waiting a while, Klein saw the refined Daxter enter the Shooting Club a few minutes past one.

He wore a black hat and a fitted tuxedo. In his hands was a cane inlaid with silver, and a pair of gold-rimmed spectacles rested upon his face.

Daxter strolled around the club without attracting attention and spotted Klein, who gave him a slight nod. Daxter immediately looked away, walked to the counter, and expertly applied for a shooting range and rented a gun.

This was not his first visit.

"Small shooting range number 7, three soli an hour. A revolver costs one soli and seven pence per hour to rent and comes with six rounds," the receptionist said, swiftly processing his request.

After confirming that he wanted the facilities for an hour and paying the ten-soli fee, Daxter took the revolver and extra ammunition and was led to the designated shooting range by an attendant.

Klein waited another five minutes before slowly rising. Gripping his cane, he walked to small shooting range number 7 and knocked on the door.

The door opened a tiny crack with a creak. Daxter first peered cautiously around before opening it fully.

Klein immediately stepped inside and locked the door.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Daxter." He took out a ten-soli note and handed it to Daxter. "We would never make our associates bear additional expenses."

Because I can claim reimbursement... he added inwardly.

Daxter did not decline. He accepted the money and asked gravely, "Mr. Moretti, why did you ask to meet me?"

Chapter 142: Association

Klein obviously could not ask for the Telepathist formula right at the beginning. Nor did he conceal his intentions. After all, the man across from him was a Spectator and would not be deceived so easily.

"Has Hood Eugen behaved unusually of late?" he began, asking Daxter Guderian about the asylum patient who belonged to the Psychology Alchemists.
