Daxter studied Klein's eyes, expression, and gestures. After considering the question, he said, "No, he has been behaving normally. Frankly, I believe that if he truly wished to leave the asylum, he could immediately present himself as perfectly healthy and normal. But he hasn't done so. He remains in the asylum and seems to be treating every patient there. Yes, the conditions of patients who display chaotic, violent, or abnormal thoughts appear to be improving. Perhaps Hood Eugen is using this method to train his Beyonder powers."

Psychiatrist, the Sequence 7 corresponding to Spectator? Perhaps even higher... Since Hood Eugen entered the asylum as a patient rather than a doctor, that means he hasn't truly grasped the acting method. It's probably as Daxter surmised: Hood Eugen is training his Beyonder abilities, and doing so resembles the "acting method." To a certain extent, that could slow the potion's negative influence. Hence, Hood Eugen has simply made the asylum his home... Klein openly displayed his deep contemplation of Hood Eugen's situation.

That would make Daxter Guderian feel that Klein knew and understood a great deal, making him appear unfathomable.

With this in mind, Klein inferred something else: the Psychology Alchemists had not grasped the "acting method." After all, even a mainstay member at Sequence 7 was unaware of it. In this era, when Beyonders were few, Sequence 7 was considered mid-level in any secret organization. Such members were important enough to know crucial secrets, especially those that could help them resist losing control.

Furthermore, the Psychology Alchemists was a secret organization established only within the past three hundred years or so. It was understandable that they had neither grasped nor deduced the "acting method." The only organization to mention the method explicitly was the Secret Order, an ancient organization with more than fifteen hundred years of history, one that could be traced back to the previous Epoch!

Wait, the Church of the Goddess is even older than the Secret Order. The Letters of the Saints in The Revelation of Evernight alone clearly indicate that it is nearly three thousand years old, to say nothing of the myths and legends predating them... How could such an ancient church have failed to discover the "acting method"?

Over the long history of such a vast organization, there must have been members who experimented with various possibilities, just as Spirit Medium Daly had. They might not have understood the principles of the acting method in detail, but they would nevertheless have acted in accordance with the name of the potion. The positive feedback they received would have allowed them to grasp the essence of the method. As such experiences accumulated across generations of Nighthawks, unless the higher-ups were all curly-haired baboons, it would have been impossible for them not to deduce the "acting method"!

As Klein connected these thoughts, he was suddenly shocked.

To Nighthawks who knew nothing of the "acting method," someone like Spirit Medium Daly was a genius, an example no ordinary member could emulate. Therefore, no one suspected that the experiences of Daly and others could be adapted for their own use.

But to anyone who had grasped the "acting method," this was extremely strange!

Klein believed that throughout the long history of the Church of Evernight, Spirit Medium Daly was certainly not the first member to use the "acting method" to digest Low-Sequence potions rapidly. She might not even rank among the first ten or fifty!

It doesn't make sense. Unless Daly didn't discover the "acting method" by herself but received guidance from someone else... In that case, one could conclude that every member of the Holy Cathedral rigidly adheres to old beliefs, trusting the experiences of their predecessors and not daring to defy their teachings. After all, rebellion would generally mean losing control... Yes, besides that explanation, there's another possibility. For some reason, the Church's higher-ups have deliberately concealed the "acting method"...

I need to search through the records for examples of Beyonders in the Church of Evernight who rapidly digested their potions, as well as what ultimately became of them... Klein thought, his expression solemn.

Daxter watched him, waited for several minutes, then asked curiously, "Officer, is there some problem with Hood Eugen's actions?"

"Not at present. They merely reminded me of other matters," Klein replied with a smile, setting aside his suspicions for the moment.

He asked instead, "Have the Psychology Alchemists taken any action recently?"

"No, apart from a small gathering in Awwa to exchange items and experiences," Daxter answered honestly.

Klein nodded slightly. "What about your own condition?"

Daxter controlled his expression as he replied, "Not very good. I still hear ravings and suffer hallucinations. If I weren't a doctor specializing in mental health, I might even believe I had some kind of disorder."

As he spoke, his face grew solemn. "I followed Hood Eugen's and your instructions and ignored the hallucinations and ravings. That made me feel much better, but they still disturb my sleep, and I've become more irritable and short-tempered. I don't feel like myself. It's as though another self is growing within me, or perhaps it could be described as a new personality. I'm deeply worried and terrified that I might suddenly lose control one day."

Just as I predicted. I didn't even need divination to see that coming... Having prepared for this, Klein smiled and said, "You needn't worry. You are now an auxiliary member of the Nighthawks, and that comes with certain benefits. As an ancient organization, we possess many methods for preventing the loss of control. They are not entirely effective, but they will certainly help you.

"Furthermore, I'm willing to share my personal experience with you. You should know that the man standing before you needed only one month to cast off the shackles of hallucinations and ravings, and they have never returned. From Hood Eugen and your other cadres, you should understand how difficult that is."

For the sake of the Sequence 8 Telepathist formula, Klein bragged a little.

"Officer, there was a small lie in what you said, but most of it was true," Daxter suddenly said in a calm voice. "What do you want from me?"

It's difficult to lie to a Spectator... Klein replied with a smile, "It isn't something only I want."

Miss Justice wants it as well.

Of course, he knew Daxter would assume the entire Nighthawks team wanted something.

"If your method is truly effective, and the item or information you seek is within my ability to obtain..." Daxter said, carefully weighing his words.

"I will give you the benefit in advance," Klein said frankly. "We want the Telepathist formula."

He had no intention of concealing the potion formula from the Captain. He would tell Dunn that Daxter had used it in exchange for Klein's personal experience in controlling his potion.

During the process, Klein would naturally verify the formula and "accidentally" commit it to memory.

Moreover, by trading his personal experience for the formula, he would earn merit with the Nighthawks.

By then, combined with his previous contributions, he might not need to make any additional effort before applying for the Clown formula and its main ingredients.

One formula, two deals. What a bargain... Klein thought happily.

Daxter looked into his eyes and remained silent for a moment before saying, "You're very frank... I'll do my best to obtain the formula, but I can't be certain how long it will take. If it becomes too dangerous, I hope I can substitute something else."

"No problem." Klein had no intention of forcing the request upon him. He then gave Daxter a vague explanation of the "acting method." "The key to resisting the loss of control lies within the potion's name. We must comprehend it and understand its true meaning. But thought alone cannot provide complete understanding; it must be gained through experience. For example, as a Spectator, you must understand that you are only a spectator, not an actor. How a Spectator should act is something you must discover through repeated attempts and experimentation, gradually deducing the principles you are required to follow. Once you have done so, adhere to them strictly."

Daxter listened attentively, then replied, "That is an entirely new way of looking at the matter. Heh, I'm willing to use the word 'theory' to describe what you just said. It's like a theory of theater and opera... I'll try it, and I hope it helps.

"If--if it truly works, I'll do everything I can to obtain the Telepathist formula for you!"

"May the Goddess protect you." Klein drew a crimson moon across his chest.

Klein did not also request the Psychiatrist potion formula, because he knew that was a task Daxter could not accomplish from his current position. If Klein was not careful, the request might expose him.

Therefore, he planned to proceed one step at a time, gradually helping Daxter rise within the Psychology Alchemists.

In time, the rewards would be abundant.

Klein looked outside through the peephole in the door, then quickly left and headed for the small shooting range reserved for the Nighthawks.

After entering and locking the door, his expression grew grave once more. While speculating why the Church of the Goddess had not developed the "acting method," he had realized something else he had overlooked!

He had missed it because he learned two crucial facts in the reverse order, preventing him from considering their connection more deeply.

The first was that the Antigonus family had been destroyed by the Church of Evernight.

The second was that the Antigonus family possessed the Seer pathway, or at least most of it.

Because a long time had passed between his learning those two facts, he had nearly failed to connect them. As a result, he had overlooked something that should have been obvious.

Since the Antigonus family possessed most of the Sequences of the Seer pathway, how could the Church of Evernight have obtained only Sequence 9 Seer?

They should have acquired far more as spoils of war!

If a member of the Aurora Order could obtain the Clown formula from the Antigonus family's magical notebook, then what about the Church that had destroyed the entire family?

Even if the Antigonus family had been well prepared and hidden their most valuable possessions atop the highest peak of the Hornacis mountain range, the Church of Evernight should not have gained so little. They were the ones who killed the members of the Antigonus family. Furthermore, the dead could be made to speak!

Chapter 143: The Fool's Real-Time Translator

Klein paced around the small shooting range, pondering the Church of Evernight's intentions regarding the Seer pathway.

Do they not want Nighthawks to choose this pathway, or do they not want Beyonders to grow powerful through it? Is that why they revealed only Sequence 9 Seer, which is clearly a support type? The Captain also mentioned that the Holy Cathedral might possess the subsequent formulas...

No, the confidential information I read didn't even provide the names of the Sequence 8 and Sequence 7 potions. It merely described the combat characteristics of each Sequence... In other words, they don't want their subordinates to realize that the Church might possess the actual formulas.

Is it possible that Nighthawks who choose this pathway might become "vengeful spirits" of the Antigonus family, prompting the Church's higher-ups to make such a decision? Or could there be some other reason?

Klein suddenly felt deeply suspicious of the Church's higher-ups, and an intense sense of wariness and vigilance arose within him. He began to reconsider whether he should openly submit a special application to become a Clown.

If some terrifying secret lies behind all this, wouldn't I be throwing myself into the fire? Frankly, I'm not someone who can withstand rigorous scrutiny...

But the Tingen branch has already handed the Clown potion formula over to the Church. It would be perfectly normal for any Seer who learned of this to hope to advance, wouldn't it? Sequence 8 is still considered a Low Sequence, so it shouldn't attract too much attention...

The only problem is that I would have completely digested the potion in just one month before submitting a special application. If the higher-ups are familiar with the "acting method," they'll immediately realize what I've done... Of course, I do have an excuse. I know Spirit Medium Daly, after all, and Old Neil, who strictly abides by the Mystery Pryer's maxim, is also my friend. It wouldn't be too difficult to believe that they inspired me to refine the "acting method."

Yes, even Daly attracted the higher-ups' attention only after showing signs of digesting a Sequence 7 potion within three years, and now she's being cultivated as a future Archbishop. At the Clown stage, I shouldn't draw too much attention--unless I completely digest the Clown potion within a few months, confirming their suspicions that I've truly mastered the "acting method"...

In other words, applying for the Clown potion isn't too risky. I can continue with that plan, but I'll have to be careful in the future. Sigh, I'll take things one step at a time. I'll perform a divination when I get home.

Klein collected himself, drew the revolver from his holster, and continued his daily shooting practice and maintenance.

The revolver he had received from his schoolmate Welch was of unexpectedly good quality. Barring any accidents, it would last for quite some time. Of course, he also had Dunn and Leonard to thank for teaching him how to maintain it.

To be honest, even if it's damaged, it doesn't matter. I can claim compensation for all of this. Klein looked at the target, holstered his revolver, and left the Shooting Club.

He took public transportation back to 2 Daffodil Street. Before reaching his destination, he spotted a young lady pacing in front of his door.

She wore a blue lace dress and a hat with a thin veil. It was Melissa's classmate Elizabeth, her face still graced with adorable baby fat.

As soon as she saw Klein, she hurried over and removed her hat, revealing a delighted expression.

She paused for two seconds before smiling.

"Good afternoon, Mr. Moretti. I'm guessing you just returned from Lamud Town, right?"

I'm sorry, but I came back this morning... Klein smiled.

"No, I've come from Zouteland Street."

Yes, that was a very honest answer... he silently joked.

Elizabeth froze for a moment, then said excitedly, "All right, I guessed wrong. I came looking for you because I wanted to tell you that I didn't have that nightmare last night. I no longer dreamed of the knight in black armor! It was exactly as your divination predicted!"

Of course. That wraith was completely purified by Sealed Artifact 3-0782. I couldn't channel his spirit even if I were there, much less have him appear in your dreams... Klein chuckled and replied gently, "I'm glad you're free of your troubles. I'm also very satisfied with yesterday's divination."

"Thank you. Thank you again! All right, I have to go now. I still have lessons this afternoon. Bye-bye, Mr. Moretti. I'll visit Melissa when I have time." Elizabeth left cheerfully and hired a carriage by the roadside.

As the carriage began rolling away, she smiled and thought proudly, Melissa definitely doesn't know how amazing her brother is...

It seems my explanation just now was useless. Young ladies would rather trust their intuition and the truths they've invented in their minds... Klein watched Elizabeth board the carriage, then opened the door to his house and went to his room.

After resting for a while, he began organizing everything that had happened over the past week, including the questions he had yet to resolve.

Once he finished, he burned his notes, took out his pocket watch, and flipped it open.

"Half past two? Another fifteen minutes..." Seeing that he still had time, Klein changed into his oldest suit and went to Smyrin Bakery on Iron Cross Street to buy a cup of sweet iced tea from Mrs. Wendy.

He drank it on his way back, then sealed his room with a wall of spirituality at a quarter to three. He took four steps counterclockwise and entered the world above the gray fog.

In the silent, ancient palace, Klein conjured a piece of goatskin and wrote down a divination statement: "I should obtain the Clown potion through the Nighthawks."

He set down his pen and unwound the spirit pendulum from his wrist. Holding its chain firmly in his left hand, he suspended the topaz directly above the paper.

He recited the statement seven times. His eyes darkened, and the pendulum began to rotate clockwise.

That's a positive answer, so it's an appropriate choice. But the Sequences after Clown are another matter. I need to develop my Tarot Club in earnest... Klein performed another divination to confirm the answer.

Afterward, he reached out and pressed the dark-red star representing The Sun.

He wanted to summon the youth from the City of Silver ahead of time and ask whether he had revealed what had happened in this world to the six-member council. If he hadn't, Klein would give him a better way to learn when the gatherings would begin.

In a room belonging to the Berg family in the City of Silver, Derrick sat silently beside his bed, awaiting The Fool's summons.

To avoid being near anyone, he hadn't left the house since "returning." He had nearly finished all the food in his room.

Enduring his hunger and the growling of his stomach, Derrick felt like a living corpse wandering across a dark plain. Nevertheless, he remained silent and did not rise.

At that moment, he saw a dark-red glow spread through the air and swiftly engulf him.

The boundless, cold, and desolate gray world once again filled his vision. At the seat of honor, The Fool, obscured by thick fog, appeared before him once more.

Klein was satisfied that his "summoning" had not been interrupted. He also confirmed that he faced no immediate danger.

"Sun, we meet again," he said with a smile in Jotun.

Derrick was stunned by what had happened. He lowered his head.

"You truly are a Fool who keeps His word."

"The other members will arrive shortly. Before then, I need to confirm a few things with you." This time, Klein spoke in Loen, willing the mysterious space to translate his words into Jotun.

The words echoed through the air and reached Derrick in Jotun. He asked curiously, "What is it?"

Now that I've attained a certain degree of mastery over Jotun, this mysterious space above the gray fog can translate everything I say in real time. That means I won't have to worry about Justice and The Hanged Man being unable to understand The Sun... Sigh, why does a boss like me have to work so tirelessly? Klein pinched the bridge of his nose, chuckled, and shook his head.

"I will permit you to recite my name. Remember the incantation I am about to teach you.

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era;

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog;

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck."

Derrick's pupils contracted when he heard this, but he didn't dare allow himself to become distracted. He recited the words over and over in his mind, then confirmed them with The Fool.

"You must perform a simple ritual and recite my name when you return to the City of Silver... In the future, I will notify you in advance of each gathering. On other days, you need not pay it much attention or avoid anyone. When you receive my notification, isolate yourself within a thousand heartbeats." Klein explained the method he had deliberated over for quite some time.

This was essentially a response to a prayer.

Given the City of Silver's circumstances, and to save time, Klein omitted the other steps of the ritual since the plea was directed toward him.

"A thousand heartbeats?" Derrick muttered.

Klein gave Derrick a general explanation of the Tarot Club, then took out his pocket watch and checked the time.

Derrick froze for a moment, instinctively staring at the wondrous object.

As three o'clock approached, Klein extended his hand and pressed the dark-red stars representing Justice and The Hanged Man.

Derrick watched without blinking. Light erupted across from and beside him, and two hazy figures emerged within it.

Audrey Hall surveyed her surroundings and abruptly froze. Then she heard Mr. Fool's eternally calm voice.

"This is our newest member. His code name is The Sun.

"This is Miss Justice, and that is Mr. Hanged Man."

Our newest member? Audrey was initially astonished, but her surprise immediately turned to joy.

She was thrilled to see the Tarot Club growing. It made her feel like the protagonist of a story.

Alger, The Hanged Man, furrowed his brow, somewhat displeased that The Fool had brought in a new member so suddenly.

He should have at least mentioned it to us... But a great figure like Mr. Fool has no need to consider our feelings... he thought in exasperation before offering simple greetings to Justice and The Sun.

During this brief exchange, Audrey entered her Spectator state and closely observed the newest member, The Sun.

He must be quite young... His body language shows that he's a little nervous and reserved... But on the whole, he maintains a passable air of silence. He gives the impression of, hmm, a lone wolf. Yes, a lone wolf... Audrey thought as she turned her gaze toward The Fool, seated at the head of the long bronze table.

She said delightedly, "Mr. Fool, I've collected another two pages of Emperor Roselle's diary."

Chapter 144: Three-Way Deal

There were actually three pages, but the characters were too complicated and difficult to memorize. A little over two pages is my limit. If I tried to memorize more, I'd make mistakes. The rest will have to wait until the next gathering... Audrey added inwardly.

New pages of Roselle's diary? Klein's interest was immediately piqued. Smiling, he asked a question to which he already knew the answer. "Miss Justice, what do you require?"

Audrey's eyes shone with excitement, but she replied with restraint, "You know that I'll soon completely digest the Spectator potion. I hope to obtain the Telepathist potion formula so that I can prepare the ingredients in advance. Hmm, I know that two pages of the diary don't contain much and might not equal the value of the Telepathist formula, so I'll give you another page. Hmm, I'll also pay you a sum of money..."

Before she could finish, she suddenly realized how wrong that sounded. She couldn't help berating herself inwardly. Mr. Fool is at the very least a great figure approaching the level of a god. How could He be bought with money!?

Unable to maintain her Spectator state, Audrey hurriedly added with a stammer, "That's not what I meant! Mr. Fool, I meant that you can decide what compensation you would like. Yes, that's what I meant!"

I preferred your first suggestion... I could answer like this: Once you have completely digested the Spectator potion, you will receive the formula you need. I have a subordinate--no, I should use the word "Blessed." That sounds more impressive. He happens to be handling certain matters that require money, and this is his anonymous account at Backlund Bank... Yes, then I'll disguise myself and open an anonymous account at Backlund Bank. Klein didn't answer immediately. With an inscrutable expression, he carefully considered his words.

Backlund Bank was one of the seven major banks in the Loen Kingdom and, as such, had the right to clear transactions.

The Loen Kingdom used settlement receipts to centrally handle transfers between banks within the same city. However, unlike the banks in the Intis Republic, not every bank belonged to the same association. The seven largest banks retained the right to clear transactions and were therefore known as clearing banks, upon which the other banks depended.

Transfers between different cities, on the other hand, could only be made within the same bank and were completed by balancing the accounts of its branches. With the invention of the steam locomotive and the telegram, the efficiency of such transfers had increased dramatically.

Just then, Derrick Berg, The Sun, suddenly spoke.

"The Telepathist potion formula? The Telepathist that is followed by Psyche Analyst?"

Audrey looked at him in puzzlement. "You know it?"

At the same time, Miss Justice's instincts as a Spectator alerted her to something peculiar.

The young man had used the ancient title "Psyche Analyst" rather than the modern term "Psychiatrist"!

This fellow is very strange... Audrey scrutinized The Sun's every movement.

Derrick didn't think he had behaved unusually. He replied earnestly, "I can obtain the formula for you!"

Then, feeling guilty that he couldn't provide it immediately, he did his best to explain. "It belongs to a Sequence pathway originating from the Dragon race. Our City of Silver was once ruled by the Giant King's imperial household. As you know, Giants and Dragons are sworn enemies. Therefore, the City of Silver possesses all the formulas for Sequences 9, 8, and 7. I have ways of obtaining them."

This kid... I already warned him not to speak carelessly or expose his origins. And yet... Klein nearly raised a hand to cover his face.

Sigh. Although The Sun appears to be in great pain and is unusually mature and taciturn for his age, he's still just a boy! Still, this does clarify something for me... So the Spectator pathway originated from the Dragon race. No wonder the constellation behind Miss Justice's high-backed chair forms a Dragon... The City of Silver has preserved its history remarkably well... Klein remained leaning against the back of his chair as he listened thoughtfully to The Sun's explanation.

In fact, he could easily have stopped The Sun from revealing those matters. If he hadn't provided simultaneous translation, Justice and The Hanged Man wouldn't have understood him at all.

However, Klein chose a different approach. He felt that this might effectively reinforce his powerful and mysterious image in the minds of all three members. Therefore, he listened with a smile and said nothing.

The Giant King, the Dragon race, the City of Silver... Audrey was bewildered. She first looked at The Hanged Man across from her, but his body language revealed that he was equally shocked and confused.

She turned toward the seat of honor at the long bronze table. The Fool sat in His high-backed chair, shrouded in thick grayish-white fog. His right arm rested on the armrest as He leaned casually to one side. He displayed no shock, no curiosity, no contemplation, and no doubt. He merely watched them with a smile.

He knows... He already knew all of this... Audrey and Alger reached the same firm conclusion almost simultaneously.

"The City of Silver... I've never heard of such a place. Where is it?" Audrey probed as Alger listened attentively.

At that very moment, Derrick Berg's mind was also filled with questions. He could tell that, apart from the godlike Fool, Justice and The Hanged Man were some kind of Beyonders.

In the Forsaken Land of the Gods, Derrick had never seen another living human outside the City of Silver.

Therefore, he asked in return, "If you aren't residents of the City of Silver, which city-state are you from?"

Sigh... Klein once again felt the urge to sigh.

Audrey's lips trembled, and she was momentarily at a loss for words.

Yes, the implication behind his question is that if you don't wish to answer the same question, you shouldn't pry into where someone else lives... Miss Justice nodded faintly and elegantly fell silent.

Alger had clearly misunderstood The Sun's intentions as well. He had no idea that the other party was asking a perfectly straightforward question, so he too remained silent.

When Derrick received no answer, he seemed to realize what was happening. He didn't pursue the subject and instead said, "I will try to obtain the Telepathist potion formula as soon as possible. I would like to exchange it for the first Sequence of the Sun pathway."

"The Sun pathway? Sequence 9 Bard?" Alger asked.

Derrick considered this and said, "Probably, but I don't have enough information about it."

Watching from the sidelines, Klein decided to intervene. He had no intention of letting anyone steal his business.

He smiled and said, "I believe Miss Justice doesn't possess the Bard formula."

But Mr. Hanged Man seems capable of obtaining it...

Seeing Audrey nod, Klein continued with a faint smile, "I will give The Sun the Bard formula. The Sun will provide Miss Justice with the Telepathist potion formula as soon as possible. Try to complete this within the next two gatherings. Miss Justice, give me the new pages of Roselle's diary, and the deal will be concluded.

"Yes, according to the law of equivalent exchange, The Sun will be at a disadvantage in this transaction. However, for now, he has made only a promise. Once he actually provides the Telepathist formula, Miss Justice can consider how to compensate him further. Alternatively, I can compensate him while Miss Justice provides money to one of my Blessed, who happens to need it for certain matters. Heh heh, after all, The Sun might not be able to receive Miss Justice's cash or ingredients as compensation."

Klein deliberately added that final sentence to shift the attention of The Hanged Man and Justice toward the fact that The Sun might be unable to receive her compensation. He also used it to place himself in an inscrutable position, ensuring that everyone overlooked the Blessed who lacked money.

Might not be able to receive the compensation... Where exactly is The Sun? The Southern Continent? Alger's brow furrowed.

The Sun's origins are mysterious as well... As expected, Mr. Fool really does have subordinates in the real world. Audrey finally saw hope of becoming a Sequence 8 Telepathist. What objection could she possibly have? Suppressing her excitement, she gave a faint smile and said, "I have no objections."

"Neither do I." Upon learning that he could obtain the first Sequence of the Sun pathway, Derrick nodded without hesitation. He didn't care in the least about the additional compensation.

Alger, who was excluded from the three-way deal, had no right to speak. Although he could obtain the Bard formula, he too would need a week or two.

At that moment, Klein, who had successfully postponed the matter of compensation until the next gathering or the one after that, happily pressed his palm forward. The Bard formula materialized.

"Main ingredients: one Crystal Sunflower, or the tail feather of an adult Flint Bird, or the tail feather of a Fire Bird... One piece of Siren Rock, or one Singing Sunflower...

"Supplementary ingredients: one blade of Midsummer Grass, five drops of July Wine Juice, one blade of Elf Dark Leaf..."

He sent the formula before The Sun and watched the young man first furrow his brow, then relax.

Yes, the ingredients in the Forsaken Land of the Gods would still be known by their ancient names. Fortunately, I obtained my formula directly from the Eternal Blazing Sun. The knowledge I received used ancient names and included various substitutes... Realization dawned on Klein, and he suddenly turned his gaze toward Miss Justice.

Audrey glanced at The Sun, who was memorizing the formula, then quickly willed the two pages of the diary she had memorized into existence.

The diary entries immediately appeared on yellowish-brown goatskin and, with a flash, arrived in Klein's hands.

As before, he began reading at once.

"November 3rd. Matilda is now three months pregnant. I even find those maidservants from the countryside beautiful. No, I cannot lower my standards. Coincidentally, Countess Florais has invited me to a private party. Hehe.

"November 8th. Archbishop Fan Estin asked for my help. Huh, what could I possibly do for an archbishop?

"November 9th. It turns out that the Sequence pathways contain a hidden secret. Archbishop Estin told me that after becoming a Sequence 5 Beyonder, the subsequent Sequences can be replaced by Sequences of the same level from one or two other pathways! In other words, this applies from Mid Sequence to High Sequence! However, it is limited to those one or two pathways. If someone substitutes a potion from the wrong pathway, partial insanity would be the mildest consequence, and they would never be able to advance again.

"In this way, one can begin switching pathways from Sequence 4 onward. The Sleepless and Corpse Collector pathways are interchangeable. Yes, the Church's Savant and Mystery Pryer pathways can also be substituted for one another at High Sequences."

Chapter 145: Request for Cooperation

Some pathways are interchangeable after Sequence 5? That's different from what the Nighthawks told me!

Isn't it an established fact that you cannot change pathways after choosing one? Wasn't it said that deviating from one's pathway might grant strange and mysterious powers, but that the person would inevitably go insane and never be able to advance again?

To think there are hidden exceptions!

As Klein stared at the diary, his pupils contracted.

He didn't believe Emperor Roselle would spout nonsense about something like this. After all, the surprise in his words seemed entirely genuine. However, Klein didn't assume the information Roselle had received was necessarily correct. It was also possible that he had been deceived or had misunderstood it.

I'll need to verify this. For now, I'll commit it to memory... Klein reminded himself before contemplating the matter more deeply.

If Roselle's description is correct, then the Sequence pathways run far deeper than I imagined... They conceal many secrets...

The complete pathway in the Nighthawks' possession is the Sleepless pathway. They also possess a relatively complete Corpse Collector pathway, with formulas up to Sequence 4. To think the two become interchangeable after Sequence 5... Their other potion chains are even less complete, with some pathways possessing only the first Sequence...

Similarly, the Church of the God of Steam and Machinery holds the complete Savant pathway and a relatively complete Mystery Pryer pathway. These can also be interchanged at High Sequences...

Interesting... I wonder which pathway is interchangeable with the Seer pathway. The Apprentice or Marauder pathway mentioned by the Emperor?

Hmm, there's a strong possibility that each of the first five Sequences of the Seer pathway grants a distinct ability and that those abilities are combined at Sequence 4. At that stage, there should be no way to switch to another potion... Klein reined in his thoughts and returned his attention to the diary.

He noticed that although the two diary pages were connected, their contents were not in chronological order. The dates belonged to two different periods. This might have been a mistake by whoever copied them.

"April 9th. Relations among the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun, the Church of Storms, and the Church of the God of Knowledge and Wisdom are poor. They regard one another as enemies. The Church of Evernight is at odds with the Church of the God of Combat in the Feysac Empire. This can be exploited. These facts are all worth considering.

"April 13th. I attended a gathering held by an ancient organization. I never expected them to be members of this organization as well. It was truly frightening.

"To think that the second Blasphemy Slate is in the hands of this organization. This is the first time I've seen this legendary object!

"Indeed, it conceals an unimaginable secret. Hehe, perhaps one day I will create a Blasphemy Slate of my own. No, a whole set, each concealing an ultimate secret!"

Holy f**k, Emperor, why didn't you write down the name of this ancient organization!? You're killing me! Perhaps... perhaps Roselle had a reason not to, or didn't dare record the organization's name even in Chinese... Klein stared at the diary, feeling both frustrated and puzzled.

But with this page of the diary, Klein could finally confirm that Emperor Roselle had seen the second Blasphemy Slate. Furthermore, he had later created a set of cards, each representing a Pathway of the Divine.

Yes, they might contain the ultimate secret corresponding to each Pathway of the Divine. I wonder where that set of twenty-two cards is now. That ancient organization actually managed to obtain the second Blasphemy Slate... Klein's thoughts raced.

However, he quickly reined them in. He looked away from the diary and turned his gaze toward The Hanged Man, Justice, and The Sun. Smiling, he said, "Actually, you didn't need to wait for me."

"It is our honor," Alger replied humbly, having already suppressed his dissatisfaction.

Audrey thought for a moment before smiling.

"Mr. Fool, the open selection of government officials through examinations that you previously described has already gained the support of the King and the Prime Minister. It will soon be passed by the House of Lords and the House of Commons and is expected to be implemented early next year."

"It appears the King and the Prime Minister still know how to use their brains," Alger mocked out of habit.

Well, with Benson's intelligence and diligence, his grammar and accounting skills should be adequate by early next year... But once the proposal passes both Houses, the newspapers will certainly publicize it widely. I wonder how long Benson's advantage will last. The sooner the examination is held, the better...

Sigh, there's no way Benson can surpass the elites graduating from the various universities in such a short time. But he doesn't need to compete with them. They won't be vying for the same positions. Those people might have their sights set only on posts such as Cabinet secretary or Finance secretary... Silently worried for his brother, Klein nodded with a smile.

Seeing The Fool's approving nod, Audrey straightened her back. With a smile, she said, "Mr. Hanged Man, you previously asked me to investigate something for you. I've received an answer. The King has been persuaded by the Prime Minister and won't seek revenge against the Feysac Empire on the East Balam Shore for the time being. I believe you can now give me the additional payment you promised."

Alger considered this for a few seconds before saying, "Miss Justice, thank you for your answer. It has eased my concerns about certain matters. What additional payment do you want? I'll consider it if it is reasonable."

Audrey smiled, clearly having prepared her answer.

"Clues concerning the Psychology Alchemists, or clues to the main ingredients of the Telepathist potion. Of course, that can wait until after The Sun gives me the formula."

"No problem," Alger replied without hesitation.

Two seats away from The Hanged Man, Derrick Berg couldn't understand a single word. He was thoroughly confused. He understood a few of the terms, but couldn't connect them into anything coherent.

A method of selecting officials through examinations? A King and a Prime Minister, the House of Lords, the House of Commons, the East Balam Shore, the Feysac Empire, the Psychology Alchemists? He understood none of it.

The root of the word Feysac came from Jotun. What connection did it have to the fallen Giant King's imperial household? Derrick looked at Justice and The Hanged Man, suddenly feeling as though they might not come from the same world.

Could there be another city-state somewhere far from the City of Silver in these cursed lands, one that had even formed a nation? Derrick remained silent and listened. He now vaguely understood why the mysterious Fool had said that he might not be able to receive the monetary compensation Justice intended to give him.

To be able to gather people separated by such vast distances, disregarding the terrifying monsters hidden in the darkness of the cursed lands... The Fool might truly be a god, an ancient god... he thought.

Having accomplished everything she had intended to do, Audrey wanted to become a silent observer, but she suddenly remembered something. She hurriedly said, "I recently came into contact with a Beyonder circle and learned of a powerful individual named Mr. A. Mr. Fool, Mr. Hanged Man, Mr. Sun, do any of you know this person's background and identity?"

I don't even know what you're talking about... Derrick remained silent.

Mr. A? I only know a Mr. Z... With such similar code names, could he also belong to the Aurora Order? Klein made a conjecture but offered no answer.

He had to preserve his image and avoid answering questions he wasn't confident about. If absolutely necessary, he would give a vague response like a charlatan.

Alger looked at The Fool and found Him as calm and unchanging as ever, making His true thoughts impossible to discern. Therefore, he said after some deliberation, "The Aurora Order is hostile to the Church of Storms, the Church of the Eternal Blazing Sun, and the Church of the God of Knowledge and Wisdom. Consequently, members of those Churches understand the Aurora Order better than any other organization does. And I happen to know something about them."

You don't need to explain. I know you serve the Lord of Storms. Of course, you might be an informant... But why is there such enmity between the Aurora Order and the three ancient Churches? Klein smiled without speaking and calmly regarded The Hanged Man.

Alger knew he couldn't hide his Sequence pathway from The Fool, but he didn't pay the matter much attention and continued.

"The Aurora Order has five Saints and twenty-two Oracles. These Oracles use letters of the alphabet as code names, such as Mr. A or Mr. X. They are Beyonders, with the weakest at Sequence 7 and the strongest at Sequence 5. All of them are adept at concealing themselves. If an Oracle dies, a new Oracle takes their place.

"I cannot guarantee that the Mr. A you mentioned is the Aurora Order's Mr. A, but there is a strong possibility. As for the details of the Aurora Order, I have told you about them before."

Audrey nodded, becoming even more wary of Mr. A.

Feeling a slight pang in her heart, she said, "Thank you for your answer, Mr. Hanged Man. You don't need to pay me anything further."

"No. In exchange for the answer I just gave you and additional compensation, I would like to ask for your assistance," Alger said in a deep voice.

"What kind of assistance?" Audrey asked curiously.

Alger thought for a few seconds before saying, "I received intelligence that the pirate Qilangos, codenamed Vice Admiral Hurricane, has secretly come ashore and infiltrated Backlund. I don't know what he's planning, but I hope you can help me locate him. You need not expose yourself to danger in whatever follows."

"Vice Admiral Hurricane Qilangos? One of the seven great pirate admirals?" Audrey's eyes widened, and she nearly slipped out of her Spectator state.

What had she most wanted to do after becoming a Beyonder? Naturally, it was to come into contact with people who had previously existed only in the tales told among nobles!

"Yes. He is a Sequence 6 Beyonder of the Sailor pathway, a Wind-blessed. He also possesses a miraculous item that could be classified as a Sealed Artifact. He is extremely cunning and cruel. Do not attempt to confront him," Alger warned solemnly.

He suddenly turned toward Klein.

"Mr. Fool, could I ask your Blessed to assist me at the critical moment? I would pay a price that interests you."

The only Blessed I have is myself... Klein lampooned inwardly to ease his tension, then smiled.

"That depends on whether my Blessed happens to be in Backlund."

"All right." Alger withdrew his gaze, somewhat disappointed but also faintly expectant.

Chapter 146: Creeping Hunger

"What is so special about Qilangos's mystical item?" Audrey asked with quiet confidence.

After careful consideration, she had suddenly realized that she possessed considerable resources for locating people in Backlund.

First, her father was one of the wealthiest, best-connected, and most respected nobles, while she herself was quite popular among the younger generation. Consequently, she had extensive resources at her disposal within the upper and middle classes of society.

Second, the two Beyonders she knew had circles of their own. Apprentice Fors had once been a clinical doctor and was now an author. She knew many people in the literary and publishing worlds, as well as among middle-class doctors.

Arbiter Xio Derecha had long helped people from the lower-middle class mediate and resolve disputes. She was also rather famous among the working class and the gangs of East Borough, giving her access to many hidden channels.

When the Beyonders they knew and those Beyonders' spheres of influence were taken into account as well, their ability to find someone could not be underestimated.

Alger answered Justice's question almost immediately, without the slightest hesitation.

"No one knows the mystical item's true name, but those who have encountered it call it 'Creeping Hunger.' Every other day, Qilangos must feed it a living person's flesh and soul. Otherwise, it will devour its owner instead."

"That could be one of the most important clues for finding Qilangos," Audrey said, knitting her brows.

She felt profound discomfort and intense loathing toward any evil item that craved the fresh blood and souls of living humans.

"Yes, but in a metropolis of at least five million people, the disappearance of a few vagrants would go unnoticed," Alger reminded her. "Ever since he acquired Creeping Hunger, Qilangos has become extremely difficult to deal with.

"He was originally a Wind-blessed, with formidable Beyonder powers in the domains of water, wind, and weather. Later, however, people discovered that he could drive his targets mad, enter other people's dreams, summon light to purify dead souls, sing to strengthen himself, and alter his appearance... There is almost nothing he cannot do," Alger explained in detail. "We suspect that all of these abilities come from the mystical item Creeping Hunger..."

Before he could finish, Derrick Berg, who had been listening in silence, suddenly blurted out, "Shepherd!"

Shepherd? Sequence 5 of the Secrets Suppliant and Listener pathway? Hmm, among the City of Silver's six-member council, there is a newly appointed elder who is a Shepherd. The Sun mentioned that she was powerful enough to contend with a Sequence 4 expert--well, an evil spirit of the same level... Klein's expression shifted slightly, but the gray fog concealed it. Besides, Justice was not paying attention to him.

"Shepherd?"

"Shepherd?"

Justice and The Hanged Man spoke in unison. One sounded completely bewildered, while the other sounded shocked, as though he had heard the title elsewhere and knew something about it but did not understand the particulars.

Seeing everyone stare at him, Derrick suddenly panicked. No matter how quiet, gloomy, or troubled he might be, he was still only a boy.

He hurriedly explained with a stammer, "I meant that the abilities The Hanged Man described resemble the Beyonder powers of the Sequence known as Shepherd. Every Shepherd can swallow another being's soul into their own body, including wraiths and evil spirits. Through a unique method, they control those souls and compel them to do their bidding, allowing them to use the souls' abilities as though releasing a god's lambs to graze.

"Therefore, no one knows how many powers a Shepherd possesses. It depends on how many Beyonder souls they have swallowed, which makes them extremely frightening. They are almost comparable to High-Sequence Beyonders.

"However, some suspect that there is a limit to the number of souls Shepherds can Devour and Graze, and that the souls within them can be replaced."

So that is what it means to be a Shepherd... The pathway controlled by the Aurora Order is truly enigmatic... No wonder they worship the True Creator--no, the Fallen Creator... Klein was suddenly enlightened, but he did not nod. Instead, he adopted the appearance of someone who had known this all along.

At the same time, he sighed inwardly. Sun, you really are just a boy. That was extremely important information, a tremendously valuable insight. You could have exchanged it for something worthwhile, yet you simply revealed everything! Just like that...

Yes, the powers exhibited by the mystical item Creeping Hunger resemble those of a Sequence 5 Shepherd... I wonder whether other Sealed Artifacts possess the same powers as Beyonders. I wonder which Sequence Sealed Artifact 2-049, the Antigonus family's puppet, resembles...

After hearing The Sun's explanation, Alger appeared to fit the pieces together in his mind and nodded silently.

Audrey grew even more curious and pressed him. "Which pathway does Shepherd belong to? What Sequence is it?"

"The Secrets Suppliant pathway, Sequence 5." Klein seized the opportunity to answer, demonstrating that he knew everything.

"Secrets Suppliant... Aurora Order..." Audrey immediately recalled Mr. A, a suspected Oracle of the Aurora Order, and her heart grew heavy.

She began seriously considering what price she could pay to persuade Mr. Fool to act and effortlessly eliminate that repulsive man. However, she could think of nothing that might move Mr. Fool to do so.

As expected, a godlike figure cannot be moved so easily... There aren't many things in this world capable of arousing His interest, after all... Audrey sighed.

Suppressing the impulse, she nodded gratefully to The Sun, thanking him for offering them a new perspective on Creeping Hunger so that they could approach it more rationally and effectively.

"Mr. Hanged Man, I am willing to accept the mission, but I cannot guarantee that I will find Vice Admiral Hurricane Qilangos." Audrey looked across the table as she spoke.

"I could not ask for a better answer. Whether or not you succeed, as long as you make the attempt, I will compensate you with secret information or intelligence. And if you do succeed, I might be able to provide you directly with the Telepathist's main ingredients. Naturally, the prerequisite is that we first learn what they are," Alger promised with uncharacteristic generosity.

"Deal," Audrey replied with a faint smile, pursing her lips.

Then, with Klein's permission and assistance, Alger conjured a portrait of Qilangos.

One of the seven great pirate admirals, he possessed a distinctive broad chin and brown hair tied into a bun behind his head like an ancient warrior. His green eyes seemed to hold a trace of laughter, yet they were abnormally cold.

Once they had concluded their discussion and exchanged their insights, Klein smiled and announced the end of the gathering. He watched as Justice and The Hanged Man swiftly rose and bowed, while The Sun imitated their movements, albeit more slowly.

Klein extended his right hand and severed the connections, but he did not leave immediately.

In the Berg residence in the City of Silver, Derrick stared at his familiar surroundings, then glanced outside at the pitch-black sky, where lightning flickered. For a moment, he was lost in a trance.

He soon came back to himself. Finding a piece of goatskin and a quill, he wrote down the Bard formula he had memorized.

He examined it several times before finally confirming that there were no errors.

Derrick was not worried that possessing the Bard formula and becoming a Beyonder of another pathway would arouse the suspicions of the City of Silver's upper echelons. During past expeditions, members of the elite teams had often recovered formulas, ingredients, and strange artifacts from monsters and from abandoned, ruined cities.

In the course of such expeditions, it was normal for people to keep a portion of the spoils for themselves. As long as nothing too important was involved, the captains and higher-ups tacitly turned a blind eye.

Over time, certain formulas had begun circulating through unofficial channels within the City of Silver. Some had become the foundations upon which powerful families endured from generation to generation. The varieties of Things of the Dark surrounding the City of Silver were relatively fixed, so some ingredients were easily obtained, while others could be encountered only by venturing deep into the cursed lands.

Setting the goatskin aside, Derrick recalled the mysterious Fool's instructions. There in his simple bedroom, he lowered his head and prayed in a straightforward manner.

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era.

"The mysterious ruler above the gray fog.

"The King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck."

Jotun was a very ancient language, innately endowed with the mystical properties required for rituals, prayers, and spellcasting. Therefore, Derrick had no need to translate the incantation into ancient Hermes.

"The Fool that doesn't belong to this era..."

Seated in the place of honor at the long bronze table, Klein suddenly heard a prayer reverberate beside his ears. He then saw the crimson star corresponding to The Sun begin to blink.

He did not attempt to touch it. Instead, he planned to respond ten minutes before the next gathering, giving the youth from the City of Silver time to arrange to be alone.

More importantly, this allowed him to avoid converting the time and date, reducing the possibility of damaging The Fool's mighty image.

After confirming his plan, Klein enveloped himself in spirituality and initiated his descent.

Back in his room, Klein removed the wall of spirituality and rested briefly before preparing to go out again.

He did not need to play the role of a Seer at all times, nor did he have to make a visit to the Divination Club part of his daily routine. He would go only occasionally, both to earn some extra pocket money and to fulfill his supervisory duties as a Nighthawk.

Klein had originally intended to laze around for the entire afternoon, but he suddenly remembered something he had yet to do and had no choice but to pull himself together. As arranged, he needed to visit Detective Henry that day and collect the final report on the red-chimney investigation.

Sigh. I've heard that important figures are always terribly busy... I still need to find time to visit the Tingen Family Servant Assistance Association with Benson and Melissa and look for a suitable maidservant... Reluctantly, Klein changed his shirt, donned his black tuxedo, picked up his silk top hat and silver-inlaid cane, and left the house like a gentleman.

On Besik Street, beneath the offices of Henry's Private Detective Company, Klein put on a mask, pulled his hat low, swiftly crossed the street, and entered the stairwell.

Chapter 147: Night Visitor

Inside the private detective's office, Detective Henry addressed the gentleman before him in his hoarse voice.

"Sir, your commission has been completed." He sighed in relief. "The job was neither easy nor especially difficult, but it consumed a great deal of our time and resources. To be honest, I regret setting the price so low."

No. No matter what you say, I won't pay a penny more! Klein declared inwardly. Pointing at the thick stack of papers on the coffee table, he asked, "Is that the investigation report?"

"Yes." Henry pressed a hand upon the report, which was at least sixty pages long, and sighed. "This is the most troublesome report I've ever completed..."

Before he could finish speaking, he saw Klein hand over four pounds in cash, and his attention immediately shifted to verifying the notes' authenticity.

"This is the remainder of the fee." Klein picked up the thick stack of papers.

Henry coughed.

"You truly are a gentleman who keeps his word. Sigh. I never expected the investigation report to require this much paper. It went completely beyond my budget."

By then, Klein had already taken the thick investigation report and risen to his feet.

He offered a slight bow and immediately headed for the door, cane in hand.

Detective Henry's final sentence remained lodged in his throat.

How can you expect me to pay for the paper used in the investigation report? That should already be included in the fee! Klein touched the five pounds and eight soli he had left and grumbled inwardly as he strode onto Besik Street.

He surveyed his surroundings and confirmed that no one was paying attention to him before leaving the area. Once the opportunity arose, he removed his mask.

Klein did not intend to return home immediately. He wanted to find a cafe where he could organize the investigation report and identify the houses whose tenants had changed since his divination of the red chimney. He could then investigate them before dinner.

There were many cafes in the vicinity, but none met Klein's requirements. Ever since steam and machinery had become symbols of the age, more and more cafes had simplified their decor and transformed into something akin to inexpensive restaurants. They served refreshments, coffee, bread, and dishes such as pea and mutton stew to busy workers. As a result, respectable ladies and gentlemen no longer visited cafes to hold discussions, nor did they regard doing so as a mark of social standing. Various clubs had emerged to replace cafes as centers of social life.

After searching for some time, Klein finally found a cafe with a decent atmosphere.

He sat in a secluded corner, took a sip of his one-penny Southville coffee, and opened the investigation report.

"Within Tingen City's North Borough, South Borough, East Borough, West Borough, Golden Indus Borough, Harbor Borough, and University Borough, there are a total of 1,179 buildings with dark-red chimneys... Along the outskirts of Tingen City, there are another 546 buildings with chimneys matching the client's description. This figure excludes buildings in more distant towns and villages, despite their being under Tingen's jurisdiction.

"Below are the addresses and tenancy records of each building. In accordance with the client's request, changes occurring within the past three months have been recorded in greater detail."

Klein turned page after page, occasionally using the fountain pen he carried with him to make notes on a separate sheet of paper.

By the time he finished identifying buildings whose red chimneys matched the one he had seen, he discovered that twenty-five of them had undergone a change of tenants.

That isn't too many. I should be able to complete the investigation within two days. After all, I saw both the red chimney and part of the house in my dream. When I encounter those features again, my spiritual intuition should give me a sense of familiarity. I can identify the target that way. In other words, I'm a living investigative machine... Klein nodded, divided the buildings according to location, and planned to investigate fifteen of them that day.

He did not need to perform a divination to determine whether these investigations would be dangerous.

Since the tenants had changed, the mastermind behind those coincidences must have already left!

Let's hope the new tenants know what the previous occupants looked like... But someone capable of manipulating my fate without anyone noticing, making every coincidence appear perfectly natural, must surely have a way to erase any traces he left behind... Sigh. All I can do is pray to the Goddess and hope he overlooked some clue... Klein sighed, rallied himself, and put on his hat. Then he picked up his cane and the report and left the cafe.

Klein spent two soli hiring a carriage and visited fifteen buildings with red chimneys before dinner. Unfortunately, none was the house he had seen in his dream.

If tomorrow's investigation produces the same result, matters will become troublesome. He may still be living in the house with the red chimney, even after I saw it in my divination. That could mean he's extremely confident and has no fear of my investigation; he might not even fear the Tingen Nighthawks. Or perhaps he doesn't know he's been exposed. If so, the power that resisted my divination didn't belong to him... Standing before 2 Daffodil Street, Klein considered the various possibilities.

A few minutes later, he smoothed his tuxedo, pressed down his hat, and took out his key. With a smile, he entered the house.

That evening, he intended to prepare stewed mutton and honey-glazed roast meat for Benson and Melissa.

At eleven o'clock, the siblings wished one another good night and retired to their respective rooms.

Klein closed his bedroom door and stood before his desk, looking through the oriel window by the light of the gas lamp. The streets were shrouded in darkness, illuminated by only a handful of streetlamps. Stars dotted the vast curtain of the night sky. They were numerous, though difficult to see clearly.

"I wonder what Backlund is like, with its titles of the Land of Hope and the Capital of Capitals..." Klein murmured. He reached for the curtain, intending to draw it shut.

Whoosh!

At that moment, a sinister wind struck him without warning. The gas lamp's flame turned dark green.

Klein instinctively retreated several steps. His professional instincts prompted him to tap his left molar twice. At the same time, he leaned toward the bed and reached for the revolver beneath his pillow.

Within his vision, a face suddenly protruded from the wall above the desk, directly beneath the gas lamp. It was translucent and possessed neither eyes nor a nose. It had only a mouth!

"Do not fire," the disembodied face said.

It can communicate? Klein had already drawn his revolver and taken aim.

"What do you want?" he asked in a deep voice.

The face chuckled.

"I'm Daly."

Daly? Spirit Medium Daly? The Spirit Medium Daly who was transferred to the Backlund diocese? Klein raised his eyebrows doubtfully.

"Madam Daly?"

"I know this way of visiting you is rather impolite. I should have warned you in advance so that you could make the necessary preparations. However, it is inconvenient for me to meet you at present, so I can communicate with you only through this little fellow." The translucent face laughed.

Although the voice is different and rather grating, the manner of speech truly is Madam Daly's. The abilities of a Spirit Medium are certainly impressive... Klein thought wistfully. Without lowering his revolver, he asked, "Madam, what did you wish to discuss with me?"

"If I were you, I would first seal the bedroom with spirituality. Otherwise, your family may think you've gone mad," the translucent face quipped. "Heh heh, there is no need to be so wary. I returned to Tingen in secret because of Dunn's letter. You know that a Nighthawk cannot leave their assigned jurisdiction at will."

"The Captain's letter?" Rather than approaching the desk, Klein felt for the Holy Night Powder in the concealed pocket of his black trench coat.

"Dunn and I both began as members of the Tingen Nighthawks, and we have always maintained a good relationship. Last Thursday--yes, Thursday--he sent me a letter in which he mentioned you. He said that you had taken inspiration from a Mystery Pryer's maxim, formulated a set of rules for a Seer, and claimed that these rules helped you gain control over your potion. Since then, you have no longer heard sounds or seen visions that you should not. Dunn said it resembled what I had done.

"Heh heh, are you not going to seal the room? Personally, I do not mind if your brother and sister misunderstand..." the translucent face continued at an unhurried pace.

So that's why... She truly is Madam Daly... Klein sighed with relief and returned the Holy Night Powder to his inner pocket. Then he walked to the desk and removed the silver ritual dagger from its drawer.

He swiftly erected a wall of spirituality before turning back to the protruding face.

"Madam Daly, what else did the Captain say in his letter?"

"He merely expressed his confusion and said that he seemed to have grasped something, yet could not articulate it clearly. He hoped to hear my opinion on the matter," Daly said through the eyeless face. "And when I read the letter this morning, I knew that you were not as ignorant as you pretended to be. Heh heh, Mr. Moretti, I believe you have deduced the 'acting method'!"

"Is that why you came to see me?" Klein neither confirmed nor denied her assertion.

Daly clearly knows about the "acting method"... he calmly concluded.

Daly's translucent face formed a slight smile.

"Yes.

"I believe we should be honest with each other. I know that you have deduced the 'acting method,' and you know that I have grasped the 'acting method' as well. Sigh. What displeases me is that it took me nearly two years to understand it, while you have been a Beyonder for only a month and a half."

Klein fell silent for a moment after hearing her words. Then he smiled sincerely.

"That is because I had you as my role model."

He had wanted to say that he was "standing on the shoulders of giants," but ultimately decided not to give Emperor Roselle an opportunity to enter the conversation.

Chapter 148: Messenger

Klein's reply made Daly chuckle. The translucent face, which possessed only a mouth, said, "Although you drew inspiration from the Mystery Pryers' maxim and confirmed your theory through my experiences and performance, it took you only a month to understand the 'acting method' and formulate your own principles as a Seer. That proves you possess exceptional wisdom and an open mind."

Rather than dwell on a subject that made him feel guilty, Klein asked, "Madam, do the Church's higher-ups know about this so-called 'acting method'?"

"Without question, they understand it very well. I once examined the Church's historical records and searched for accounts of people who defied convention and advanced rapidly. I discovered that more than a few Nighthawks and bishops had done so. I am far from unique. But their endings..." Daly deliberately paused, her tone suddenly somber.

"What sort of endings did they meet?" Klein asked, his heart tightening.

Could the Church of Evernight regard the "acting method" as a temptation from the devil or some evil god?

The translucent face suddenly laughed. "Their endings were quite splendid. Apart from the few who lost control or were sacrificed in Beyonder incidents, the rest became archbishops or high-ranking deacons at the very least. Some among them even succeeded in becoming High-Sequence Beyonders. Within the Church of the Goddess, Sequence 4s and Sequence 3s are known as Saints, while Sequence 2s and Sequence 1s are called Angels on Earth. Naturally, every angel was once a Saint."

...Madam Daly, you deliberately tried to frighten me... The corner of Klein's mouth twitched. Without concealing his doubts, he asked, "Since the Church has mastered the 'acting method,' why doesn't it simply tell every Nighthawk? Although that wouldn't prevent every instance of losing control, it would certainly lower the probability and reduce needless casualties."

A trace of dejection appeared on the translucent face. "I do not know why either. They told me that once I become an archbishop or high-ranking deacon, I will be qualified to learn the secret. I came here today because I hope you will explain the 'acting method' to Dunn more clearly before submitting your special application."

Klein was not foolish enough to ask why she could not do so herself. Instead, he thoughtfully asked, "Once the Church learns that someone has discovered it, that person must swear not to tell anyone about the 'acting method'?"

"Yes. You must take an oath before the Goddess's holy relics and swear upon Her name. That provides more than sufficient binding force. Trust me, you do not want to know the consequences of violating it. I can discuss the matter only with people who have already mastered the 'acting method,' like you. Your body language gave me the answer before you spoke, which is why I dared utter the term." Daly made the eerie face sigh.

After a brief pause, she continued, "Back then, I had only vaguely grasped the essence of 'acting,' yet I digested the potion very quickly. Yes, among the Church's higher-ups, 'digestion' is considered a very apt term for describing one's control over a potion. In any case, before I took the oath and learned of the 'acting method,' I had no clear understanding of it, so I could not explain it accurately to Dunn and the others.

"At first, I gave up. I never imagined I would encounter you, an eccentric prodigy who clearly understood the 'acting method' before submitting a special application--no, a genius."

So that's how you see me, Madam... The corner of Klein's mouth twitched before he solemnly promised, "I had originally intended to use my special application to alert the Captain to the existence of the 'acting method.' With your explanation, I no longer need to worry."

"Very good. You are such a kind lad." Daly sounded relieved.

Madam, you're only two or three years older than I am... Klein inwardly objected to her choice of words.

Without waiting for him to speak, the eerie translucent face continued, "If you encounter any problems or require assistance, you may write to me. Wait for me, heh heh. When I become an archbishop or high-ranking deacon and gain the right to learn why the Church conceals the 'acting method,' I will hint to you whether the reason is good or bad."

Klein immediately became energized and asked without hesitation, "Madam, what is your address?"

As far as he was concerned, the more help he received, the better. Besides, she was quite a powerful Spirit Medium!

Seeing that Klein had no objection whatsoever, Daly fell silent for a moment before laughing.

"We should not communicate through the post office, since we would be using ordinary letters. That would be extremely dangerous.

"I will teach you a comparatively simple piece of ritualistic magic. You can use it to summon a special spirit that belongs to me. Give the letter to it, and it will deliver it directly to me. It is not as fast as a telegram, but it is faster than a steam locomotive. If you send a message at noon, I will receive it in Backlund that same night."

Klein listened with complete attention and gave a slight nod. "A very practical piece of ritualistic magic."

Daly chuckled.

"What makes this ritualistic magic unique is that you pray to yourself. You draw power from your own spirituality without involving a god. Therefore, it is quite secretive, though not especially powerful.

"First, choose an herb and essential oil from the corresponding domain. This is no different from ordinary ritualistic magic. However, you require only the candle that represents yourself. The incantation consists of three parts. The first part is 'I.' Say 'I' in ancient Hermes, Jotun, Dragonish, or Elvish. The second part is 'I summon in my name.' This may be spoken in Hermes. The third part is a precise description of the entity being summoned. For instance, in the future you would say: 'the spirit that wanders about the unfounded, the higher-dimensional creature that a human commands, the messenger that belongs to Daly Simone.'"

Higher-dimensional? In mysticism, that normally refers to the spirit world... Klein memorized the ritual procedure while analyzing it.

In this field, he could barely be considered an expert.

The advantage of this form of ritualistic magic is that it avoids invoking a god and relies entirely upon an individual's power. It can produce various magical effects without the restrictions imposed by a god's particular domain. The difficulty lies in the individual's strength. The weak obtain weak results, while the strong obtain powerful ones... Klein felt that he had once again acquired mystical knowledge he would never otherwise have encountered at his present Sequence.

Daly repeated the description several times, then solemnly emphasized, "Remember, do not alter the precise description of the summoned entity, or the ritual may easily attract a terrifying monster."

"All right." Klein nodded obediently.

At the same time, a thought suddenly occurred to him.

If I changed the description of the summoned entity to "The Fool that doesn't belong to this era, the mysterious ruler above the gray fog; the King of Yellow and Black who wields good luck," what would be summoned?

Would nothing happen? Would the gray fog suddenly descend? Or would I need to respond from within that mysterious space?

Could this help me draw more power from the world above the gray fog?

Could it trigger some terrifying chain reaction?

Klein still felt lingering fear from his experiment with the Mutated Sun Sacred Emblem. In the end, he obeyed his instincts and decided to perform a divination above the gray fog before determining whether to make the attempt.

After some thought, he asked curiously, "Madam, if someone adheres strictly to the 'acting method,' how long does it take to advance from Sequence 8 to Sequence 7, and from Sequence 7 to Sequence 6?"

"According to the records I have read, advancing from Sequence 8 to Sequence 7 takes anywhere from three months to two years. It depends on whether you can grasp the core spirit and corresponding principles during the process of 'acting.' Sequence 7 to Sequence 6 takes between half a year and three years, as does Sequence 6 to Sequence 5. As for Sequence 5 to Sequence 4, it takes between three and twenty years..." Daly gave a rough account.

Klein suddenly smiled.

"Then, Madam, have you already reached Sequence 6?"

He had heard from Dunn that Daly had taken a year to advance from Sequence 9 Corpse Collector to Sequence 8 Gravedigger, then another year to advance from Gravedigger to Sequence 7 Spirit Medium. She had been a Beyonder for five years. In other words, Daly had remained at the Spirit Medium stage for approximately three years.

"Yes. That is why I was transferred to the Backlund diocese," the eerie translucent face answered frankly. "My current occupation is Spirit Guide, although I prefer the title Spirit Medium. All right, this little fellow is growing tired. I must leave. Under these circumstances, I will not say, 'May the Goddess bless you.'"

"Sweet dreams." Klein pressed a hand to his chest, smiled, and bowed.

"No, there will be no sweet dreams tonight. I must hurry back to Backlund. It is not a pleasant experience. It is rather like being in a relationship with someone you dislike..." Daly's voice gradually softened, and the translucent face without eyes or a nose slowly withdrew into the wall without leaving a trace.

The gas lamp's light abruptly brightened, and the gloom vanished into thin air.

Klein, who had kept his Spirit Vision active throughout the exchange, stared blankly at the transformation. It took him some time to return to his senses.

"A Spirit Medium--no, a Spirit Guide--is truly impressive. She can actually conjure a 'messenger.' I wonder what special abilities my Sequence 7 and Sequence 6 possess?" he muttered to himself. Then he swiftly dispersed the wall of spirituality, extinguished the gas lamp in his bedroom, and lay silently in the darkness.

He did not intend to enter the world above the gray fog that night, just in case Daly suddenly returned and uttered Dunn Smith's classic words: "Oh, yes, I forgot one thing."

If that happened, he would not even be able to silence her by killing her!

The following morning, Klein arrived at the Blackthorn Security Company three minutes early.

"Good morning, Klein. The new clerk is here!" Rozanne greeted him with a radiant smile.

Klein was sincerely happy for her.

"Congratulations, Rozanne. The Goddess heard your prayers."

"My skin will finally return to perfect condition!" Rozanne nodded, her eyes shining with joy.

After chatting with her for a while, Klein passed through the partition and knocked on the door of the Captain's office.

"Please come in," Dunn's mellow voice called.

Klein pushed open the door and entered. He saw his captain instinctively sit straighter as his gray eyes darkened, as though bracing himself for trouble.

"Ahem." Klein cleared his throat, set aside his hat and cane, and sat down. "Captain, I have something I would like to report."

"What is it?" Dunn asked in a deep voice, crossing his arms.

Chapter 149: Direct Hint

Klein looked at the solemn Dunn Smith and suddenly smiled.

"Captain, I realized something yesterday."

"And what was that?" Dunn repeated in a serious tone, leaning back and uncrossing his arms.

Klein recalled the speech he had prepared.

"As I reflected on my past experiences, I realized that the names of Sequence potions encompass an entire set of principles that can help us gain control over them and avoid their negative effects. When we act according to those principles, we seem to become a member of the corresponding profession.

"Likewise, these principles are hidden. They aren't revealed to us directly. All we can do is draw conclusions from the corresponding profession little by little, then adjust our understanding according to the feedback we receive.

"Thus, after I became a true Seer at the Divination Club and established my own set of principles for being a Seer, the auditory and visual hallucinations that had plagued me simply vanished.

"That is what I realized."

After finishing his explanation, Klein inwardly breathed a sigh of relief. He had said everything he needed to say without explicitly mentioning the word "acting."

Sigh, let's hope the Captain doesn't tell the Church that I've already developed such ideas when they question him. That would draw far more attention to me... There's also the connection between the Seer pathway and the Antigonus family. That might eventually cause trouble. But the Captain has been through all kinds of situations. He's experienced and intelligent. Once he understands the "acting method," he'll certainly realize that the Church is concealing relevant information. He'll know what he should and shouldn't say... Complicated thoughts crowded Klein's mind.

However, he quickly reached a decision and formed a plan.

If the Captain still can't grasp the "acting method" or sense the Church's concealment, then I'll tell him directly before submitting the special application!

Yes, I'll probe him first and find out what he knows...

Dunn listened to Klein's explanation in silence, his gray eyes growing even deeper.

He remained silent for nearly twenty seconds, rubbing his temples before picking up his pipe and taking a sniff.

After smelling it, he took out a box of matches, seemingly having forgotten the Nighthawks' rules.

White smoke billowed into the air. Dunn closed his eyes as though savoring the scent of tobacco.

After a while, he opened his eyes and smiled at Klein.

"I'm sorry. I forgot that you don't smoke."

"Smoking is bad for your health," Klein replied with complete seriousness.

Dunn contemplated this, his pipe still in hand.

"I seem to have realized something as well."

No, Captain, you haven't realized anything! Just don't wander into my dreams too often! Klein said nothing, offering only a friendly smile.

"Perhaps it won't be long before you submit that special application to me..." Dunn said half-jokingly as he took a deep puff of mint and tobacco.

Can I submit it tomorrow? Klein replied inwardly. He took out his pocket watch and checked the time.

"Captain, I have to be at Old Neil's. Today's mysticism lesson is about to begin."

"All right." Dunn watched Klein leave, pipe still in hand.

After closing the door to the Captain's office, Klein headed toward the stairs leading underground in high spirits. As he passed the clerks' office, he saw two strangers, a man and a woman.
