The new clerks... Klein's thoughts wandered before he added inwardly, In another two days--definitely before the end of this week--I'll submit my application to the Captain!

Then I'll pass a series of examinations and become a Sequence 8 Clown!

Following the silent underground passage, Klein turned toward the armory and pushed open the guardroom door.

"What happened to you?" Klein was startled when he saw Old Neil.

Old Neil looked listless, his face pale. Yawning incessantly, he said, "I've been a little constipated lately. Last night, I tried a ritualistic magic that can solve such problems. In the end... I didn't sleep well all night. I had to go to the bathroom over and over, and I nearly fell asleep on the toilet."

Well, at least the constipation is cured... Once he realized it was nothing serious, Klein almost burst out laughing.

However, he restrained himself and asked, "Are you feeling better now?"

At the same time, his concern prompted him to tap his left molar twice. He activated Spirit Vision to examine the aura of Old Neil's health.

There are some dark patches and impurities in the yellow of his digestive system and the orange of his kidneys, but nothing serious. They're within an acceptable range... Klein sighed in relief.

"I'm fine now. I got some antidiarrheal medicine from Frye." Old Neil yawned like a drug addict. "You'll have to study by yourself for today's supernatural lesson. There's only enough material for two or three days anyway."

"All right," Klein replied politely. "I can guard the armory and study here. Why don't you rest in the break room?"

Old Neil immediately straightened, his eyes gleaming as he answered, "Lad, you are surely the kindest Nighthawk after Frye!

"I'll leave the armory to you!"

He snatched up the cushion from his knees and swept out of the guardroom like a typhoon, leaving Klein alone and dumbfounded.

Blackthorn Security Company accepted an additional mission that morning. The assignment involved escorting a wealthy merchant to the harbor for a business deal. Leonard and Kenley completed it with ease and earned some extra pay, much to Klein's envy.

He went about his day studying mysticism, practicing his shooting, and being tormented by Instructor Gawain, who seemed to have been agitated by something.

Huff, huff... Klein gasped for breath. It took quite some time before he recovered enough to shower and change.

After leaving Gawain's house, he continued his arduous work. He spent two soli on a carriage and investigated the remaining ten houses with red chimneys.

Klein's expression grew grave as the final house with a red chimney disappeared from view.

The house with the red chimney that I saw in my divination isn't among those that recently changed tenants... If that's the case, this has just become troublesome. I wonder how long it will take to investigate roughly sixteen hundred houses... Sigh. I can't ask anyone for help with something like this. After all, only my spirituality will give me that sense of familiarity when I see the target...

Don't be discouraged. Don't give up. I'll continue investigating whenever I have time. I'll try to finish within three months--no, two months! Who knows? I might find the target during tomorrow's search!

And when I get back, I'll organize the material and plan a route based on the distance between districts!

Klein encouraged himself, banishing his dejection.

Now that he had made his decision, he intended to ask the driver to turn toward Daffodil Street. However, he suddenly realized that he was near Mr. Azik's residence.

Before Mr. Azik left on holiday, he wrote to tell me that he would return sometime this week, but he didn't specify the exact date. Since it's on the way, I'll leave him a note. Besides, I hired this carriage for an hour at two soli, and the time is almost up anyway. I'll simply stop at Mr. Azik's house, then take a public carriage home... Klein quickly made his decision.

Four minutes later, he alighted from the carriage outside Mr. Azik's house.

The houses here were clearly of higher quality than those on Daffodil Street, though not as fine as those on Howes Street. A patch of lawn lay in front of the house, with a small garden behind it.

Ding! Ding! Ding!

Klein pulled the rope beside the door, ringing the bell inside the house.

A few moments later, he heard footsteps from within, and the door swung open.

Azik's gentle features and bronze complexion appeared before Klein. Since he was at home, he wore only a simple white shirt, brown vest, and matching trousers.

"Klein? I was just about to write to you," Azik said warmly in greeting. "I only arrived home last night."

Klein stared at the small mole near Azik's right ear.

"Mr. Azik, I found a clue about your past."

"Really?" Azik instantly grew excited, and the sorrow in his eyes faded.

"Let's speak inside." Klein glanced around.

Azik quickly nodded and stepped aside to let Klein enter.

He locked the door and led Klein into the first-floor living room, where they sat on the soft sofa.

"What clue did you find?" he asked impatiently.

Not having expected to meet Mr. Azik that day, Klein took a moment to organize his thoughts.

"I recently received a mission that required me to deal with a wraith in Lamud Town."

"Lamud..." Azik softly repeated the name, his brow furrowing.

Klein watched his expression and slowed his speech.

"While dealing with the wraith, we discovered something and consequently conducted an investigation in the town...

"One of the residents possessed a portrait of the first Baron Lamud, which he tried to sell to me. Out of curiosity, I asked to see it and discovered that, apart from the hair, the man depicted had features resembling yours.

"He even had the same mole near his ear, in a similar position and of a similar size.

"When I questioned him, the man told me that the portrait was about forty years old, but the person in it definitely came from the abandoned castle. It was a copy of an ancient portrait excavated from the castle.

"You should know that people like us, with our unique abilities, can more or less tell when someone is lying. I could tell that the man was telling the truth."

Azik leaned forward as he listened to Klein. He crossed his arms and remained silent for a long while.

Five minutes later, he exhaled.

"Your description didn't awaken any memories. Perhaps I should visit the abandoned castle myself. Can you take me there?"

"It would be my honor," Klein replied. "But I need to go home first. I don't want my siblings to worry."

"No problem." Azik stood.

Chapter 150: Azik's Discovery

At 2 Daffodil Street, Klein nodded to Azik, briskly walked to the door of his house, fished out his keys, and unlocked it.

Melissa was already home. Hearing the lock click, she hurried out of the kitchen and into the living room.

The moment she saw Klein, her eyes lit up with joy. "I bought groceries. There's chicken, potatoes, onions, fish, turnips, and peas. I even bought a small jar of honey."

Sis, are you growing accustomed to the occasional luxury too? Klein chuckled.

"You'll have to make dinner tonight. Don't count on me, because I'll be out of town. I may not return until dawn. Yes, I'm doing a favor for Mr. Azik, a teacher in Khoy University's Department of History."

As he spoke, he turned sideways and pointed toward the carriage waiting outside.

Melissa opened and closed her mouth twice before pursing her lips and saying, "All right."

Klein bade his sister farewell and left. He climbed into the rented carriage Azik had hired, and after a journey of two hours and forty minutes, they reached Lamud Town.

By then, it was almost nine o'clock. The sky was dark, and in places without streetlamps, they could rely only on the crimson moonlight and twinkling starlight filtering through the clouds.

After instructing the driver to wait in town, Klein led Azik toward the ancient, abandoned castle.

As they walked, he noticed Azik moving faster and faster, until Klein had to break into a light jog to keep pace. In the end, it was Azik who led the way.

Klein initially wanted to say something, but when he saw Azik's solemn expression and tightly pursed lips, he wisely swallowed his words.

At that pace, they soon reached the ancient castle.

Little more than a ruin, the castle sprawled in every direction, its spire desolate, untamed, eerie, and dark.

Azik gazed at the ancient castle and slowed his steps.

He came to a halt, his gaze profound yet lost, as though he hovered between dream and reality.

Suddenly, he groaned in pain and raised a hand to clutch his forehead, his features contorting in agony.

"Mr. Azik, are you all right?" Klein asked cautiously as he activated Spirit Vision.

While they were aboard the hired carriage traveling from Daffodil Street to Lamud Town, he had performed a quick divination by flipping a coin to determine whether the journey would be dangerous.

However, he did not believe divination was omnipotent, and he remained vigilant in case he had misinterpreted the result. Furthermore, Azik was deeply mysterious. No one knew his past, and there was no telling how he might react when confronted with it. Caution and unease had accompanied Klein throughout the journey.

Azik did not reply immediately. Wearing a pained expression, he took two more steps forward and lowered the hand clutching his forehead. Then, in a dreamlike voice, he pointed ahead.

"I've seen this ancient castle in my dreams.

"Back then, it was still intact, with a sturdy outer wall and a tall spire.

"I remember there was a stable there, a well there, and barracks there. Over there was a garden where we grew potatoes and sweet potatoes...

"I remember a training ground. My child was a boy. He was only seven or eight years old, but he loved running around while dragging a broadsword taller than he was. He said he wanted to become a knight when he grew up...

"My wife was always complaining that the castle was too gloomy. She loved sunlight and warmth..."

Klein observed the colors of Azik's energy field. The man's words made his scalp tingle, but they also moved him slightly, as though he were personally living through a supernatural tale.

The ancient castle really is connected to Mr. Azik... Could he truly be the first Baron Lamud, a supernatural creature who has lived for fourteen hundred years? Is he human or an evil spirit? No, that can't be. Evil spirits don't stroll around in broad daylight and associate with the Nighthawks... Klein made no effort to restrain his thoughts, allowing them to collide and spark new ideas.

At that moment, Azik stopped murmuring and strode through the main gate.

Without any guidance from Klein, he made his way deep into the castle. With unmistakable familiarity, he found the concealed mechanism, opened the secret door, and entered the basement.

Gripping his cane tightly, Klein followed behind Azik. They descended the stairs and returned to the chamber containing the coffin.

Unlike the last time, the coffin was closed, and the warm, pure sensation had vanished.

The coffin is closed... That must have been Frye. It's his professional instinct as a Corpse Collector... Klein nodded thoughtfully and, through Spirit Vision, watched the conflicted Azik approach the coffin.

Azik extended both hands and pushed the coffin lid aside until a gap appeared.

He stared at the headless skeleton for a long time, then suddenly let out a cry of pain and grief.

Azik stumbled backward, his steps heavy. Before Klein could react, he struck the wall and collapsed against it.

He covered his face with both hands and sat there, utterly despondent. The surroundings abruptly grew even darker.

Klein hurried forward and reached out, but quickly withdrew his hands, not daring to disturb the man.

At that moment, his spiritual intuition warned him that the present Mr. Azik was terrifying--so terrifying that the entire basement had become gloomy and dreadful.

Klein slowly edged closer to the stairs.

He trusted Mr. Azik's character, but he feared that the man might lose control.

He waited in tense unease for several more minutes. At last, he saw Azik lower his hands and slowly rise.

Mr. Azik seems to have changed... That's what my spiritual intuition tells me... But in Spirit Vision, the colors of his aura show no obvious change. His emotions remain as low, depressed, and anguished as before... Klein swiftly assessed the situation and felt that Azik had become gloomier and more imposing.

"I remembered something, but only fragments," Azik said in an emotionless voice.

Then he looked around and said, "I can sense the power that disrupted your fate."

"What?" Klein was stunned. Pleasantly surprised, he asked, "Can you trace its source?"

The person behind the scenes, the one who lived in the house with the red chimney, secretly engineered those coincidences and came to Lamud's ancient castle to take the black-armored knight's head?

What is he trying to do? What is his true objective?

"Too much time has passed, but I'd like to try." A volcano seemed poised to erupt beneath Azik's deep voice.

"How?" Klein asked curiously.

Azik walked to the coffin and gazed down at the skeleton within.

"He took my child's skull. I want to find him through our blood connection."

Your child? Mr. Azik, are you sure the black-armored knight is your child? So you really are an antique... Did you truly lose your memories after living for so long? Is this the price you must pay for such longevity? Klein drew a silent breath, struck by the strange sensation of interacting with a legendary creature.

Azik extended his right hand and abruptly sliced his index finger with his thumbnail.

A drop of fresh, crimson blood fell precisely onto the white skeleton.

It swiftly seeped into the bones, and the entire skeleton suddenly turned bloodred.

Wah! Wah! Wah! Klein abruptly heard the cries of a baby and sensed someone staring at him from behind.

He drew his revolver and aimed it behind him before slowly turning around. However, there was nothing there. Nothing existed behind him.

Even the stairway leading to the ground floor had vanished!

Wah! Wah!

The wails of a baby bored into Klein's ears. When he looked back toward the coffin, he was shocked to see countless indistinct, distorted faces rising amid billowing black fog. Together, they formed a strange door.

Creak!

The illusory door opened, and pale-white arms reached through one after another, only to vanish into the black fog before Azik.

Through the narrow opening, Klein saw a white skull. It had been cast beneath a brown tree and weathered into powder by the elements.

Creak!

The door abruptly slammed shut, severing countless pale-white arms and sending them tumbling to the floor.

Then Klein heard a long sigh--Mr. Azik's heavy sigh, laden with the weight of a long and storied past.

With that sigh, the black fog dispersed and the baby's cries ceased. Everything returned to normal, except for the deepened chill.

Klein clenched his chattering teeth and looked into the coffin. The red skeleton had returned to its original crystalline white.

"I'm sorry. I couldn't find him..." Azik said in a deep voice, his back to Klein.

At the same time, he closed the coffin.

"It's no surprise that we couldn't find him. It would have been surprising if we had," Klein consoled him.

After all, I've already been disappointed many times over this matter... he added inwardly.

Azik gave the coffin before him one final glance. He slowly turned and said, "I'll continue investigating, and I hope I can count on your assistance."

"No problem. That's exactly what I want to do." Klein suppressed the impulse to tell Azik about the red chimney.

There was no point in bringing it up. He could rely only on himself to identify the target.

However, this solved one of his greatest problems: how to involve the Nighthawks once he found the house with the red chimney. He did not believe he could defeat such a mysterious and terrifying puppet master alone.

Now he could ask Mr. Azik for help!

Azik opened his mouth, but in the end, he said nothing. He merely sighed and silently walked toward the stairs.

After leaving the basement and closing the secret door, the two followed the path overgrown with weeds and brambles. Neither spoke as they walked away from the ancient, abandoned castle.

In the darkness of night, Azik suddenly said, "Until this matter is resolved, I will resign from my position and leave Tingen to search for my lost past."

"Mr. Azik, did you discover what happened to you?" Klein asked, unable to conceal his curiosity.
